| D366 🐞 | **Our most-used endgame opponent is the one instrument never measured in endgames.** Owner 2026-08-16: *"isn't that THE place where humans play so different from agent?"* Measured on the corpus: **12 of 14 endgame packs declare `human_common`** (Maia) and 2 declare `perfect_tablebase`. **Every instrument we own is mismatched exactly here.** (a) `perfect_tablebase` orders winning moves by **shortest DTZ** — distance-to-*zeroing* — so it provably beelines for the next capture or pawn move; "simplifies happily" is a property of the shipped code, and no human plays that way. (b) The explorer has **no endgame coverage at all** — R9 measured usable data ending at ply ~20 and zero by ply 27, while endgames begin far later. (c) **Maia is a policy network with no search**, which is precisely the regime where search matters most and a policy head is weakest — and it is our default. **The product question the owner's framing exposes:** a human-like endgame opponent is simultaneously the **most valuable** thing we could have (it is where technique gaps actually decide games) and the **least verified** thing we ship. The measurable form is not "is Maia strong" but *"is Maia wrong the way a human at that band is wrong"* — plausible technique failures — **or wrong arbitrarily**, which is noise wearing a human's name. The tablebase gives exact ground truth for result-preservation at ≤7 pieces, so this is answerable | R4, R9, D333, resistance spectrum |
| D365– | next free |  |

**Live collision, 2026-08-16: `D203` is used twice.** The `pack-graduation` cross-review was
issued the block **D203–D212** and may use no other id; while it was measuring, the selection-
integrity commits (`fc99ba1`/`5835dfc`) took **D203** as *"the next free id above the highest
issued block"* — which it was not, because this block had been issued in a brief but not yet
registered on this line. Both rows are below. **The convention has a hole and it is this one:
"the highest issued block" is only knowable from a file that the issuing brief does not write
to.** Resolution is the owner's: renumber the selection-integrity row to **D233** (the first id
above the highest block now registered), or renumber this cross-review's first row. Fix for the
hole: a block is registered **on this line the moment it is issued**, not when its rows land.

| # | Defect | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D148 🐞 | **The D126 ruling does not reach a single `cost: unmeasurable`, and the reason is the schema, not the corpus.** `$defs/deviationCost` admits exactly three shapes — `cp` (basis `engine` or `material`), `mate` (basis `engine` or `tablebase`) and `unmeasurable` — so there is no corpus-basis cost, and a result split cannot be written into the field at all. The semantic block is stronger than the structural one: *cost* is a quality claim by definition, so converting a split into one is precisely the conversion the ruling refuses. Measured on the corpus rather than argued: the four deviations carrying `unmeasurable` are played **30 of 10987** (`kid-mar-del-plata-white` c4-c5), **2 of 158** (`iqp-white-panov-attack` d4-d5), **0 of 742** (`nimzo-doubled-c-pawns` c4-c5) and **0 of 730** (`grunfeld-exchange-fianchetto` …Bxd4) at ratings 1400/1600/1800, rapid+classical, 2023-01..2025-12, retrieved 2026-08-15 — all four below the 100-game floor at which `explorer.ts:91` abstains. **A move worth authoring as a deviation is a move the band does not play, which is the same property that denies it a split.** Two corrections fall out: D126's own summary says the ruling unblocks *"five packs carrying `cost: unmeasurable`"* and the middlegame-wave correction says all five `concept_violation`s carry `unmeasurable` — there are **four**; `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze`'s c4-c5 carries `{cp, 194, engine}` from that wave's single engine pass. The four reasons now state the measured count and the abstention instead of *"no corpus evidence bears on this"* | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D149 🐞 | **`explorerUrl` hard-coded `moves=12`, so an authored deviation outside the twelve most-played could be bounded but never counted.** `ExplorerQuery.moves` now accepts a positive safe integer and canonicalization defaults it to 12; the URL, priority artifact, and job digest all record the applied depth. Tests pin both the unchanged default and a 40-move request. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the authoring-instrument batch |
| D150 🐞 | **No evidence record kind at HEAD can carry a result split, so every split this wave authored is unbound prose.** Three gates close in series: `EVIDENCE_KINDS` (`sourcing/types.ts`) has no census kind; `explorer_frequency`'s values are validated **key-exact** against the eight move-share fields (`sourcing/check.ts:128-132`, `exactKeys`), so adding `white`/`draws`/`black` fails `EVIDENCE_VALUES_INVALID`; and the supported text must be **byte-equal** to `renderExplorerFrequency` (`check.ts:144`), which renders a share and no outcomes. `check.ts:202` then maps `corpus_observed` to `explorer_frequency` alone, so a split sentence labelled `corpus_observed` is `EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED` the moment its pack acquires a ledger. The eleven packs edited this wave have **no `.evidence.json` at all**, which is the only reason the label passes today — the same hole D124 names from the band side. `rfc/claim-backing.md`'s `explorer_position_census` is the named fix and is not landed, so this row is the standing statement of what the ruling can and cannot be bound to | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D151 🐞 | **The 100-game abstention floor sits exactly where timing windows live, and two of the four shipped windows are entirely beneath it.** A window is declared several plies into a spine, and band data thins with depth. Measured at the wave's population: `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze` completes readiness after b3 at **80 games** (one ply earlier, after Rac1, 101 — the floor is crossed *inside* the window); `iqp-white-panov-attack` completes readiness at **29** and its arrival close fires at **12**; only `kid-mar-del-plata-white` (639 at readiness, 644 at arrival) and `dragon-yugoslav-race` (239 at readiness, 439 at arrival) stay above it. So the tempo layer's authored budgets and deadlines are unreachable by corpus evidence in half the cases before the conditional-instrument problem (D155) is even reached. Worth recording as a **content-planning** fact, not only a defect: window depth and evidence availability trade off against each other, and nothing warns an author | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D152 🐞 | **`make expression-census` was blind to corpus grounding.** Its canonical report now includes a per-pack evidence census: label-to-rung mapping, exact claim counts, pointer-matched ledger backing, and machine-readable explorer populations where records provide them. Missing sidecars remain visible as zero backing and an empty population rather than being inferred from prose. A synthetic backed corpus record pins the positive path. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the authoring-instrument batch |
| D153 🐞 | **[[D123]]'s 400-character `timingWindows[].note` cap now blocks the D126 ruling specifically, measured.** A split is only evidence with its population attached — game count, rating band, speeds and window — which is ~120 characters in this repo's own phrasing. The four shipped window notes are **337, 394, 372 and 359** of 400 characters, so the headroom is 63, 6, 28 and 41: **no window can state the population of a split in the field that exists to justify its thresholds.** The wave routed the sentences into `feedbackClaims` and `provenance.sources` instead, where the runtime shows the first and never shows the second. This is D123 with a number on it and a second independent cause, which is the argument for lifting the cap rather than trimming prose | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D154 🐞 | **The D126 row's illustrative split is a manufactured chess number sitting in the ledger next to a real game count.** The row reads *"At this position 1400–1800 players scored 47/31/22 over 5,069 games"*. **5,069 is real** — it is `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze`'s start position at the middlegame wave's band, re-queried and reproduced exactly this wave. **47/31/22 is not**: the measured split there is **2629/371/2069 = 51.9/7.3/40.8%**, and a 31% draw rate does not occur anywhere in the eleven positions this wave censused (range 4.7–9.8%). The illustration is doing its job and no one has been misled, but a plausible-looking split paired with a real count is exactly the shape [[D110]] describes one tier up — *the number was typed and no instrument produced it* — and this ledger is the document every agent reads first. Remedy is one edit by the owner or claude-on-a-ruling: either mark the numbers illustrative or replace them with the measured Maroczy split | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D155 💡 | **The instrument the tempo layer needs is a game-level corpus, and calling it "the explorer" is why four waves have re-derived that it does not exist.** Every timing-window graduation blocker in the corpus asks for the same thing — *"a corpus measurement relating the arrangement's completion to results at this band"* — and the explorer cannot answer it for a structural reason, not a coverage one: it aggregates **per position**, so no query can condition an outcome on whether an event happened earlier or later in the same game. D126 does not touch this; admitting result splits changes what may be *said* about a position, not what may be *asked* of the index. The distinct capability is per-game traversal (replay games at the band, test a predicate over the move sequence, partition outcomes) — the Go corpus pipeline `design/04` §4 already names for endgame mining, pointed at middlegame timing. Proposal: name it as its own instrument class wherever the content map lives, so *"the explorer cannot do this"* stops being rediscovered and starts being a queued capability | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D156 💡 | **Two disposable authoring instruments landed under the exploration gate and one should be promoted; this is [[D121]]'s class exactly.** `apps/server/src/split-probe.ts` prints a position's W/D/B split and every returned move's split at the wave's stated population, live and uncached, with the 100-game floor flagged and a `MOVES=` override for [[D149]]; `apps/server/src/fen-walk.ts` resolves a pack's start, every spine node and every deviation's before/after position to FENs so a probe can be aimed at them. Both are headed DISPOSABLE and neither is imported by production code. The promotion case is measured: **no shipped command answers "what did the band score here"** — `make candidate-attach` writes a move-share sentence and nothing else — so the next content wave rebuilds this, which is the D121 pattern (three authors, three identical evaluators, one missing print statement). `fen-walk` has a second use the wave did not expect: it is the only way to get the FEN of an authored deviation's *result*, which every deviation-grounding question needs | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D157 🐞 | **`carlsbad-minority-attack` — the pack `design/04` §8 names as the middlegame exemplar — carried no corpus evidence of any kind until this wave, and nothing flags it.** Its four `provenance.sources` entries are an opening-name citation, a chessops verification, an UNGROUNDED disclaimer and an authoring note; there is no rating band, no game count, and no explorer query anywhere in the file. Every one of the ten packs the middlegame wave authored states its band in prose; the pack they were written to match does not. It now carries the position census (815 games, 390/60/365) as its first corpus fact. The general defect is that **a pack quoting no population is indistinguishable from a pack quoting the wrong one** — D124 measures the second and nothing measures the first. A single required field, or a check that warns when a pack has no band-bearing source, closes both | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D110 🐞 | **No position in the corpus has a complete legal-move tablebase census, and thirteen claims assert one.** Enumerating the legal successors of the root and of every authored spine node across the 12 `tablebase_exact` packs and matching them against the packs' `tablebase_result` FENs: **0 of 241 positions is fully censused** — root coverage runs from **1 of 28** (`mate-two-bishops`) to **5 of 11** (`pawn-breakthrough-convert`), and `philidor-passive-rook-convert` covers **4 of 21** while its `one-move-wins` claim reads *"All twenty-one legal moves were enumerated and queried: thirteen draw … and seven lose."* `mate-bishop-knight`'s and `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight`'s `stalemate-is-the-default` assert *"nine win, nine draw, and eight of those nine draws are stalemate"* against a 2-of-23 root. Either the queries ran and were never recorded, or the counts came from elsewhere — this is *the author ran the query, typed the number, and the ledger never learned about it* generalised from one worked example to a measured population. **The tablebase evidence that IS recorded is complete and live** (root FEN in 12 of 12 packs, 229 of 229 authored spine positions, 0 of 341 manufactured at HEAD); it is the *enumerations* that are missing. Remedy is a query wave, not a contract change **Independently verified 2026-08-15 by claude, and the core holds:** `philidor-passive-rook-convert`'s ledger carries 25 `tablebase_result` records at **25 distinct anchor FENs** — a walk *down the spine*, one record per position — and **exactly one** of them anchors at the root the claim enumerates. So *"thirteen draw, seven lose"* is a precise cardinal breakdown no instrument in this repo produced. The claim may well be TRUE; truth is not the standard, traceability is, and this is law 8 failing from the **authoring** side rather than the rendering side — the product manufacturing chess truth in its own content. **Two different scopes are in play and both are recorded rather than one being chosen:** a phrase-level sweep for asserted enumerations finds **4 assertions across 3 files** (`philidor-passive-rook-convert` ×2, `mate-bishop-knight`, `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight`); the drafting agent's claim-level detector finds **13**. They measure different things. A first sweep by claude reported *23 packs* — that count was **wrong and is withdrawn**: 22 of the 23 hits were the **opposite**, the honest disclaimer *"all legal moves were not enumerated"* that the engine-pass note carries by design, caught by a regex that missed the negation. **ROW TITLE OVERSTATED — corrected 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing`'s cross-review, which re-derived both detectors and found BOTH RIGHT about different things.** The lexical sweep's **4** is the count of explicit *"all N legal moves were enumerated"* assertions. The draft's **13** is neither that nor a census count — its own list mixes genuine censuses with off-tree positions and one subset quantification. **The actual law-8 escalation is 7 full-set census claims.** And the headline *"no position has a complete census"* is false as stated: **36 positions are fully censused** — all of them single-legal-move links, where the census is trivial. The true and **stronger** figure is **0 of 199 choice-bearing positions**, which is the population the claim actually concerns. Escalation stands at 7; the number was wrong in the alarming direction and is corrected here rather than quietly. | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by `rfc/claim-backing.md`; 13 claims in that RFC's Bucket 2 |
| D111 🐞 | **Five claims assert what no instrument in this repository measures**, and they are the permanent residue of D97 once the debt is made payable. `mate-bishop-knight/opponent-plays-the-spine` and `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight/spine-is-the-opponent` cite *"a reimplementation of the shipped rule [that] reproduced all nineteen of them"* — an instrument that left no artifact; `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight/wrong-corner-is-forced` cites *"took twenty plies"* against *"the most resistant defence available at every turn"*, a search nothing recorded, and carries no self-declared label to fall back on; `philidor-passive-rook-convert/sibling-drill` cites a record in **another pack's** ledger; `philidor-passive-rook-convert/why-the-skewer-works` carries a `tablebase_exact` label for a legality/geometry fact no tablebase record states. **Each remedy is authoring** — add a self-declared label and mark the span authored, copy the record, or re-label — **and none is tooling.** That the residue is 5 of 131 rather than 0 is the law-8 check working; a binding mechanism admitting all 131 would have stopped checking | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by `rfc/claim-backing.md`; owner call recorded as that RFC's Open question 4 |
| D112 🐞 | **`$defs/feedbackClaim` is `additionalProperties: true`** — uniquely among the shapes the claim work touched (`$defs/spineNode`, `$defs/deviation` and `$defs/reasoningKeyPoint` are all `false`; only `$defs/provenance` shares the openness, which is D25's hazard one object over). So any future field on a claim — an anchor, a binding, a trust marker — **validates silently and unchecked before it is specified**, and absence can never be proved from the schema, only counted from the corpus. Noted inline by `rfc/feedback-delivery.md` §2.1 as the reason its key-set measurement rather than the schema is what its C1 rests on; ledgered here so it is destinated rather than living in one RFC's parenthesis. `claim-backing` routes around it entirely by putting the binding in the evidence ledger, which is why it stays open | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D121 🐞 | **`make shape-check PROBE=<fen>` computed the probe and printed nothing.** The CLI now prints `PROBE FIRES` or `PROBE DOES NOT FIRE` with the checked file and `/trigger` pointer for every probe; unit coverage pins both outputs and a real CLI invocation observes the result. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the authoring-instrument batch |
| D122 ✅ | **A unit test pins two content facts, and authoring correct content turns `make verify` red.** `apps/server/src/expression-census.test.ts:28-41` asserts that `knight-vs-bishop/black-anchor-the-knight` fires **0** times in the corpus and that all five `opposite-castling-race` subjects carry `IN_SHAPE_DENOMINATOR_EMPTY`. The 2026-08-15 middlegame wave falsified both by authoring packs: the plan signature now fires **7 of 791** (all in `iqp-white-panov-attack`) and the race trigger fires **12 of 791** (`dragon-yugoslav-race`), so four of the five race subjects have a real denominator. Nothing is wrong with the content or the instrument — the test encodes a census snapshot. Same class as **D47** (pin tests encode content facts, queued on `rfc/fixture-realism.md`); recorded separately because this instance blocks the required gate rather than merely embedding a fact. The wave deliberately did **not** edit the test: a content wave rewriting a server test to match its own output is the failure mode the pin was meant to catch | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-15 by `aee7c64`** — `expression-census.test.ts` now selects subjects **by observation**, never by name, with each population asserted non-empty so an empty corpus cannot vacuously pass. Row stood open for a day because claude fixed it inside a content commit and never flipped it — the same defect-vs-doctrine mismatch that left D59 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D123 🐞 | **`timingWindows[].note` is capped at 400 characters — the one field that must justify an authored threshold is the most constrained in the format.** Spine annotations, deviation notes and provenance strings are unbounded; the window note, which is where a luxury budget of 1 and a deadline of 4 have to be explained, truncates. All four window-declaring packs of the 2026-08-15 wave hit or worked around the cap (three authors reported it independently, ~4 minutes each), pushing the rationale into `provenance.sources` where the runtime cannot show it. Either lift the cap or give a window a `rationale` sibling that is not learner-facing | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D124 🐞 | **The corpus quotes two different explorer populations and no pack states its band in a machine-readable field.** The opening wave and `content/candidates/priority/priority.json` used ratings 1400/1600/1800, **blitz+rapid, 2024-01..2026-07**; the 2026-08-15 middlegame wave used ratings 1400/1600/1800, **rapid+classical, 2023-01..2025-12**. Both are honest and both are quoted in prose inside `provenance.sources`, so nothing can compare them, detect the mismatch, or re-derive a percentage — the checker's byte-equality rule for `explorer-move-share/v1` applies only to attached candidates, not to hand-authored packs. A corpus-evidence block (population, speeds, window, retrieval date) beside the claim would make band a fact rather than a sentence | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D125 💡 | **A named structure family can have no band-attested tabiya, and the content map has nowhere to say so.** `design/04` §3 lists **hanging pawns** as one of ten middlegame families. Measured 2026-08-15 on the standard QGD Tartakower route at ratings 1400/1600/1800 (rapid+classical, 2023-01..2025-12): the pre-break node has **39 games** and the first position where the shape entry's trigger can fire has **14** — below the 100-game floor the explorer client itself uses to abstain. So the wave shipped nine of ten families and skipped this one on evidence rather than on effort. The gap is invisible in `04`, in the ledger, and in `content/shapes/hanging-pawns.json`, which reads as an ordinary unreferenced entry. Proposal: record attestation per family (position, band, count, date) wherever the content map lives, so "not authored" and "not reachable at this band" stop looking the same | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D126 💡 | **Owner-gated: are explorer W/D/B result splits admissible as `corpus_observed` evidence?** The explorer returns a white/draw/black split per move, and the wave's instrument printed it on every call. **All three authoring agents independently refused to use it**, reading a win-rate at a rating band as a graded move assessment under law 8, and the exemplar refused it too. The cost of that refusal is measurable: roughly half the wave's "could not ground" list — *is this recapture better*, *does ...h5 stop the storm*, *is the symmetric isolani balanced* — becomes partially groundable at **zero additional API cost** if the answer is yes, with a stated population and no engine. The argument against is that outcome share at a mixed-Elo band is confounded by everything except the move. This needs a ruling, not an authoring convention, because the corpus is already full of packs that quote frequencies and refuse results | ✅ **OWNER RULED 2026-08-15: ADMISSIBLE as `corpus_observed` (rung 4).** The boundary is now written so authors stop self-refusing: **the split may be stated; it may never be converted into a move verdict or a quality claim.** *"Over 5,069 games at this position the outcomes were 2,629 White wins, 371 draws and 2,069 Black wins (51.9/7.3/40.8%)"* is what happened — rung 4 is defined as *"says what happened, not what is good"*, and the ladder's named failure is reading **popularity as quality**, which a result split is not. *"…so Black is better here"* stays refused. Unblocks roughly half the ungroundable list at zero extra instrument cost, including five packs carrying `cost: unmeasurable` and four authored tempo budgets. Owned by `rfc/claim-backing.md` (the `explorer_position_census` record kind) , raised 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D127 🐞 | **The shape library can author a plan success signature the pack layer refuses to grade on.** `queenless-middlegame/white-king-into-the-game`'s signature is built from placement/quantified nodes, and a pack that copies it verbatim into a `structural_feature` success condition is rejected with `STRUCTURAL_CONDITION_HAS_NO_FEATURE` ("derives no rules evidence reference"). The two layers therefore disagree about what is expressible: `shape-check` accepts the entry, `pack-check` refuses the pack that uses it. `berlin-queenless-press` works around it with an implied `piece_distance` conjunct and discloses the substitution in its objective summary and provenance — i.e. the authored content now differs from the library text it claims to grade. Either the shape validator should refuse un-gradeable signatures at authoring time, or the objective compiler should accept them where a checkpoint already may | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D128 🐞 | **Fifteen packs carry claims and no evidence ledger at all — a debt kind `claim-backing` §4 has no bucket for.** At `d2f34f9` this was 5 packs / 1 machine-checkable label; `aee7c64`'s ten middlegame packs took it to **15 packs / 55 claims / 15 machine-labelled / 5,353 chars** at `67f6ee0` [V]. §4's Bucket 2 is defined as *"the instrument exists and is wired, but the query was never recorded"* — for these there is no sidecar to record into, so the debt is a sourcing run **plus** a ledger **plus** a binding. `feedback-delivery`'s C7 fails them closed correctly; what is wrong is counting them inside a wave that does not pay them | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the `claim-backing` cross-review |
| D129 🐞 | **Ordinals are numerals the residual sweep cannot see, and this corpus writes ranks and files as ordinals.** `claim-backing` §3.4's frozen table was *"an English cardinal 0–999"*; **24 of the 75 machine-labelled claims at HEAD carry an ordinal** [V] — *"every rook slide along the **sixth** rank"* (`tempo-is-the-lesson`), *"rook to the **fourth**"* (`bridge-not-squeeze`), *"the **third** rank"*, *"queen-versus-**seventh**-pawn"*. Omitting them did not make them safe, it made them undeclarable and unswept. Fixed in the draft by extending the table to ordinals 1st–99th; the residue (fractions, *"twice"*, *"a third"*) is 4 occurrences and stays `authored` | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.4 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D130 🐞 | **An assertion's `fen` argument need not name a position the pack reaches.** In `claim-backing` §3.3's registry v1, only `tablebase.lineUniformCategory@v1` checked its FENs against the authored spine; the other eight entries accepted any FEN holding a record in that ledger. `philidor-third-rank-hold`'s `philidor-is-drawn` opens *"This **exact position** is a tablebase draw"* and its ledger holds 23 `tablebase_result` records, so the span binds to a different position and validates. This also falsified Open question 3's premise, which borrows `feedback-delivery`'s *"author-supplied and machine-checked against the pack's own spine"* verbatim. Fixed by a standing precondition + `CLAIM_FEN_OFF_PACK` | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.3 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D131 🐞 | **The residual sweep polices numerals; the owner's D126 boundary polices inference from numerals — and the second is not mechanically checkable.** D126 admits explorer W/D/B splits *"the split may be stated; it may never be converted into a move verdict or a quality claim"* and refuses *"…so Black is better here"* by name. That clause carries **no cardinal, no SAN token, no result word**: appended to a claim binding one percentage it survives the sweep with an empty residual and ships `ledger_bound`. Four families sit in the gap — qualitative, causal, comparative, scope — and three corpus claims already contain no digit and no spelled cardinal at all [V]. **Not fixable by any check short of reading English.** Recorded, tested as a ceiling (criterion 4), and answered only by §3.6's paired rendering | 💡 open — **law 8 residue, escalated not absorbed**, found 2026-08-15 by the `claim-backing` cross-review |
| D132 🐞 | **`feedbackClaims[].id` uniqueness is refused nowhere, which reopens D98 through the digest.** `validatePackDocument` builds `const claimIds = new Set(...)` (`apps/server/src/pack-validation.ts`) to resolve `stated_reasoning` references and never compares its size to the array length; the only `DUPLICATE_*` codes in the file are for timing windows and trajectory legs [V]. Give claims 0 and 5 the same id, bind claim 0 honestly, and every `claimBindings` check passes — while an id-keyed backing test admits claim 5's unrelated prose as `ledger_bound`. Fixed by `CLAIM_ID_DUPLICATE` plus resolving the backing test by pointer rather than by id; **0 committed packs affected today** | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.2 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D133 🐞 | **The `EVIDENCE_OVERREACH` template exemption is record-level, not pattern-level, so narrowing it for one pattern widens it for four.** `check.ts`'s guard reads `(!isExplorerTemplate && !isEngineTemplate && PROSE_POINTERS.some(...))` — a record satisfying either template is exempt from **all five** prose patterns at once. `claim-backing`'s drafted §3.2(5) struck the exemption only for `/feedbackClaims/…` while also deleting the templates' must-be-a-claim-pointer clause and their byte-exact prose comparison, leaving a templated `engine_eval` free to support `/objective/summary`, `/planClasses/<i>/description`, `/deviations/<i>/note` or `/spine/…/annotations/<i>` with nothing checking it. Fixed by striking the exemption for all five patterns | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.2 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D134 🐞 | **Three measurements in `rfc/claim-backing.md` were falsifiable and one criterion was vacuous.** (a) *"0 of 241 positions is fully censused"* — **36 are** [V], all single-legal-move links of the mating spines; the true and stronger statement is **0 of the 199 choice-bearing positions**. (b) Centipawn `1` listed as appearing nowhere — it is present among 157 distinct `candidates[].centipawns` values, as is `52`; only `109`, `−106`, `−120`, `−170` are genuinely absent, and `−107`/`−110` are present, which shows value-membership is a weak instrument. (c) The Summary attached prose-**character** shares (49.0 / 64.5 / 95.4%) to claim-**count** ratios (70/131 = 53.4%, 90/131 = 68.7%, 126/131 = 96.2%). (d) Criterion 9 required *"Bucket 3 must remain non-empty"* — a threshold of 1, sitting on the boundary the owner's own DTZ-floor ruling forbids — and pinned it to *"131 of 131"*, unfalsifiable against a corpus of 166. All four corrected in place; the §1.2 and §4 baselines and the root-coverage row reproduced **exactly**, which is what shows the method was sound and only the totals slipped | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/claim-backing.md` by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D135 🐞 | **The `authored` escape's fence is one array element wide, and the drafted rules pushed every author against it.** Nothing checks that a claim labelled `author_principle` contains a principle — the only gate is the seven-member enum at `schemas/drill_pack.schema.json` `$defs/feedbackClaim.evidenceTypes`. Measured at HEAD: **0 of 36 `corpus_observed` and 0 of 8 `engine_validated` claims carry a self-declared label; 18 of 37 `tablebase_exact` do** [V]. Since every `corpus_observed` claim carries a digit-shaped explorer window (`2024-01`–`2026-07`, or `2023-01`–`2025-12` on the new packs) that registry v1 could not return, the drafted rules refused **all 36** unless relabelled — making the relabel the cheap move and converting machine-checkable claims into self-declared ones at scale. Half-fixed by binding the window and band; the residual pressure is measured by criterion 13 and escalated, not prevented | 💡 open (pressure), 🔨 (window/band bound) — found 2026-08-15 by the `claim-backing` cross-review |
| D136 🐞 | **`attachExplorerEvidence` mutates the pack twice, so deleting the prose overwrite does not stabilise `packDigest`.** Two lines apart, `explorer.ts` assigns `pack.feedbackClaims[i].text = renderExplorerFrequency(values)` **and** `pack.provenance.sources = [...new Set([...])]`, and `digestDrillPack(pack)` then runs over the mutated object. `claim-backing`'s drafted §3.5 and criterion 6 name only the first, so a faithful implementation would still move the digest and rewrite `pack.json`. The second assignment is also the only route by which the Lichess attribution reaches `provenance.sources`, so dropping it silently is a licence defect. Fixed by removing both and refusing up front with `ATTACH_SOURCE_LINE_MISSING`; §3.5 also missed that the flat-file write branch — the one `content/drafts/*.json` uses — bypasses `atomicCanonical` entirely | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.5 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D105 🐞 | **A `retryVariants` note names a pack that does not exist, and nothing can see it.** `content/drafts/queen-vs-pawn-seventh-convert.json`'s `opposite_side` entry defers its save-side sibling to a pack nobody has written — *"The companion c2 query in provenance is the seed for that hold pack"* — and because the referent is a substring of a free-text `note`, no validator can resolve it. Measured 2026-08-15 in the `format-surface` cross-review, which built the set of **79** real pack ids in `content/` and matched every one of the **11** committed `retryVariants` entries against it: **8 name a real id, 3 name none** (this one, plus the two entries in `schemas/drill_pack.example.json`, which carry no `note` at all). This is the concrete cost of *"names no referent"*: a dangling cross-reference is indistinguishable from a live one. **`format-surface`'s `RETRY_VARIANTS_NOT_EXECUTABLE` warning does NOT discharge this row** — it fires on the field, not on the unresolvable name inside the note. Closing it needs the `variantOf` array widening (that RFC's Open question 4), which is `VARIANT_PACK_UNKNOWN`-checked already (`pack-validation.ts:670-679`) | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D106 🐞 | **`targetElo` is accepted beside `strong_engine` and silently dropped.** `$defs/opponentPolicy` admits `targetElo` for all seven modes, but `appliedTargetElo` returns `undefined` whenever `health.identity.eloHonored !== true` (`apps/server/src/engine-band.ts:72`), and the shipped predicate naming the band-honouring modes — `policyUsesMaiaBand` — admits `human_common`, `theory_strict` and `practical_resistance` and **not** `strong_engine`. So an author may declare a Stockfish Elo, the format takes it, the engine ignores it, and `eloApplied` never records it. Textbook declared-vs-executable, and it cannot be fixed by honouring the value: weakened Stockfish is rejected doctrine (`AGENTS.md` §Rejected). Found 2026-08-15 in the `format-surface` cross-review while testing that RFC's per-leg narrowing, which now refuses the pair at leg level (`LEG_POLICY_ELO_UNHONORED`) — **the pack-level instance is untouched and is this row** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D107 🐞 | **Opponent modes were silently rewritten when unavailable.** The browser and group-reply service now refuse an unavailable authored mode with `POLICY_MODE_UNSUPPORTED`; neither substitutes `human_common` or the first advertised mode. The recorded off-spine `theory_strict → human_common` transition remains the sole intentional compatibility case inside the selector. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the selection-integrity batch |
| D108 🐞 | **Fixed group-reply reuse omitted `eloApplied` from engine identity.** The equality guard now includes the applied Elo, and the current target's applied band is included in the expected Maia identity. Tests pin reuse at one band and refusal across two bands. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the selection-integrity batch |
| D109 🐞 | **The browser and server built different `policyConfigDigest` values.** Both request builders now use the durable run `sessionDigest`; a regression uses deliberately different pack/session digests and proves the browser sends the session identity, while the group path asserts the same value from storage. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the selection-integrity batch |
| D117 🐞 | **`/capabilities` advertised opponent modes without executable providers.** Mode publication is now derived per requirement: Maia/opponent for `human_common` and `theory_strict`, judge for `strong_engine`, tablebase for `perfect_tablebase`, and both opponent plus tablebase for `practical_resistance`. Provider matrix tests include the empty, judge-only, opponent-only, and opponent-plus-tablebase cases. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the selection-integrity batch |
| D100 ✅ | **The live vote editor exposes the server's full contract.** Since `f2be4ed`, hosts can edit the prompt and independent plan/move labels, add or remove 2–8 options, and choose 15–600 seconds. The zero-retry browser acceptance fills eight options, verifies the cap, changes labels independently, and opens a 90-second vote. The open ledger state was stale. | ✅ closed 2026-08-15 by `live-surface-honesty` (`f2be4ed`); ledger corrected 2026-08-16 |
| D101 ✅ | **`SILENT_ASSISTANCE` is now silent in all nine fields.** Fresh profiles keep board lighting off; migrations from v1–v3 preserve their historical legal-square setting rather than silently changing an existing preference. Runtime and profile tests pin both directions. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the permission-and-correctness batch |
| D102 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-15.** The expression census and its tests now default to the canonical `content/witnesses/expression-witnesses.json`; the obsolete one-witness server fixture was removed. *Original:* **The expression census reads its witnesses from a server test fixture by default.** The command defaulted to `apps/server/src/fixtures/expression-witnesses.json`, while the 26 content witnesses required an explicit override. | ✅ closed by the queued mechanical sweep |
| D103 🐞 | **A shape entry has nowhere to record why its trigger says what it says.** D75's fix added two clauses to `rook-4v3-same-side`'s trigger to exclude Philidor positions the loose form admitted; the *reason* those clauses exist is now recoverable only from a commit message. The wave tried a `triggerNote` and the shape-entry schema rejected it — `additionalProperties: false`. Called by the wave *"the cheapest schema addition this wave found"*, and it is the shape-library counterpart of the provenance the pack format already carries everywhere else. The next author to find the trigger over-tight will loosen it and re-open D75 | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D104 🐞 | **The served-Najdorf browser walkthrough is nondeterministic after a branch move.** On an unchanged post-archive tree, one zero-retry `make test-browser` run timed out waiting for `Active line 4 plies` after `clickMove("d1", "d2")` (**23 passed, 1 failed, 1 baseline skip**); the immediately preceding and following full runs both passed **24 with the same skip**. This is not the retired stale-bounds D14 failure: it is the run state failing to advance or render after the move. Retrying in configuration remains forbidden; the race needs its own diagnosis. **2026-08-16 negative reproduction:** the exact test passed 20/20 isolated zero-retry repetitions plus one full-suite run; no patch was inferred from the absence. | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-15; not reproduced in 21 fresh runs on 2026-08-16 |
| D113 💡 | **Hint DISTANCE as an assistance axis — the owner's 2026-08-15 idea, and the mechanism for it already ships unused.** Today a hint is all-or-nothing: rung 0 states a fact, rung 5 states an authored claim, and neither has a *dial*. The proposal is a hint whose vagueness is **derived rather than chosen** — point at a square, a piece, a ply-distance or a move, four increasing disclosures over **one** piece of evidence. `bestline` records carry the full PV (`movesUci`) and the plumbing is end-to-end — `api.ts` → `rest.ts:1354` → `evidence-queue.ts:394` → `compare.ts:204` — yet **0 of 764 committed records are `bestline`** (391 `engine_eval`, 341 `tablebase_result`, 32 `position_legality`, zero bestlines). Same shape as the tempo layer and `arrows`: shipped, plumbed, never authored against. **This is what the campaign's loadout slots want to be made of** — a slot that buys *how close to the answer* you may look is opportunity cost that does not price retrying | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-15 |
| D114 🐞 | **Eval swing ALONG a principal variation is the wrong instrument for "where does it get sharp", and it is wrong by construction.** The PV is the line where the evaluation *does not* move — that is what makes it the PV — so score fluctuation along it detects search instability, not a threat. The signal the hint idea actually wants is the **MultiPV gap**: best minus second-best at a ply. A large gap is mechanically *"one move holds here and everything else drops X"*, which is a rung-2 fact about the position rather than a judgement about the learner. Recorded because the intuition is right and the instrument is subtly wrong, and building the obvious version would produce a hint that fires on search noise. `MultiPV` is already plumbed (`strong-engine.ts:30,55`, `evidence-queue.ts:338`) and `api.ts:925` requests `bestline` at `multiPv: 1` — the one setting that cannot produce the gap | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D115 🐞 | **A PV-derived hint is a CONDITIONAL presented as a prediction unless its scope is carried.** *"Something happens on f7 in three moves"* is true only under best play by both sides; the learner deviating voids it, and the learner is the one being hinted. `design/05` §2's rung-0 scope corrections already establish the rule — *"b5 is denied **while the a4 pawn stands**"* — and this is the same failure one rung up. Any hint drawn from a line must say **under whose play**, or it is the `outpaced` trap in a new location | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D116 💡 | **Widen the evidence PACKET, not the LLM's licence — the transformation of the owner's 2026-08-15 "doesn't it have access to ALL the info" question.** It does not, and the gap is exact: `evidencePacket` (`guidance.ts:30`) carries `structuralReading` + `pivotalMarkers` (rung 0), `endgameReading` (rung 1, tablebase) and matched shape plans + authored items (rung 5) — **rungs 0, 1 and 5, three of six.** Absent entirely: **rung 2 engine evaluation, rung 3 Maia, rung 4 corpus frequency** — precisely the three the owner named. The architecture is the *opposite* of a coach with access to everything: `renderVoice` hands the model a closed list of pre-computed `sentences`, `voiceCheck` validates the output against that packet, and two failures fall back to the deterministic join. That is ADR-0005 implemented rather than merely stated. **So the honest move is transformation, not exemption** (`design/02` §Adoption posture, owner 2026-08-14: *"a conflict with an invariant is a design prompt, not a veto"*): give the renderer more **validated** facts to word, never more freedom to reason. Widening the packet to carry rungs 2–4 keeps `voiceCheck` binding and is law-8-legal by construction; loosening `voiceCheck` is the named anti-pattern. Depends on [[D113]]/[[D114]] for the threat vocabulary the packet would carry | 💡 open, owner question 2026-08-15 |
| D118 🐞 | **The evidence ledger and the run-time packet are two disconnected worlds, and this is the largest unexploited asset in the repo.** `content/**/*.evidence.json` holds **764 records** — 391 `engine_eval`, 341 `tablebase_result`, 32 `position_legality` — every one machine-validated with full provenance. **None of it reaches a learner.** `evidencePacket` (`guidance.ts:30`) takes `{run, node, pack.document, authored, shapes}`; a pack's evidence ledger is a *sibling file* that authoring-time validation reads and the runtime never opens. The two consumers of position facts, `classifyPhase(fen)` and `structuralReading(fen)`, take **a FEN and nothing else** — so the classifier and the structural detector cannot see the engine even in principle. The ledger is currently a *content-validation artefact* that stops at the commit; a run re-derives from scratch what the corpus already proved. This is the substrate [[D116]] wants and the reason it is cheap: the evidence exists, is provenance-carrying, and is already digest-bound | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D119 🐞 | **Claude reported the rung coverage of `evidencePacket` as "rungs 0, 1, 5 — three of six". Wrong, and withdrawn.** It also carries **rung 3**: `pivotalMarkers` emits `human_divergence` from the opponent selection's **recorded Maia policy masses** (`pivotal.ts:36-38`), rendered as *"N's recorded policy split: 41% / 22% / 15% of recorded mass"* (`:107`). It is narrow — only where the distribution is flat (`max ≤ 0.5` and `≥ 3` candidates at `≥ 0.15`) and only about the *opponent's own* selection — but it is rung 3 in the packet. The corrected map is **rungs 0, 1, 3, 5 present; rung 2 (engine evaluation) and rung 4 (corpus frequency) absent.** Recorded because the error understated what ships and would have led an implementer to rebuild a path that exists | 🐞 **THE CORRECTION WAS ITSELF WRONG — corrected again 2026-08-16, verified by claude at HEAD.** The rung-3 half stands: `human_divergence` markers really do carry recorded Maia policy masses. **The rung-1 claim does not.** `endgameReading` **never touches a tablebase** — it is a pure material census over a FEN (`endgame.ts`: `classifyPhase`, then piece-role counts), and its own provenance string is literally *"Tabiya's material-census convention"*. It is **rung 0**, not rung 1. **The true map is rungs 0, 3 and 5 present; rungs 1, 2 and 4 absent.** Found by the `evidence-at-runtime` draft (D139). **Two wrong answers in a row about the same six-item list, and the shared cause is the lesson: both were read off function NAMES rather than function BODIES.** `endgameReading` sounds like a tablebase probe and is not; `pivotalMarkers` sounds like rung-0 geometry and carries rung 3. **Rung membership is a property of what a function reads, and may only be asserted after reading it.** Sibling of D161 (a fabricated illustration) and of the two line-based greps that produced confident wrong answers on 2026-08-15 — the same failure mode, which is asserting from a plausible surface instead of the source |
| D120 🐞 | **Claude described the LLM as reaching one narrow surface. Wrong, and withdrawn.** `VoiceScope` has **six** values — `marker`, `reading`, `steering`, `story`, `compare`, `reasoning` (`guidance.ts:20`) — so the renderer is already wired to the highlight cards, the story screen, the compare view and the reasoning surface. **The breadth is in the SURFACES; the narrowness is in the EVIDENCE**, because all six scopes construct the same `evidencePacket`. That is the correct diagnosis of the owner's *"missed opportunity"*: nothing needs new wiring to reach a surface — the packet needs more in it | 💡 open, correction 2026-08-15 |
| D138 🐞 | **`content/packs/` is empty — the entire authored corpus is dev-only, and no RFC says so.** The directory holds `.gitkeep` and nothing else; all **47** authored packs and all **32** evidence ledgers live in `content/drafts/`, which `PackRegistry.loadDefault` includes **only when `development === true`** (a `draftFile` outside development is a `TypeError`). So every pack-attached capability in flight — bound `deviation.cost`, engine conditions, claim bindings, recorded readings — measures its payoff against a corpus a production deployment does not load, where the only pack is `schemas/drill_pack.example.json`. Not an argument against any of them; an argument against quoting the number without the contingency. Found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md`, whose §8.4 states it rather than assuming it away | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D139 🐞 | **The evidence packet's "rung 1" never touches a tablebase.** [[D116]] and [[D119]] both read `endgameReading` as the packet's rung-1 layer. It is not: `endgameReading(fen)` calls `classifyPhase`, counts material, and returns one of five endgame type labels plus up to three technique names, with `provenanceNote = "Tabiya's material-census convention"`. No `category`, no `dtz`, no `dtm`, no piece-count abstention contract, no probe of any kind. Its honest rung is **0** — a material census is arithmetic over the position. The packet's true map at HEAD is therefore **rungs 0, 3 and 5**, with **1, 2 and 4 all absent**, which makes the ledger's 341 `tablebase_result` records a genuine gain rather than a duplication. Escalated as a row rather than written into `design/05`, per law 5 | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D140 🐞 | **One of five evidence-packet construction sites omitted the packet's disclosure gate.** `/voice` (both branches) and `/speech` called `requireGuidanceDisclosure(access)`, while `/reasoning-review` gated only on read authorization and a role-blind checkpoint predicate. Because `evidencePacket` includes rung-3 `human_divergence` sentences, a participant or spectator could cause those bytes to reach the external voice provider. `/reasoning-review` now applies the same `guidanceAccess` disclosure gate before packet construction; a regression proves participant and spectator requests are refused before the provider is called while the host path remains available. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the disclosure gate and role regression |
| D141 🐞 | **Fifteen of forty-seven authored draft packs have no evidence ledger, and the corpus is two-tier with only one bit published about it.** The ungrounded fifteen are all 2026-08-15 middlegame-wave packs: `berlin-queenless-press`, `carlsbad-minority-attack`, `conversion-up-a-piece`, `dragon-yugoslav-race`, `french-advance-chain-white`, `grunfeld-exchange-fianchetto`, `iqp-black-tarrasch-defence`, `iqp-white-panov-attack`, `kid-mar-del-plata-white`, `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze`, `nimzo-doubled-c-pawns`, `open-centre-ruy-exchange`, `rook-4v3-same-side`, `trajectory-caro-advance-chain-bishops`, `trajectory-qgd-exchange-minority`. Grounded coverage is **68%** of authored packs (32/47), and the only signal any surface carries is the binary `assessmentGrounding` (`ledger_verified` / `unverified`) — so "no instrument was ever run for this pack" and "the ledger drifted from the document" are indistinguishable to a learner. Same shape as [[D125]] one tier down: *not authored* and *not reachable* look the same | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D142 💡 | **The runtime already opens every pack's evidence ledger and throws it away — [[D118]]'s cost is a discarded object, not a missing contract.** `PackRegistry.loadDefault` resolves `<stem>.evidence.json` through `sidecarPaths`, reads it with `optionalJson`, and hands it to `fromDocuments`, where exactly one call consumes it: `assessmentGrounding({document, ledger, manifest})`, which returns a **two-valued string**. The ledger object then falls out of scope; `PackRecord` has seven fields and none of them is the ledger. So the file read, the parse and the digest-linkage check are already paid for every pack, on every start, and **one bit of 764 records is retained**. This is why the fix needs no pack version, no run version and no migration — the join is keeping an object that is already in hand | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D143 🐞 | **No position in the corpus carries both an engine reading and a tablebase reading, so nothing can corroborate one instrument against the other.** Across the 32 committed ledgers: **569** distinct anchor FENs, of which **281** have an `engine_eval` and **288** have a `tablebase_result` — 281 + 288 = 569 exactly, with zero overlap. The instruments partition the corpus **by pack**: 17 packs are 100% Stockfish (openings), 15 are 100% Syzygy (endgames), none mixes. Consequences worth having written down: no cross-instrument agreement check is possible on committed data; any design assuming a position can be read two ways is assuming a corpus that does not exist; and the tablebase/engine split is a property of the authoring pipeline (`verifyEngineDraft` vs `verifySyzygyDraft` each stamp only their own kind), not of the positions | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D144 💡 | **158 FENs are recorded in more than one pack's ledger, and single-ledger resolution refuses all of them.** Of 569 distinct anchor FENs, 396 appear once, 159 twice, 8 three times, 4 four times and 2 five times — opening transpositions across sibling repertoire packs. `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.3 established the rule that makes them unusable: *"a binding that reaches outside its own digest is unverifiable"*, so a pack may resolve only against its own ledger. That is correct for prose binding and it is inherited by any runtime projection for the same reason — but it is also the single cheapest coverage widening available to the pack-attached evidence work, and admitting it would make a pack's evidence depend on which other packs happen to be loaded. An owner call, not a tooling one. Sibling of `anti-scandinavian-white`'s split backing in [[D110]]'s neighbourhood: the same position is measured in one pack and unavailable in the next | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D145 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` validates only `packet.sentences`, so any packet field that produces no sentence is invisible to the renderer AND to the check.** The check builds `const source = packet.sentences.join("\n")` and tests renderer output against that string alone; `ExternalHttpVoiceProvider.render` transmits `{personaPrompt, sentences, scope}` and nothing else. `EvidencePacket` has nine fields and eight of them are server-local bookkeeping. So [[D116]]'s *"widen the evidence packet"* means, mechanically, **widen `sentences`** — via a frozen deterministic renderer in the `renderEndgameReading` / `renderPivotalMarker` mould — and a structured field added without one is decoration that no learner and no validator will ever see. Recorded because it is the first thing any implementer of D116 or D118 will get wrong, and because it is also the reason the widening is law-8-safe: a sentence is what the check binds | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D146 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` is token-membership, not proposition-checking — and the residual gets more expensive with every rung added to the packet.** It extracts squares, UCI, SAN, `CHESS_LEXICON` nouns, `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` and `PRESCRIPTIVE_VERBS` from the renderer's output and refuses any that is not a substring of the packet's sentences. It cannot refuse a *join*: two admitted facts recombined into a claim neither record makes passes, because every token was present. Pre-existing and structural, but the cost rises sharply with rung 2, because a centipawn number is exactly the token that invites a causal join ("+0.54, so the knight on e5 is strong" — where `+0.54` came from a record and `knight`/`e5` came from the rung-0 structural sentences). The two things that blunt it are not fixes: past tense with authoring attribution in the frozen sentence, and the banned-judgement vocabulary. This is the ceiling of any mechanical check over free prose — the same ceiling `rfc/claim-backing.md` §2 names for its span binding (*"it does not guarantee that the sentence composed from those fragments is true"*) | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D147 💡 | **Rung 4 reaches a live surface but never the evidence packet, so corpus frequency can be shown and can never be worded.** `GET /runs/:id/corpus` ships: a disclosure-gated, ephemeral, explicitly LLM-free explorer panel with closed sentences and the byte-fixed `CORPUS_GUARD` line, gated by the same `permittedAssistance` predicate as the human split. `EvidencePacket` has no rung-4 field and no rung-4 sentence, so `renderVoice` cannot voice it, `voiceCheck` does not bind it, and the six `VoiceScope` surfaces are blind to it. The two rung-4 paths are unjoined, which means [[D119]]'s *"rung 4 absent"* is true of the packet and false of the product — and joining them is not a plumbing job, because the packet path would need an `explorer_position_census` record kind (`rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.7) rather than a live query, to keep the packet free of instrument calls | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D158 💡 | **`arrows` is THREE different things sharing one field name, and the owner's 2026-08-15 ruling is that all three need a home — not that the field is retired.** Offered retire / `unmeasured` / retire-with-the-promise-recorded, the owner refused all three: *"these are useful for coaching, teaching, streaming, ideating… during play you want to be able to draw arrows or highlight shit… in drill packs sure they are optional… we need to find a place for them."* **`rfc/format-surface.md` and claude both analysed `arrows` purely as a SYSTEM-OUTPUT channel** — a rung-gated disclosure — which is why retirement looked right. The owner's primary want is the opposite thing. Verified at HEAD: **(1) user-drawn annotation is one boolean away and switched off** — `Chessboard.svelte` sets `drawable: { enabled: false, visible: true, autoShapes: [...overlays] }`, and chessground supports user drawing natively. **A mark the learner draws themselves discloses nothing and asserts nothing** — the product makes no claim — so it is *outside the assistance ladder entirely*, not a rung-0 case. **(2) Host/teacher-drawn marks relayed to viewers** are a *person's* claim, not the product's, so they need attribution rather than a rung — `teacher-surface` and `live-surface-honesty` territory, and for streaming and teaching this is the core communication tool. **(3) System-drawn directed marks** are the only one the ladder governs, and they genuinely have no producer: `boardOverlays` (`DrillScreen.svelte:298`) is derived from **`boardLighting`, not `arrows`**, and emits `{orig: square}` with **no `dest`** — square highlights, not arrows, exactly as the RFC found. Meanwhile `assistance.arrows` is read by **`AssistanceSettings.svelte` alone**: a three-value dropdown selecting a preference nothing consumes | 💡 **OWNER RULED 2026-08-15: find all three a place.** Leg 1 is the cheapest real feature in the repo and is nobody's; leg 2 belongs to the live/teacher surfaces; leg 3 stays `unmeasured` with the named gap (the structural reader emits square *sets*). `format-surface` may not retire the field |
| D159 💡 | **Learner-drawn board annotation — one boolean, and it is nobody's.** Split out of the `arrows` ruling (2026-08-15) as its own row because it is not an assistance feature and was hiding inside one. `Chessboard.svelte` sets `drawable: { enabled: false, visible: true, autoShapes: [...overlays] }`; chessground supports learner drawing natively and the product switches it off. A mark the learner draws is **their own thought** — the product asserts nothing, so law 8, the assistance ladder and the disclosure model are all silent on it, and it needs no rung, no permission and no evidence. The owner names it for **coaching, teaching, streaming and ideating**, and it is the core communication tool in the last two. Probably the highest value-to-cost item in the ledger; the open work is persistence (are marks per-node, per-branch, exported with the PGN?) and the relay path for hosts, not the drawing itself | 💡 open, owner ruling 2026-08-15 |
| D160 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16 by `vocabulary-wiring`.** `docs/engine-workers.md` now states the superseding clause: `record` covers every instrument, stateful or not, and persists what it actually applied and returned. No fourth attest law was created and the archived RFC remains immutable. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by `vocabulary-wiring` |
| D161 🐞 | **Claude fabricated a result split inside the ruling that admits result splits, and paired it with a real count.** D126's illustration read *"At this position 1400–1800 players scored **47/31/22** over **5,069** games."* The count is real — the Maroczy root, reproduced exactly by a live re-query. **The split is invented.** The measured value is **2,629 / 371 / 2,069 = 51.9 / 7.3 / 40.8**. Caught by the explorer-grounding wave and **corrected in place**. This is **D110's exact shape one tier up**: a plausible cardinal breakdown attached to a genuine population, in the document every content agent reads first, teaching the boundary it violates. Two things make it worse than a typo and both are the lesson: the number was **not plausible for the band** — a 31% draw rate occurs **nowhere** in the eleven positions censused, whose range is **4.7–9.8%** — so it was generated from a wrong mental model (GM draw rates) rather than mistyped; and it survived because **an illustration reads as prose, not as a claim**, so no instrument, validator or reviewer was pointed at it. **Consequence: worked examples in ledger and design tier are claims and must carry their provenance or be marked synthetic.** `rfc/claim-backing.md`'s residual sweep would not catch this either — it governs pack prose, not process documents | ✅ **corrected 2026-08-16**. Paired with **D154**, which is the wave's own filing of the same finding; this row carries the correction and the standing rule, that one carries the measurement; the standing rule it produces is open |
| D162 🐞 | **A production deployment serves ONE pack — the schema example — and none of the 47 authored ones.** `PackRegistry.loadDefault` builds `productionPaths` as `[schemas/drill_pack.example.json, ...jsonFiles("content/packs/")]`, and **`content/packs/` contains only `.gitkeep`**. `content/drafts/` — all 47 authored packs, including tonight's eleven middlegame packs and every graded endgame — loads **only in development mode**, and `draftFile` outside development is a hard `TypeError`. So the entire content era has produced **nothing a deployed instance would serve**. Found 2026-08-16 while checking `evidence-at-runtime`'s claim that its payoff is zero in production; that claim is correct and this is the reason, but the consequence is far larger than that RFC — it applies to every pack, every ledger, every shape wiring and every gate that reasons about "the corpus". **Nothing can graduate today either: 0 of 47 packs has an empty `provenance.graduationBlockers`**, so the drafts→packs path is not merely unused, it is unmet by every candidate. This is not a bug in the loader — the split is deliberate and the gate is honest. It is a **product-state fact nobody had stated**: the deployment story and the content story have never been connected, and `design/02`'s hosted-multi-user ruling makes that gap live rather than theoretical | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16, **owner-gated** |
| D163 🔨 | **Owner ruling 2026-08-16: route the claim, do not forbid the sentence — an instrument label is earned per *segment*, and an unbound assertion demotes the label rather than deleting the prose.** [[D131]] escalated the residual sweep's ceiling as unfixable and the cross-review proposed shipping with it stated; the owner refused, on the ground that the boundary was never *"never say Black is better"* but ***"never say it AS CORPUS OBSERVATION"***. `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.4a specifies it: `text` is cut at a frozen boundary set (sentence terminators, `;`/`:`, dashes, and the inference markers `so`/`therefore`/`thus`/`hence`/`which means`/`because`/`since`), a segment holding a validating instrument span is instrument-attributed, **every other segment is author-attributed**, and there is no unattributed remainder. Two refusals follow — `CLAIM_AUTHOR_LABEL_REQUIRED` (authored segment without `author_principle`) and **`CLAIM_LABEL_UNEARNED`** (an instrument label with zero instrument-attributed segments), the second being the only refusal in the RFC that fires on the absence of everything and therefore the only one that reaches *"Black is better here"*, which has no span to bind and no token to sweep. `CLAIM_AUTHORED_SPAN_UNLABELLED` is withdrawn: a refusal of the sentence becomes a routing of the label | 🔨 owned by `rfc/claim-backing.md` §2.1/§3.4a, ruled 2026-08-16 |
| D164 🐞 | **The unbound-proposition hole is 76% of the machine-labelled corpus, not a corner of it.** Cutting the **91** machine-labelled claims at HEAD (`ab662f9`) on `claim-backing` §3.4a's frozen boundary set, **69 carry at least one segment with no cardinal, no ordinal, no SAN or square token and no result word** — **126 such segments**, provenance 53 sentence-initial after a terminator, 31 claim-initial, 26 after `;`/`:`, 9 after a dash, 7 after an inference marker [V]. [[D131]] was filed as a stated ceiling; it is the load-bearing wall. Corroborating shape: eleven `da77c56` claims hand-write the mechanism's job into the prose (*"Stated as what happened, per the owner ruling … No move, plan or side in this pack is graded from these numbers"*), which is an author compensating for a missing disposition. The measurement is what turned the cross-review's *state-and-ship* into an owner refusal | 🔨 measured and answered by [[D163]]'s routing, found 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing` round 2 |
| D165 🔨 | **Owner ruling 2026-08-16: `author_principle` becomes a resolving reference against a principle registry, because the rung the routing targets has to be real first.** [[D135]] found the label gates nothing — one member of a seven-member enum, with nothing checking that a claim carrying it contains a principle — and the cross-review offered measure-and-escalate. The owner ruled build. `content/principles/<id>.json`, loaded by `PrincipleRegistry.loadDefault` mirroring `ShapeRegistry.loadDefault` member for member, validated against a new `schemas/principle_entry.schema.json` at `urn:chess-tabiya:schema:principle-entry:0.1`. An entry carries `id`, `version`, `name`, **`statement`**, `phases` (the `SHAPE_REFERENCE_NEVER_PRESENT` analogue, raising `CLAIM_PRINCIPLE_OFF_PHASE` at warning), a frozen `standsOn` enum (`chess_tradition`/`authors_practice`/`instrument_pattern`), **`counterCase`** (the `typicalMistakes` analogue — *when the principle is wrong*, required and non-empty, because `design/05` rung 5 says an authored claim *"can simply be wrong"* and an entry that cannot say when is a slogan) and `shape_entry`'s `$defs/provenance` verbatim. `official` channel only | 🔨 owned by `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.10, ruled 2026-08-16 |
| D166 🐞 | **A rate hides its denominator and a count carries it — the one refusal [[D163]]'s routing must not lift.** Routing an unbound assertion to the author is honest for a proposition and is **laundering** for a measurement: attributing *"…f5 scores 90.9% for White"* (Grünfeld, **11 games**, below the 100-game floor at which `explorer.ts` abstains) to the author converts an inadmissible reading into an admissible opinion. `claim-backing` §3.4a step 3(3) therefore refuses a **rate** — a token normalising under the percentage or `displayCp` entries of the frozen table — inside an author-attributed segment with `CLAIM_READING_UNATTRIBUTED` at error, **and no label lifts it**. Raw counts are *not* refused: *"d4-d5 scored 1/0/6 over seven games"* states its population in the same breath, so a learner reading it at rung 5 under the author's name can discount it. This asymmetry is the whole difference between the ruling and `claim-backing` §2's rejected candidate (E) | 🔨 specified in `claim-backing` §3.4a, found 2026-08-16 by round 2 |
| D167 🐞 | **`feedback-delivery`'s `binding` field is two-valued and the routing produces three dispositions, so a mixed claim would render as fully backed.** C4's `claim` arm carries `binding: "ledger_bound" \| "self_declared"` and C8 renders two provenance lines from it. A claim binding one percentage and appending an authored verdict is `ledger_bound` under C6 — **indistinguishable from a claim every word of which is a re-derived instrument reading**. The bound number buys the badge and the verdict rides in under it, which is the owner's own test of the ruling: *a routing that relabels in the ledger but renders identically has changed nothing*. `claim-backing` §3.11 asks that RFC for `author_attributed` as a third value plus `authorSpans[]` and a projected `principles[]`, and for a third C8 line naming the principle's `statement` **and its `counterCase`** — which is what makes [[D165]]'s registry pay for its migration instead of being validator bookkeeping | 💡 open, owned by `rfc/feedback-delivery.md` C4/C8, found 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing` round 2 |
| D168 🐞 | **`feedback-delivery`'s C9 goes from correct to load-bearing under the routing, because `voiceCheck`'s allow-list is computed FROM the packet.** [[D145]]: `voiceCheck` builds `source = packet.sentences.join("\n")` and `absentWords` permits an output word **iff** it appears there — so widening the packet widens the renderer's licence rather than narrowing it. [[D163]]'s routing deliberately increases the verdict prose inside claims. Re-measured at HEAD over all **182** claims: **57 carry a `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` word and 109 carry a `PRESCRIPTIVE_VERBS` word** [V], against that RFC's 44 and 75 over 131. C9 (`authoredText` silent on `kind: "claim"`) already closes it and **must not be weakened**; the successor rule for anyone widening the packet later is *instrument-attributed segments only*, which `claim-backing` §3.4a's cut is the first mechanism in the repo able to express | 💡 open (a standing constraint, not a defect to fix), found 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing` round 2 |
| D169 🐞 | **Pack schema 0.26's release is WITHDRAWN and the lane is claimed — a headline finding two rounds carried, inverted by [[D165]].** `$defs/feedbackClaim.evidenceTypes` is a bare enum array with nowhere to put a principle id, so a resolving `author_principle` needs `$defs/feedbackClaim.principles`, which is a `$defs` change and therefore a pack lane. Ledger-side was rejected on a measurement, not a preference: **18 of the 81 `author_principle` claims sit in packs with no evidence ledger** [V], so a binding-side reference would leave 22% of the label's uses resolving against nothing — and those 18 are in the same fifteen packs [[D128]] is about. The lane pays for a second thing free: `$defs/feedbackClaim` flips to `additionalProperties: false`, **closing [[D112]]** at zero corpus cost, since **all 182 committed claims have the key set exactly `{id, text, evidenceTypes}`** — 182 of 182, one distinct shape [V]. `rfc/README.md`'s Active row and pack register both record the release and need reversing; the next free lane after this claim is **0.27** | 🔨 claimed by `rfc/claim-backing.md` §5.1, found 2026-08-16 by round 2 |
| D170 🐞 | **The principle-registry migration edits 35 packs and moves their content digests — the cost the withdrawn *"no committed pack byte changes"* was hiding.** All **81** `author_principle` claims gain a `principles` array, across **35 pack files**, of which **29 have an evidence ledger and 6 do not** [V]. Each of the 29 stored `packDigest` values goes stale. The blast radius is bounded and the reason it is survivable is worth pinning: `check.ts` raises **`EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE` at `"warning"`, not error** [V], so the corpus goes noisy rather than red and the re-confirmation — a `verify-draft` re-run that rewrites `packDigest` from `digestDrillPack(pack)` — can be sequenced across 29 sidecars instead of landing atomically. Recorded because a migration priced at zero in two rounds is not zero, and the number is 29 | 🔨 priced in `claim-backing` §3.10, found 2026-08-16 by round 2 |
| D171 🐞 | **The principle registry is ~12 entries for 81 claims, and one of its twelve families is a finding rather than a cluster.** At HEAD, **81 claims across 35 packs carry `author_principle`; 34 carry it alone; 15 also carry a machine label (all `tablebase_exact`); 18 sit in ledger-less packs** [V]. The ids are not 81 things: **`result-not-moves` recurs 13 times**, `order-on-your-side` twice, the other 66 are unique [V]. Clustering by assertion gives roughly twelve families, so day one is of the order of the shape library's 25 entries — the test that this is a registry and not a rename. **The finding is the twelfth family:** ~8 claims (`window-thresholds-are-authored`, `phases-are-authored`, `wall-then-king`, `shape-fires-late`, `fortress-is-not-a-census` and siblings) assert nothing about chess — they say which parts of the pack are authored and which are machine truth. That is **provenance narration wearing a rung-5 label**, the most honest prose in the corpus sitting in the wrong slot; the clean answer is a `provenance_note` member on `evidenceTypes`, which is intent tier and law 5 and is not claimed | 💡 open (the eighth enum member); 🔨 (housed under one `authors_practice` entry meanwhile) — found 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing` round 2 |
| D172 🐞 | **`hypothesis` and `derived_feature` gate nothing either, so [[D135]]'s one-array-element escape would simply move one label to the left.** The drafted `authored`-span fence accepted any of `author_principle`, `hypothesis` or `derived_feature`; [[D165]] makes only the first resolve against anything, so an author unwilling to publish a principle entry writes `hypothesis` and nothing checks that either. `claim-backing` §3.4 therefore narrows the fence to **`author_principle` alone** — the other two stay valid `evidenceTypes` members and are not deprecated, they simply stop licensing an author-attributed segment. Measured cost at HEAD: **0 of 52 `corpus_observed` and 0 of 8 `engine_validated` claims carry any self-declared label; 18 of 37 `tablebase_exact` do, and 15 of those 18 are already `author_principle`** [V] — so the narrowing costs **three claims**, and all three (`mate-bishop-knight/opponent-plays-the-spine`, `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight/spine-is-the-opponent`, `philidor-passive-rook-convert/why-the-skewer-works`) are **Bucket 3 rows**. The label that gated nothing had drifted onto exactly the claims with nothing behind them | 🔨 closed by `claim-backing` §3.4's fence narrowing, found 2026-08-16 by round 2 |

Coverage is per instrument, empty/absent tables refuse, the eight missing Stockfish options have explicit dispositions, and the public register test requires substantive Stockfish and Maia rows rather than `expect.any(Array)`. *Original:* the register was tested only against a hand-authored matching list and passed vacuously | ✅ closed by the returned `engine-leverage` implementation |
| D194 🐞 | **`opponent.move_selected` carried Stockfish's centipawn score into the ungated public event stream.** The durable event still retains exact candidate measurements, but `publicEvents`, mutation `emitted` projections, and public run snapshots now remove `scoreCp` and `wdl` until feedback disclosure opens while leaving the selected move available. Regressions pin both the withheld and disclosed projections. The separate direct-response channel is tracked as D203 rather than hidden inside this closure | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by disclosure-aware selection projection |
| D195 🐞 | **Nearly half the recorded `scoreCp` values were aspiration-window bounds, not evaluations.** `candidateLines` now refuses to turn `upperbound`/`lowerbound` lines (including their associated WDL) into exact candidate measurements, with a regression using an exact line followed by a final bound. The remaining representational gap is explicit: `score mate` has no `SelectionCandidate` field, so criterion 17 still cannot call two `undefined === undefined` comparisons score agreements without a versioned format change | 🐞 narrowed 2026-08-16; mate-score representation remains open |
| D196 🐞 | **A failed tablebase probe silently suppressed ENGINE evidence for the same node.** `EvidenceJobFailure` now retains `kind`, and story completion filters failures to `eval` just as it already filtered outstanding jobs. A regression first records a tablebase failure at an imported run's root, then proves the complete Stockfish story pass is still enqueued | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by kind-scoped evidence failures |
| D197 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16.** Automatic tablebase evidence now uses a one-attempt-per-node producer lane that enqueues only while the evidence queue is idle; busy and failed producer probes are dropped without entering the failure ledger. Explicit tablebase requests keep their ordinary refusal semantics. *Original:* §3.6's cache-first, best-effort producer budget did not ship | ✅ closed by the returned `engine-leverage` implementation |
| D198 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16.** Runtime firing, deviation reachability validation, and the all-disabled check now share `guard-conditions.ts`; learner-relative tablebase mapping and category ordering reuse the sourcing implementation. *Original:* guard desugaring shipped as independent runtime and validator copies | ✅ closed by the returned `engine-leverage` implementation |
| D199 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16.** Scalar overrides now replace or remove matching engine arms in place and leave every unrelated authored condition at its declared index; a regression pins engine-before-tablebase order. *Original:* overrides rebuilt and reordered the condition list | ✅ closed by the returned `engine-leverage` implementation |
| D200 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16.** Explicit positive scalar shorthands are merged into an authored `conditions[]` list, and runtime plus validation consume the same result. A regression pins the mixed declaration. *Original:* declaring `conditions` silently killed `evalSwingCp`/`fireOnMate` | ✅ closed by the returned `engine-leverage` implementation |
| D201 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16.** `verify-draft` checks cost backing against current-invocation records before merging preserved sidecars; mate/category equality is structural rather than JSON-order-sensitive; contradictions use `VERIFY_DEVIATION_COST_CONTRADICTED`. *Original:* the cost checker lost provenance and compared author JSON by serialization order | ✅ closed by the returned `engine-leverage` implementation |
| D202 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16.** Rare moves retain their observed count/share, but their W/D/L split is withheld below an explicit 100-game per-move floor; a four-game regression proves the percentage never renders. *Original:* per-move explorer outcomes rendered to 0.1% without a per-move sample floor | ✅ closed by the returned `engine-leverage` implementation |
| D203 🐞 | **`content/candidates/`'s 36 pack documents are inside the closed schema policy and outside every graduation instrument.** `packages/schema/src/drill-pack.test.ts:209-227` (*"validates every committed pack document under the closed policy"*) enumerates `content/drafts/*.json` **and** `content/candidates/*/pack.json`, so all 36 candidate packs validate against `drill_pack.schema.json` — and they carry **143 bare-string `graduationBlockers` entries in 7 distinct texts**, mostly emitter placeholders. `rfc/pack-graduation.md` scoped its migration to the 240 entries in 47 draft packs, so typing the field would redden `make verify` from a directory no section, report or criterion mentions. The general form: **a "corpus-wide" count is only as wide as the `readdir` that produced it, and this repo has four different ones** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; fixed in that RFC's §3.1a/§4.0 |
