| D223 🐞 | **`assessmentGrounding` never compares the ledger to the pack, so `ledger_verified` does not mean "this ledger describes this document".** It validates ledger and manifest, runs `linkage` (record → **manifest entry**, by `sourceId`/`retrievedAt`, never touching the document), then requires **exactly one** record matching `/start/fen` and `objective.grading.assessedBy`. **`ledger.packDigest` is never read on the runtime path** (`sourcing/ledger-validation.ts`); the repository's only digest check is `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE` in `sourcing/check.ts`, severity **`warning`**, in the authoring CLI. Measured at `a7e700d` by evaluating the shipped function over all 32 committed ledgers: **32 of 32 return `ledger_verified`, including all 5 whose `packDigest` is stale** — `mate-bishop-knight`, `mate-k-q-technique`, `mate-k-r-technique`, `philidor-passive-rook-convert`, `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight`, the same five [[D179]] found at `1b89123` — carrying **186 records, 181 of them machine readings** [V]. So the one bit the runtime keeps out of 764 records attests **one** record, not the ledger, and it is published on `GET /packs/:id` as `objective.grading.grounding`. `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` §2.1 adds a digest conjunct for its own consumer; the general defect is this row | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D224 🐞 | **`transposeKey` collides inside a single pack's ledger, so "one authored position, one record" is false.** `lucena-bridge-convert` records `8/1P2k3/2K5/8/3R4/8/8/1r6 b - -` **twice** — halfmove clock **9** supporting a spine pointer, clock **13** supporting `/deviations/7/moveUci` — with byte-identical values (`category: "loss"`, `dtz: -8`, `precise_dtz: -8`, `dtm: -28`). Across all 32 ledgers, **732 admitted records occupy 731 distinct `transposeKey`s**; it is the only collision, and there are zero among the 391 `engine_eval` records [V]. Any position-keyed index over a ledger must be multi-valued: a scalar `Map` drops one record and which one depends on iteration order. Pointer coverage is unaffected (the two records back two different pointers) | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D225 🐞 | **A transposition index measured on the authored tree measures the wrong set, and hides an 11.6% refusal.** `evidence-at-runtime` §2.2 admits a `tablebase_result` only when the node's halfmove clock equals the record's, and expected that to cost nothing "because records are produced by replaying the same moves" — true, and true only of the tree. Measured over the **one-ply frontier** (every legal successor of the 497 spine-line positions of all 32 packs): **372 frontier positions land on a key carrying a `tablebase_result`, and 43 of them (11.6%) arrive with a clock no record at that key holds** [V] — all transpositions, differing by 4–6 halfmoves (`mate-bishop-knight` node clock 7 vs recorded 3, 13 vs 9, 15 vs 11, 19 vs 15). The rule is right and is kept; the generalization is that **coverage claims about a transposition index must be measured on the frontier, never on the tree the records were written from** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D226 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` is a token filter, and every RFC that treats it as a proposition binder over-claims it — demonstrated twice, by execution against the shipped function.** (1) **Absence is speakable.** Against a packet whose only sentence is `"This pack declares: opening."`, `voiceCheck` returns `valid: true` for *"No reading was recorded at this position."*, *"The engine is silent here."*, *"Nothing was recorded for this spot when the pack was authored."* and *"No measurement exists here; elsewhere on this line there was one."* [V] — none contains a square, a move, a `CHESS_LEXICON` noun, a `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` word or a `PRESCRIPTIVE_VERB`, which is the whole of what the check inspects. An entirely invented recorded-reading sentence passes the same packet for the same reason. So *"packet membership is a speaking licence"* holds only for chess **tokens**; its converse — non-membership as prohibition — is false for all other prose, which is [[D146]] at full strength with an executed counter-example instead of an argument. (2) **The token admission is `String.includes`, not a word-boundary match:** `source.toLowerCase().includes(token)` means a sentence containing `sf-a1x` licenses the square `a1`, while a `\b`-anchored regex over that same sentence finds nothing to object to — so every acceptance criterion that tests *"the sentence does not **match** `SQUARE`/`UCI`/`SAN`"* is testing the wrong direction. Both are pre-existing and orthogonal to any one packet field; both become load-bearing the moment a frozen sentence carries a machine-stamped identity string, or a design leans on `voiceCheck` to make absence unspeakable | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D227 🐞 | **`content/drafts/` holds 53 pack documents and `PackRegistry.loadDefault` loads all 53 in development, six of which are browser test fixtures.** `jsonFiles` excludes only the four `SIDECAR_BASENAMES` suffixes, so `immediate-guard.browser.json`, `line-boundary.browser.json`, `outcome-hold.browser.json`, `outcome-resist.browser.json`, `stated-reasoning.browser.json` and `trajectory-legs.browser.json` are indexed as ordinary packs [V]. Every corpus figure quoted as "of 47 authored packs" — [[D138]], [[D141]], [[D162]] — is silently excluding those six, and **no code performs that exclusion**, so no test can assert the number the prose uses. 21 of 53 documents carry no ledger. Either the fixtures move out of `content/drafts/`, or "47" stops being a denominator | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D228 💡 | **Two capability registers now describe the same instruments and nothing makes them agree.** `a7e700d` shipped `CAPABILITY_DISPOSITIONS` and `assertAdvertisedCapabilityDispositions` (`apps/server/src/capabilities.ts`): 32 rows, a four-valued `reached`/`refused`/`unmeasured`/`impossible`, a `reason` on every row, an `experiment` required on every `unmeasured`, and a coverage gate that throws when a ready engine advertises an option no row dispositions. `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` §5.1 proposes a **second** registry — admitted evidence-record kinds, published as `Capabilities.recordedReadingKinds` — computed independently. They overlap on Stockfish, Syzygy and Explorer and already differ in emphasis: the shipped register says `Explorer / position white / draws / black → reached (corpus panel)` while the RFC refuses rung 4 as having no producer. Both are true of different layers, which is exactly how two registers drift | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D229 🐞 | **`packages/runtime` cannot see `apps/server`, so any evidence-packet field typed off a server constant is unbuildable — and the runtime already exports the type each attempt reaches for.** The runtime's dependencies are `@chess-tabiya/schema` and `chessops`, and nothing else [V]. `EvidencePacket`, `voiceCheck` and every `render*Reading` live there; `ASSESSMENT_CATEGORIES`, `TABLEBASE_CATEGORIES` and `EVIDENCE_KINDS` live in `apps/server`. `evidence-at-runtime` §3.4 typed a packet field as `(typeof ASSESSMENT_CATEGORIES)[number]` with the note *"there is no `AssessmentCategory` type in the repo"* — but `packages/runtime/src/branch-scale.ts` exports `AssessmentCategory = "win" \| "loss" \| "draw" \| "cursed-win" \| "blessed-loss"`, exactly the five determinate values, re-exported from the runtime index [V]. The rule is always **type in the runtime, narrow in the server** (`apps/server/src/tablebase.ts` already imports `transposeKey` from the runtime on line 1), and it should be stated once rather than rediscovered per RFC | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D230 💡 | **[[D194]] has a general shape and no general guard: a type-keyed barrier in front of a payload passthrough.** `publicEvents` allowlists event **types** (`engineFeedbackEvent` matches `evidence.attached` and machine-ref `objective.state_changed`) while `GET /runs/:id/events` forwards whole event objects — so a new **field** on an unlisted type egresses by default, and nobody reviewing the field is looking at the barrier. Pack egress does **not** have the defect and is the contrast worth naming: `projectPackDocument` and `PackSummary` are built field by field, so a new `PackRecord` field is invisible to `GET /packs/:id` and `GET /packs` by construction [V]. The general rule: anything reaching a passthrough must itself be projected by enumeration, or the barrier must key on payload shape rather than on type | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D231 🐞 | **Rung 4's refusal is now a refusal of a *specified* record kind, and no single row says so.** Four rows hold the pieces: [[D126]] ruled explorer W/D/B splits **admissible** as `corpus_observed`; `da77c56` authored **16 such claims across eleven packs**, its own message calling them *"the first position-level rung-4 evidence in the repo"*; [[D150]] measures that all 16 are unbound prose and pass only because those eleven packs have no `.evidence.json`; `rfc/claim-backing.md` §3.7 specifies `explorer_position_census` as the fix. Re-derived at `a7e700d`: **zero `explorer_frequency` records exist anywhere** — 0 in the 32 draft ledgers, 0 in the 36 candidate ledgers, whose 129 records are 52 `opening_identity`, 27 `position_legality`, 26 `puzzle_provenance` and 24 `engine_eval` [V]. So every clause-1 refusal is still correct; what no reader of any one row can tell is that the refusal has become a **sequencing** state with a named owner rather than an absence of demand | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D232 💡 | **Nothing pins that a new evidence-packet construction site arrives with its disclosure gate.** `a452abb` ("fix: gate reasoning review evidence") closed [[D140]] by **adding the missing call**, not by adding a guard: the disclosure gate is four hand-written `requireGuidanceDisclosure(...)` lines in `rest.ts` sitting beside four hand-written `evidencePacket({...})` calls, and the failure mode is a fifth site. The fix's shape is worth keeping — it adds a second, independent `service.guidanceAccess` call rather than widening the role-blind `reasoningReviewAccess` — but a structural test (every `evidencePacket(` preceded by a gate in the same block), or a constructor that takes the access object, costs one test and removes the class | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D257 🐞 | **The corpus figure the whole project quotes — "47 authored packs" — is computed by no code, so no test can ever assert it.** `PackRegistry.loadDefault` walks `content/drafts/` with `jsonFiles`, which excludes only the four `SIDECAR_BASENAMES` suffixes; `*.browser.json` is not one of them, so the registry indexes **53** documents in development, six of them browser fixtures [V]. "47" is an **editorial** exclusion applied in prose by every RFC and ledger row that quotes it ([[D138]], [[D141]], [[D162]]) and performed by nothing in the tree. This is the general form of [[D227]] and it earns its own row because the defect is not the number: **a figure with no producer cannot be pinned by an acceptance criterion**, so it drifts silently and every consumer re-derives it differently. Two cheap fixes: move the fixtures out of `content/drafts/`, or export the exclusion as a predicate the prose and the tests share | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D258 💡 | **`AssessmentCategory` is declared three times, and each RFC picks a different one.** The identical five-member union `"win" \| "loss" \| "draw" \| "cursed-win" \| "blessed-loss"` appears in `packages/schema/src/drill-pack/types.ts`, in `packages/runtime/src/branch-scale.ts` (re-exported from the runtime's `index.ts`), and as the value-level tuple `ASSESSMENT_CATEGORIES` in `apps/server/src/tablebase.ts` [V]. [[D229]] found the second and concluded *"type in the runtime, narrow in the server"*, which is right for `evidence-at-runtime` — but it is a choice among three, and the **schema** copy is the only one both other packages already depend on, so it is the only declaration that could be single. Not consolidated by any in-flight RFC because none has a consumer asking for it; recorded so the next one reads a row instead of discovering three declarations | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D259 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` has exactly one production call site, and `/reasoning-review` is not behind it.** The check runs only inside `renderVoice` (`apps/server/src/guidance.ts`) [V]. `/reasoning-review` calls `voiceProvider.render(packet, voicePersona, prompt, "reasoning")` **directly** and filters the *response* with `reasoningMatchCheck`, so `packet.sentences` — including its rung-3 `human_divergence` sentences — reaches an external provider with **no `voiceCheck` in front of it at all**. [[D140]] fixed the *disclosure* gap on that route at `a452abb`; the *check* gap is a different hole and is still open. It matters wherever an RFC reasons that "the packet is bound by `voiceCheck`": on one of the four packet construction sites it is bound by nothing | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D260 🐞 | **A persona-prompt constraint cannot make a concept unspeakable when the packet names the concept.** The `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review moved absence-honesty onto a criterion reading *"the persona prompt must never mention recorded readings, their absence, or coverage"*, on the reasoning that a renderer never told the concept exists has no reason to word it. The reasoning is sound; the premise fails **inside the same RFC**, because every frozen sentence arm opens *"Recorded reading at this position"* — so the provider is taught the concept by the payload at every node that carries one. **General form: constraining the prompt is worthless whenever the data carries the vocabulary.** Answered in that RFC by §3.8 (the provider never receives a reading at all); recorded here because the reasoning error is reusable and the next design to reach for a prompt constraint should check what its payload says first | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D261 💡 | **`voicePersona` is a deployment option, so every persona-prompt criterion pins a default and nothing more.** `ApplicationOptions.voicePersona` flows through `createRestHandler` and defaults to *"Clear, concise Tabiya voice. Do not add chess claims."* [V]. A test can assert that string; it cannot assert what an operator configures. Any RFC treating a persona constraint as a guarantee is treating a default as an invariant. Either the persona becomes a frozen constant with an assertion at construction, or persona-based constraints are documented as defaults — the second is honest and free, the first is the one that would actually bind | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D262 🐞 | **A `transposeKey` index over the corpus has three different "position counts" and the RFCs have been mixing them.** Measured at `0241a98` over the 32 draft ledgers [V]: **732** admitted records occupy **731** `(pack, key)` index entries — one intra-pack collision, `lucena-bridge-convert` at halfmove clocks 9 and 13 ([[D224]]) — over **568** distinct keys corpus-wide, because **158** anchor FENs recur across packs. The same three-way split runs through the frontier: **11,464** per-pack-distinct successor positions against **7,589** corpus-wide, a 34% difference. No number is wrong; quoting one under the other's label is, and the cross-review's *"731 distinct positions"* did exactly that. **Rule: any corpus figure derived from a per-pack index must say "summed per pack" or "corpus-wide" in the same sentence as the number** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D263 💡 | **Publishing a pack moves its digest, so the corpus is designed to go stale under its own ledgers — and the only runtime consequence today is a CLI warning.** `rfc/pack-graduation.md` §4.5 establishes that setting `reviewStatus: "published"` moves `digestDrillPack(document)`, because `provenance` is inside the digested document; that RFC accepts a 32-of-32 `packDigest` re-stamp as a **landing-order obligation** shared with `claim-backing`, whichever lands second. Meanwhile the repository's only staleness check is `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE` in `sourcing/check.ts` at severity **warning**, in the authoring CLI ([[D223]]). So the drift is **scheduled, recurring and invisible at runtime**. `evidence-at-runtime` §2.1 fences its own consumer with a load-time refusal; nothing fences the others. **Demonstrated live during the author round, in both directions:** the stale set was **5 packs / 181 machine readings** at `0241a98` and **3 packs / 104** at `c55b9cf`, because `caa8afa` (*"feat: wire pack vocabulary reach"*) added a `variantOf` block to two packs and **re-stamped both ledgers in the same commit** [V]. The in-commit re-stamp is the discipline working; the fact that the number moved 43% within hours is why any figure quoted about staleness has a shelf life and why criteria should report it rather than freeze it | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D264 🐞 | **The `CORPUS_GUARD` "one constant, one render site" pattern has already forked, which is what any criterion written against it must catch.** `apps/web/src/lib/corpus-sentences.ts` declares `CORPUS_GUARD` = *"These counts say what this population played, not what is good."*; `apps/server/src/repertoire.ts` declares `REPERTOIRE_CORPUS_GUARD` — the same sentence **minus its trailing period**, in a different package — and interpolates it into two generated strings [V]. Two near-identical population guards already exist, so a test asserting *"one reference to the constant"* is satisfied by a **copy of the string**, which is the failure that actually happened. Any guard-sentence criterion must assert the sentence is not **declared** twice, not that the constant is referenced once | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D265 💡 | **The digest-conjunct pattern generalises: a two-valued grounding verdict is not an admission gate, and every consumer of one needs its own second predicate.** `assessmentGrounding` returns `"ledger_verified" \| "unverified"` and attests **one** record; `evidence-at-runtime` §2.1a adds `ledger.packDigest === digest` for its own consumer and converts an authoring-time **warning** into a load-time **refusal**. The shape is reusable and the reasoning is: a summary verdict computed for one purpose (does the *assessment* have a backing record?) is being read for another (does this *ledger* describe this *document*?). Three properties made the fix cheap and are worth naming as a checklist — both operands were already in scope one line apart in `PackRegistry.fromDocuments`; the cost was measurable before writing any code (5 packs, 181 readings); and the failure direction is silence rather than a false statement. [[D223]] is the defect, this row is the pattern | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D266 🐞 | **Three instruments guarantee tokens while being described as guaranteeing propositions, and the description error is the recurring bug rather than the instruments.** [[D234]] names `voiceCheck` (token membership described as a speaking licence); [[D131]] names `claim-backing`'s residual sweep (numeral policing described as inference policing); the third is the shape they share — **an allow-list over a lexicon cannot bind a claim, and every design leaning on one must argue its own safety separately.** All three were described correctly *once* and cited loosely afterwards, which is how the error propagates: the citation is shorter than the caveat, so the caveat is what gets dropped. Proposed convention, cheap and checkable in review: **an instrument's description must name what it inspects, not what it is used for** — *"`voiceCheck` compares square/UCI/SAN tokens and three word lists against the packet"*, never *"`voiceCheck` binds the sentence to the packet"* | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D267 🐞 | **No instrument in this repo can see a citation, so the one step `rfc/pack-graduation.md` §7 prices as the cheapest on the ladder moves no measured number at all.** Run rather than reasoned, at `c55b9cf` and again after this wave's 22 packs were cited: `checkSourcingFile` reports **32 of 47 clean at draft severity and 4 of 47 at published severity BEFORE**, and **32 / 4 AFTER** [V]. The 4 are `anti-caro-advance`, `opening-principles-black`, `opening-principles-white`, `opponent-intent-early-queen`; the 15 draft failures are all `EVIDENCE_READ_ERROR` + `MANIFEST_READ_ERROR` (no sidecars), reproducing [[D238]]. Every remaining published-severity error in the corpus is `EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED`, which demands an **evidence record of a named kind**, and a bibliographic source produces none. §7's *"10 after a citation pass"* is a hand audit against the **blocker gate**, which is prose — so it can be true while the checker is unmoved, and both statements are now measured | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D268 🐞 | **A retrievable, licence-compatible source cannot enter the sourcing manifest, because `linkage` demands every manifest entry be consumed by an evidence record or abstention and `EVIDENCE_KINDS` has no bibliographic member.** The six kinds are `opening_identity`, `position_legality`, `explorer_frequency`, `tablebase_result`, `engine_eval`, `puzzle_provenance` (`apps/server/src/sourcing/types.ts:57`) [V]; `linkage` raises `MANIFEST_ENTRY_UNUSED` for any entry no record or abstention names (`ledger-validation.ts:383`) [V]; and `evidenceSupports` raises `EVIDENCE_OVERREACH` for any non-template record supporting a `PROSE_POINTERS` target [V]. So the only encodable home for a citation at HEAD is `provenance.sources` (free text) plus `provenance.attribution` (licence-checked only). This is [[D150]]'s exact shape one field over: **the wave produced attribution that no instrument reads.** An abstention was considered and rejected as dishonest — the sources are available; what is missing is a record kind, not a source | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D269 🐞 | **Three evidence sidecars stored a `packDigest` that did not match their pack at HEAD, before this wave touched anything.** `mate-bishop-knight`, `mate-k-q-technique` and `mate-k-r-technique` — 3 of the corpus's 32 sidecars — fail `digestDrillPack` against their stored digest at `c55b9cf` [V], i.e. their evidence was confirmed against a different version of the file and `checkSourcingFile` has been emitting `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE` ("re-confirm evidence") on them unnoticed. It is a **warning**, so nothing fails and nobody looked. This wave did **not** re-stamp them — that would erase a signal it did not create — and instead wrote the finding into each pack's `graduationBlockers` so it survives the next re-stamp. Directly relevant to [[D209]]: `pack-graduation` §4.5 plans to re-stamp all 32 ledgers, which would silently retire these three | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D270 🐞 | **Two of the 22 packs cited this wave now hold a source that argues AGAINST the objective it was fetched to support, and that is the pass's most valuable output.** (1) `berlin-queenless-press` wants *"the king-activity plan for queenless middlegames"*; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin_Defence (CC BY-SA 4.0, read 2026-08-16) calls the position an **endgame**, calls Black's king **"misplaced in the centre"**, and says *"Black can hold the endgame with accurate play"* — the reverse valence, and it names no king-activity plan. The pack's own corpus measurement already said nobody plays it, so **two independent signals now lean against that objective**. (2) `french-advance-chain-white` premises that *holding the base census is the White plan*; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawn_structure §e5-chain gives White's themes as *"Kingside mating attack, f2-f4-f5 break"* and mentions d4 only as what **Black** attacks. Neither pack was edited beyond recording this: a citation that refutes is evidence, and law 6 says it gets escalated | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D271 🐞 | **`carlsbad-minority-attack`'s three-plan taxonomy — the pack's self-declared foundational claim — is two-thirds citable and one-third contradicted in shape by the same source.** en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawn_structure §Carlsbad formation gives *"Themes for White: Minority attack on the queenside. e3-e4 break, sometimes prepared with f2-f3. e5 outpost, sometimes supported with f2-f4"* and *"Themes for Black: e4 outpost, kingside attack"* [V, section wikitext, 2026-08-16]. So plans 1 and 2 are cited; the pack's third White plan, a **kingside attack**, is what the source assigns to **Black** in this structure, and White's third theme there is an e5 outpost. Two routes out and both are authoring, not measurement: re-cut the plan class to the outpost the source names, or find a source that states a White kingside attack in the Carlsbad. The blocker was split rather than retired | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D272 🐞 | **The residue a citation pass structurally cannot clear is *order*, and it is the majority of the middlegame class.** Five of the ten objective-premise blockers reduce to *arrangement before X* — `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze` (arrangement before lever), `kid-mar-del-plata-white` (before the lock), `dragon-yugoslav-race` (before the h-pawn arrives), `iqp-white-panov-attack` (Re1 and Be4 before ...Nce7), `carlsbad-minority-attack` (implicitly) — and **every source consulted lists themes without sequencing them**. Wikipedia's structure articles are theme inventories by construction. So these are not *"no source exists"* and not *"no compatible source exists"*: they are **claims of a kind no encyclopedic source settles**, and per [[D155]] the instrument that would is a game-level corpus that does not exist at HEAD. This is the third wave to arrive at the same missing instrument from a different direction | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D273 🐞 | **One claim in the corpus has a genuinely empty compatible-source shelf, and it is worth distinguishing from the other two failure modes.** `pawn-breakthrough-convert` asserts the three-against-two breakthrough mechanism and that its sacrifice order is not interchangeable. Checked 2026-08-16: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakthrough_(chess) **does not exist (HTTP 404)** [V]; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passed_pawn covers **piece** sacrifices clearing a promotion path, not the pawn breakthrough [V]; en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Chess/The_Endgame/Pawn_Endings covers the opposition and the rule of the square and has no breakthrough section [V]; the MediaWiki search API returns no article for it on either wiki [V]. The only partial hit is en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Chess_Strategy/Queenside_pawn_majority (*"without kings, a pawn breakthrough becomes easier"*), which backs the premise and not the mechanism. The order claim is separately tablebase-grounded; the technique is not, and no CC-BY-SA/CC0 text found states it | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D274 💡 | **Wikipedia's rendered page truncates before the section a citation needs; the MediaWiki `action=parse&section=N` API does not, and that is the method every future citation wave should use.** `en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawn_structure` carries 20 structure sections and a plain fetch was truncated before *Carlsbad formation* — the exact section three packs needed. `action=parse&page=Pawn_structure&prop=sections` lists the indices and `&prop=wikitext&section=18` returns that section alone, in full, in one small request [V, 2026-08-16]. This turned one article into the single highest-yield source of the wave: §8 Maróczy Bind, §13 e5-chain, §15/§16 the two Isolani formations, §18 Carlsbad and §19 Panov each carry an explicit *"Themes for White / Themes for Black"* list, which is exactly the shape a pack's `planClasses` wants. Cheap, retrievable, CC BY-SA 4.0, and section-addressable | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D275 🐞 | **`provenance.attribution` is validated for its licence string and nothing else, so an attribution chain can be complete, well-formed and entirely fictional.** `licenceObligations` (`apps/server/src/sourcing/check.ts:318`) does two things: it demands an attribution row for every **manifest** entry that is CC-BY-SA-4.0 *and* contributes prose through an evidence record, and it refuses any attribution row whose `licence` is not `"CC-BY-SA-4.0"` [V]. Since [[D268]] means a bibliographic source can never be a manifest entry, the first check is vacuous for this wave's 19 sources, and the second only reads one field. No `url` is fetched, no `sourceId` resolved, no `noticeText` checked against anything. The 22 packs this wave cited pass `sourcing-check` identically to how they would with invented URLs — which is why every source here was fetched and quoted in `provenance.sources` by hand, and why that discipline is currently a convention rather than a gate | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D276 🐞 | **Editing a pack with a JSON round-trip destroys the corpus's hand-formatting, and editing a sidecar with one destroys its single-line form — a 22-pack pass produced 2,101 insertions before it produced 431.** `content/drafts/*.json` mixes pretty-printed and hand-compacted arrays (`berlin-queenless-press.json` keeps `movesSan` on two lines and structural expressions inline), and `*.evidence.json` files are single-line; `json.dump(indent=2)` reformats both, turning a one-field edit into a 293-line or a 1,222-line diff [V, measured both ways this wave]. The fix that worked and should be shipped rather than re-invented: **splice only the block you own** — locate `\n  "provenance": {`, brace-match its span, re-render that object alone; and for a digest, replace the digest literal by string substitution. There is no `make` target that edits a pack field, so every content wave writes this by hand | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D204 🐞 | **Four emitters write `string[]` into a field an in-flight RFC types.** `distill.ts:82`, `sourcing/openings.ts:116`, `sourcing/position-seeds.ts:249`, `sourcing/syzygy.ts:187` all emit bare-string `graduationBlockers`, and `validatePackDocument` compiles `drill_pack.schema.json` through Ajv (`pack-validation.ts:86-88`) — so a typed array is enforced against **emitted** documents, not only committed ones. `distill.test.ts:11` asserts `validatePackDocument(...).issues` `toEqual([])` over a `distillRun` output and `syzygy.test.ts:143` asserts a bare-string `toContain`. The general rule: **a format change that migrates content without migrating its emitters lands green and breaks on the next emission** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; fixed in that RFC's §1.6 + criterion 12 |
| D205 🐞 | **A `state: "accepted"` blocker is reachable by assertion — `kind` has no per-kind required field and `ruling` is checked against nothing.** `rfc/pack-graduation.md` §1.2 makes `accepted` the one state that can graduate a pack that should not, then guards it with a single lint on *non-blank `accepted.ruling`*: `permanent_property`'s "stated impossibility" and `out_of_scope`'s "named boundary" require no field `owner_ruling` does not, so all three collapse to one free-text string. The fallback guard — a printed corpus-wide page — has no criterion requiring anyone to read it. The repo can afford better: **21 dated owner rulings are greppable in `design/`**, and the 2026-08-13 no-review-workflow ruling is recorded in five living-tier files. Fix is law 3 applied to a field that exempted itself from it: a `rulingRef` that must resolve, plus a committed page so each new acceptance is a diff | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; fixed in that RFC's §1.2 + criterion 13 |
| D206 🐞 | **Three of the seven packs recorded as clean at published severity fail `sourcing-check` today, at draft severity.** `conversion-up-a-piece`, `rook-4v3-same-side` and `trajectory-qgd-exchange-minority` have no `.evidence.json` and no `.sources.json`, so `checkSourcingFile` raises `EVIDENCE_READ_ERROR` + `MANIFEST_READ_ERROR` at the default severity, which `issue()` defines as `error` (`sourcing/ledger-validation.ts:38`). Measured by **running** the checker over all 47 packs at both review statuses: **4 clean at published, 43 fail, and 15 of 47 fail at draft severity now.** The general rule this cost: **the absence of evidence scores as clean in any derivation that counts unbacked labels instead of running the check** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; corrected in that RFC's §5/§7 and in [[D177]] |
| D207 🐞 | **The corpus's 97 unbacked machine-checkable claims are 66 reported + 31 masked.** 66 sit in packs with a ledger and surface as `EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED` across 28 packs; the other 31 sit in the 12 ledger-less packs that carry machine labels, where `checkSourcingFile` fails on the missing sidecar first and never reaches the label check. 66 + 31 = 97 exactly. The number is right and it is not what the gate says | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review |
| D208 🐞 | **`make verify` never runs `sourcing-check`, and after a pack graduates it would run on the wrong root.** `verify` is `typecheck test schema-check` (`Makefile:20`); `sourcing-check` is a separate target requiring an explicit `DIR=`/`FILE=` (`Makefile:60-63`), and every caller of `checkSourcingDirectory`/`checkSourcingFile` in the repo is an emitter's internal re-check or a unit test over a temp directory — **no test runs it over `content/drafts/` or `content/packs/`**. So the published-severity escalation that `pack-graduation` §5 makes load-bearing is enforced by a command someone must remember to type, aimed at `DIR=content/drafts` — **the one directory a published pack has just left**. Same family as [[D175]]: a maturity step that exits its own gate | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; floor set by that RFC's criterion 14 |
| D209 🐞 | **Two in-flight RFCs each assert a digest-freshness criterion the other breaks.** `pack-graduation` §4.5 moves all 47 pack digests and re-stamps 32 ledgers; `claim-backing` round 2 §3.10/§5.1 moves 35 packs and 29 ledgers over an overlapping set. Neither names the other, and each pins *0 `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE`* as an acceptance criterion. **A criterion that asserts a repo-wide state rather than an end-of-commit state is one two RFCs can both pass and jointly violate** — the fix is to make the re-stamp a landing-order obligation | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; fixed in that RFC's §4.5 + criterion 6 |
| D210 🐞 | **At least ten blocker entries carry an ALLCAPS token whose case reads as a status marker and whose meaning inverts it — and the inversion runs both ways.** `CORPUS-CHECKED ... and NOT ANSWERABLE` is in three packs (`opening-principles-black/white`, `opponent-intent-early-queen`), not one; two `ENGINE-CHECKED ... REFUTED` entries record refutations; `anti-caro-advance`'s `ENGINE-CHECKED` entry **opens** a new defect (*"the spine's model answer is the third-ranked of its own four candidates"*) in the corpus's most graduation-ready pack; four entries use ALLCAPS `UNGROUNDED` to mark debt; and `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze` records a **completed** engine pass in lowercase with no marker at all. Extends [[D174]] from two examples to a measured population, and confirms its rule: **a status recorded in prose cannot be migrated mechanically** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review |
| D211 🐞 | **Sixteen files hardcode a real `content/drafts/<pack>.json` path, five of them `anti-caro-advance`.** `grep -rl "content/drafts/[a-z0-9-]*\.json"` over `apps/` + `packages/` returns 16 files; `refusal-coverage.test.ts` (the only one [[D182]] named) is one. `anti-caro-advance` is the single pack that is clean at published severity **and** carries engine evidence, i.e. the likeliest first graduation — and moving it reddens `authored-feedback.test.ts`, `line-drill.test.ts`, `sourcing/verify-draft.test.ts` and `session-controller.test.ts` as well. The `resolvePackPath(id)` fix was right and scoped to one file | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; fixed in that RFC's §6 + criterion 9 |
| D212 🐞 | **A drafting-evidence rule, found by an RFC whose scripted counts all reproduced and three of whose pack-name citations did not.** `STILL UNTESTED` is in `anti-italian-center-attack-black`, not `kid-mar-del-plata-white`; the *"deliberately is not smuggled in here"* boundary is in `pawn-breakthrough-convert.json:389` about a pack that does not exist, not in a `rook-4v3-same-side` sibling; and the corpus holds **one** `permanent_property` entry, not two. Every number in the same RFC re-derived exactly. The general form: **a figure derived by script and a name recalled from reading are not the same evidence class, and a single `[V]` on both hides which is which** — worth a convention in `design/research/README.md` | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review |
| D237 💡 | **The cheapest moment to build a gate is while the thing it guards does not exist.** `content/packs/` holds only `.gitkeep`, so a strict `checkSourcingDirectory` sweep over it inside `make verify` costs **zero** today and can never be red without someone having graduated a pack that should not have. Added after the first pack lands, the same gate is either redundant or under pressure to be weakened on its first use — by the author who most wants that pack to pass. The general form: **a gate added before its first subject is free and uncontested; the same gate added afterwards is a negotiation** | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round; landed as that RFC's §5.1(i) + criterion 14(a) |
| D238 💡 | **A ratchet is the honest form of a gate you cannot afford to close.** **15 of 47** `content/drafts/` packs fail `sourcing-check` today at *draft* severity [V], and `pack-graduation` §0.3(a) refuses to pay content debt — so it asserts **≤15**, prints the failing set, and names the wave that drives it to `0` as the owner of the strict flip. **A criterion that reddens the build for debt the RFC declines to pay is a criterion that gets waived, and a waived gate reads as an enforced one.** The ratchet number is the destination that *"a later wave"* was standing in for | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round; landed as that RFC's §5.1(ii)–(iii) + criterion 14(b)–(c). Closes with [[D208]] |
| D239 🐞 | **Three of the four `graduationBlockers` emitters validate their own output and throw, so an un-migrated emitter does not emit a bad document — it stops emitting.** `sourcing/openings.ts:120`, `sourcing/position-seeds.ts:252` and `sourcing/syzygy.ts:190` each call `validatePackDocument` on the document they just built and raise `SourcingError("EMITTED_PACK_INVALID")` when it fails; `valid` is `!issues.some(severity === "error")` and schema violations are error-severity (`pack-validation.ts`) [V]. Only `distill.ts` lacks the self-check, and it is the one whose test carries the assertion. Sharpens [[D204]]: the two red tests were the visible half of the break, and the run-time half is larger | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round; fixed in that RFC's §1.6 + criterion 12 |
| D240 🐞 | **A blocker `id` derived from the emitted statement is not stable, because two emitter blockers are templated.** `sourcing/syzygy.ts` interpolates `options.opponent` into two of its three blocker texts, so the same blocker renders differently per caller [V] — a text-derived id moves with the call site, and the migration's ids then disagree with the emitter's. Ids must key on the **template**, in a checked-in registry both the emitter and the migration read. The general form: **an identity derived from rendered output is an identity that moves when the caller changes** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round; fixed in that RFC's §1.6 + §4.0 |
| D241 🐞 | **A citation lint that cannot resolve its own anchor form fails open on every typo.** `pack-graduation` §1.2's worked `rulingRef`, `planning/exploration/log.md#2026-08-13-owner-rulings`, is **not** the slug of that heading — the GitHub-style slug carries the whole title through *"…not-verdicts"* [V]. Fix: narrow the grammar to `path` or `path#L<line>` and nothing else. What makes the line form safe is a rule already on the books: **law 7 makes `planning/exploration/log.md` append-only, so it is the one living-tier file whose line numbers are a stable citation target.** `#L<line>` is permitted only into append-only files | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round; fixed in that RFC's §1.2 |
| D242 💡 | **A citation requirement is a real guard only when the honest case can pay it cheaply and the dishonest case cannot pay it at all — so verify payability before ratifying the lint.** [[D205]]'s `rulingRef` was checked against its own migration before acceptance: the 37 no-review-workflow entries quote **one** ruling in seven wordings, so they take **one** reference (`planning/exploration/log.md#L1231`, whose file contains `2026-08-13`) [V], and `GRADUATION_RULING_UNCITED` checks exactly path existence plus date containment. **A lint that blocks the migration it exists to enable is worse than no lint**, and that is a check the author owes before ratifying any reviewer-proposed requirement | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round |
| D243 🐞 | **A merged corpus-wide `blocking` total has no honest reading, so the instrument must not print one.** ~340 across three roots adds **143** emitter placeholders no wave is scheduled to clear to ~197 authored debts [V]. Segregating a number that still gets printed only slows its misreading down. Strengthens [[D203]]'s reporting fix from *per root as well* to **per root only** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round; fixed in that RFC's §3.3 + criterion 3 |
| D244 💡 | **`content/candidates/` is inside the pack schema and outside graduation, and one `readdir` conflated the two questions.** Its 36 `pack.json` documents migrate because `packages/schema/src/drill-pack.test.ts` validates them under the closed policy [V]; they are **not** graduation subjects, because the gate is a `content/drafts/` → `content/packs/` move. The alternative — excluding them from the closed-schema policy so the schema change stays cheap — is declined on the same RFC's own argument: **buying a cheap schema change by narrowing a corpus sweep is the failure shape *"a gate you graduate out of is not a gate"* names.** The candidate→draft promotion path owns their entries | 💡 open, ruled 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round (§0.3, open question 6) |
| D245 🐞 | **A proxy built to be loose was quoted to the unit and read as a bound.** `pack-graduation` §4.3's residual-clause proxy over the 48 resolution-marked entries returns **45** at HEAD with the nine tokens as written and **47** only with a looser tokenization [V]; the cross-review published 47 as a reproducible floor under a hand-counted 42. Both runs are correct and neither is the answer. The general form: **a proxy that moves by two on a word-boundary choice cannot also be cited to the unit — quoting its exact value repeats the error it was built to expose.** Companion to [[D212]] on the other axis: that row is about names recalled versus numbers scripted; this one is about scripted numbers whose script is a guess | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round |
| D246 🐞 | **Two cross-RFC lane facts moved under an RFC mid-review, in opposite directions, and both had been cited `[V]`.** `engine-leverage` **landed** 0.23 (`18d2832`), so the register's *"implementing 2026-08-16"* is stale; and an uncommitted `vocabulary-wiring` implementation carries `DRILL_PACK_SCHEMA_VERSION = "0.24"` and `$id … :0.24` in the working tree [V]. Neither touches the 0.27 lane. The general form: **an RFC round that measures a moving tree must pin its commit and re-check every cross-RFC fact, because a lane citation is true when written and can be false when read** — and the same round found `sourcing/check.ts` and `pack-validation.ts` had moved since the cross-review's commit, which is why its §5 numbers were re-run rather than re-read | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` author round |
| D233 🐞 | **The selection measurements fixed at the public event projection still cross the wire earlier through direct selection responses.** `/select-move`, `/runs/:id/prediction`, and `/runs/:id/group-reply` return `OpponentSelection` before disclosure; the browser must then echo ordinary/group selections into `/moves`, which is how the same full object becomes durable. Redacting those responses would also delete the RFC's required production record. Closing this honestly needs a server-owned selection/opaque-receipt contract (or an equivalent atomic select-and-commit route), not another projection filter invented during a defect patch | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 while implementing D194; requires an RFC/API lane |
| D183 🐞 | **`projectRun` enforces adjacency invariants over the run event stream, so a new `DrillRunEvent` member is only safe if its emission instant is controlled — and a user gesture's is not.** Three checks constrain what may sit *immediately* next to what: `outcome.reached` must directly follow its `move.committed` on the same node (`events.ts`, *"must immediately follow its move.committed"*), `segment.completed` must directly follow its ending checkpoint (`segmentFromEvent`'s `events[event.seq - 2] !== end` guard), and every opponent `move.committed` must be directly preceded by an authoritative `opponent.move_selected` (`opponentMovesFromEvents`, `replay.ts`). Because `RunStorage.read` rebuilds every run through `readBackReplay`, an event interleaved at the wrong instant does not degrade the run — it makes `projectRun` throw, and **the run becomes permanently unloadable**. Generalizes well past the RFC that found it: *"just add an event type"* is not a cheap move, and any proposal to add one must name **when its event can fire** and prove that instant is controlled. Compounding costs: `appendEvents` re-runs `projectRun` over the whole log on every append, and `RunStorage.save` rewrites the entire `snapshot_json`, so a high-frequency event is also a per-emission full replay | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/board-annotation.md` |
| D184 ✅ | **Move authorship now reaches the granted live-session viewer.** `LiveSessionService.detail` projects the already-specified `deriveMoveAuthorship` result from the durable run and possession journal. The browser renders each committed move with the current granted handle, or honestly labels an identity that no longer resolves as `former member`. Server and real two-learner browser regressions pin White/Black attribution without adding persistence or exposing it outside the grant-scoped session detail. *Original:* `deriveMoveAuthorship` was specified, implemented, exported and tested, but no REST route served it and no component rendered it. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the D184 live-attribution batch |
| D185 ✅ | **Live-session kind has one shared declaration.** `LIVE_SESSION_KINDS` and `LiveSessionKind` now live in the runtime package; the server aliases its validator/DDL source to that constant and the browser API aliases its public type to the same union. Widening only one package no longer compiles cleanly, and an exact vocabulary test pins the three shipped members. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the shared live-session vocabulary fix |
| D186 💡 | **chessops ships the `%csl`/`%cal` codec and the repo uses none of it, in either direction.** `makeComment(Partial<Comment>): string`, `parseComment(string): Comment` and `CommentShape { color: 'green'\|'red'\|'yellow'\|'blue', from, to }` are all in chessops 0.15.1, with `makeComment` partitioning on `to === from` to emit `[%csl …]` for circles and `[%cal …]` for arrows. A repo-wide search for `%csl`, `%cal`, `%eval`, `%clk`, `makeComment`, `parseComment` and `CommentShape` returns **zero** hits outside `node_modules`. `exportPgn` sets `PgnNodeData.comments` never and `nags` never — its only comment output is one `startingComments` entry per non-primary branch — and **both** importers (`parsePgnMainline`, `parseRepertoirePgn`) walk the parse tree reading only `data.san`, discarding every comment, `startingComments` and NAG they pass. Board annotation is one call away in each direction, and so are `%eval` and `%clk` if anything ever wants them. Also latent: chessground 10.1.1's brush set (`green`/`red`/`blue`/`yellow` **plus** `paleBlue`/`paleGreen`/`paleRed`/`paleGrey`/`purple`) is wider than `CommentShapeColor`, so any shape vocabulary that is not closed at the four base brushes is unexportable by construction | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/board-annotation.md` |
| D187 🐞 | **`Chessboard.svelte` is destroyed and recreated on every node change, so no board-local state can survive a ply.** `DrillScreen.svelte` wraps it in `` {#key `${displayedNode.id}:${groupOpen ? groupCandidates.length : -1}`} ``, and the component itself rebuilds its entire chessground `Config` inside an `$effect` that touches every prop and then double-`requestAnimationFrame`s a `redrawAll()` to fix cached DOM bounds. Any feature wanting state *on the board* — drawn marks, a persistent selection, a scratch overlay — must hold it in the parent and pass it down. Recorded because it is invisible from the component's own source, is not a bug (the remount is deliberate and the redraw fixes a real chessground problem), and is the first thing an implementer of the learner-drawn-annotation row will get wrong | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/board-annotation.md` |
| D188 💡 | **`RunService.pgn` is `requireRead`-gated, so a run's PGN is exportable by every grant-holder — and it is the least-audited byte stream the product emits.** Any spectator, any granted teacher, anyone holding a `session_join`-minted grant can `GET /runs/:id/pgn`. Today that is correct and not a defect: the PGN contains moves, branch labels and headers, all of which the same principal can already read through the run projection. **The standing rule the observation produces is the point** — any per-author or per-viewer content added to the PGN must carry an explicit principal filter, because the route's gate will not supply one, `exportPgn`'s signature suggests nothing of the kind, and a leak through an export is invisible to every test that reasons about the run projection. Sibling of the disclosure-surface class that `run_grants`' nine-reader enumeration exists to police, one artifact over. `rfc/board-annotation.md` §3.2 is the first case and closes it by construction (own marks only, with the withheld count stated in a `TabiyaMarks` header rather than silently omitted) | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/board-annotation.md` |
| D213 🐞 | **The run event log's adjacency invariants are held open only by every emitter's synchronous bracketing, and nothing asserts it.** Amends [[D183]], which recorded the invariants correctly and their consequence wrongly. Measured at `a7e700d`: `commitMove` pushes `move.committed` and `outcome.reached` **in one `appendEvents` drafts array** (`runtime.ts:346-351`), `reachCheckpoint` emits its checkpoint then its segment in two back-to-back calls (`:454`, `:461-473`), and `commitMove` deliberately emits any `branch.forked` *before* the opponent selection (`:298-322`) — so all three gaps are empty by construction and **no existing event type is tolerated in any of them**. Two corrections follow. (1) *"The run becomes permanently unloadable"* is wrong for the normal path: `appendEvents` re-runs `projectRun` over the whole log **before** anything persists (`events.ts:377-385`), so a bad-adjacency event throws at the append and never reaches `snapshot_json`; the unloadable state is reachable only from a hand-written or migrated log. (2) The real hazard is subtler and is not written down anywhere: the three gaps stay closed **only because every emitter happens to bracket them inside one synchronous call**, a property no test asserts, no type enforces and any future `await` between two `appendEvents` calls silently breaks. A user-gesture-timed event would make that accidental bracketing load-bearing forever, and its failure mode is a `TypeError` surfacing as a 500 that discards the learner's gesture. Also weakening the read-time framing: `RunStorage` serves a live run from `#snapshots` without replaying, and `listRuns` reads `summary_json` only | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D214 🐞 | **`EvidencePacket.sentences` is the `voiceCheck` allowlist, which makes it a law-8 boundary rather than a projection — and `rest.ts` already splices derived sentences into it at two sites.** `voiceCheck` (`packages/runtime/src/voice.ts:32-33`) builds `source = packet.sentences.join("\n")` and then permits **any** square, UCI, SAN token, chess noun, judgement word or prescriptive verb in the model's output that appears in that string. So whatever reaches `sentences` defines what an LLM is licensed to assert about the position. `rest.ts:1178` and `:1191` already do `Object.freeze({ ...basePacket, sentences: [...basePacket.sentences, suggestTitle(story), ...] })` — the precedent for appending arbitrary derived text at the REST layer is established — and `rest.ts:1168` replaces `sentences` wholesale with comparison narrative. The general rule: **any feature adding learner- or author-controlled text near the packet must be checked against `sentences`, not against `DrillRun`**, because a `DrillRun`-shaped isolation argument cannot see this door. Board annotation is the first case (one line — *"the voice should acknowledge what the learner circled"* — would put learner-chosen squares into the allowlist and let the model say `e5` about a position where nothing validated mentions `e5`), and a byte-identity test over the un-plumbed state cannot detect it | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D215 🐞 | **`Cursor.branchId` is history-dependent, so any key built from it is a function of the visit rather than of the tree.** `rewind` (`packages/runtime/src/runtime.ts:398-400`) selects `isAncestor(run, nodeId, run.activeCursor.nodeId) ? run.activeCursor.branchId : target.branchId` — rewinding to an **ancestor** keeps the current branch id; rewinding anywhere else adopts the target node's. `Node.branchId` is fixed per node and `Cursor.branchId` is not, so the same node in the same tree presents different `(branchId, nodeId)` pairs on different visits: fork from N to B′, then rewind to N, and the cursor reads `B′:N`, not `B:N`, until a rewind lands off the current path. Nothing is wrong with `rewind` — the behaviour is what makes "the fork clears the board" work at the fork node itself, which `Node.branchId` could not deliver — but it means **branch-keyed persistence is conditionally invisible**, and no existing consumer had noticed because nothing else persists against the cursor's branch id. Recorded for every future feature keyed on "the line I am on" | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D216 🐞 | **A `story_read` token passes `requireRead` as the token's *creator*, through a fabricated principal — the scoping is a hand-narrowed projection, not an authorization scope.** `Principal` (`authorization.ts:12-15`) is `{learnerId, handle}` and has **no token variant**, so no anonymous caller can be represented. `RunService.publicStory` (`service.ts:570`) therefore resolves the token hash, loads `learnerById(record.createdBy)`, **builds a principal out of the sharing host**, and calls `this.story(...)` — which passes `requireRead` on the host's own grant. What limits the anonymous reader is the hand-written narrowing that follows (`moments.slice(0, 8)`, five fields per moment), not the authorization layer. `session_join` is the safe sibling: it is `authenticate()`-first and *materializes* a real `run_grants` row on redemption (`storage.ts:1771`), so the joiner is a genuine principal thereafter. The standing rule: **any new route must take its principal from `authenticate()` and never inherit one from a token path**, or an anonymous link holder acts as the host on a surface nobody scoped for them. Sibling hazard on the same page: `RunService.pgn`'s optional-principal overload (`service.ts:1430-1432`) falls back to the auto-created `__legacy` learner, which also passes `requireRead` — unreachable from REST today (`rest.ts:1035`), in-process only | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D217 🐞 | **`exportPackRunPgn` exports a *synthesized* run with regenerated ids, so anything keyed on a run's node or branch ids silently vanishes on the pack export path.** `RunService.pgn` sends every pack session to `exportPackRunPgn` (`service.ts:1451`) — the **default** arm — which does not export the run: it returns `exportPgn(combinedRun(pack, run, branchIds))` (`packages/runtime/src/pack-pgn.ts:205`), and `combinedRun` (`:122-152`) builds a fresh `DrillRun` through `createRun`, splicing the authored spine together with the played paths. Node ids, branch ids and the branch list are all regenerated, and the final `exportPgn` call is passed **no `branchIds`** at all. FEN-derived keys (`transposeKey`) survive because the positions replay identically; id-derived keys cannot match anything. The first `exportPgn(run, branchIds)` at `:204` is a legality validation whose output is discarded, so a feature that plumbs only "the `exportPgn` call" has a 50% chance of plumbing the wrong one. Recorded because the asymmetry is invisible from `exportPgn`'s signature and from `RunService.pgn`'s three arms | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D218 🐞 | **A withheld-count in a filtered artifact is a side channel, and on an ungated export route it is a live one.** Stating *"N of yours shown, M of theirs withheld"* reads as the honest disclosure that `design/05:41` asks for, and it is not: the count is information about the other principal's activity, and a three-arm table distinguishing `none` from `own (0); withheld (M)` is an oracle for whether anybody else is annotating at all. `RunService.pgn` is `requireRead`-gated with **no terminal gate** (`service.ts:1429-1452`) and both seated players of a `match` session hold `run_grants` rows on the same run (minted at `storage.ts:1771-1776`), so polling the export turns the counter into a live meter of the opposing seat's private thinking — D80's seat asymmetry arriving through the least-audited byte stream ([[D188]]) rather than through the relay the design refuses it on. **The invariant `design/05:41` actually requires is that the gap be *visible*, not that it be *measured*** — a constant clause stating the artifact is filtered satisfies it and carries zero bits. Generalizes to every future filtered projection: state the omission, never count it | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D219 🐞 | **A skipped migration number is a one-way door, not a gap waiting to be filled.** The migration loop is `for (const migration of migrations) { if (migration.version <= version) continue; … }` (`apps/server/src/storage.ts:2197-2199`). The forward direction is fine — applying 23 to a database at 21 runs cleanly and sets `user_version = 23` — and it is the half that gets checked. The reverse direction is fatal and is the half that does not: **a database that has reached 23 will skip a later-landing 22 forever**, silently, with no error at open (`#migrate` throws only on `version > STORAGE_VERSION`, the opposite direction). So a draft that claims a number past an unlanded sibling's does not defer the sibling — it strands the sibling's tables on every existing database while fresh ones get them, which is worse than a collision because a collision is loud. The register's own practice already refuses holes and every one of its 22 rows is contiguous: migration 16 was *"rebased from an initial 15 claim"* and 21/22 were **reassigned to each other on 2026-08-16** at a cost its own row calls *"nothing but text"*. **Rule: a draft claims the next contiguous number at implementation time and reassigns a blocked sibling upward; it never leaves a hole.** Cheap guard: assert the migration array's versions are exactly `1..STORAGE_VERSION` | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review · **rule superseded 2026-08-16 by `rfc/README.md`'s landing-order rule and [[D250]]: a draft claims a *position*, not a number, and reassigns nobody** |
| D247 🐞 | **`combinedRun` preserves exactly one identity across the pack-export boundary — the move path — so any key that must survive it is keyed on the UCI sequence, never on ids.** The remedy half of [[D217]], measured at `7650d41`. `exportPackRunPgn` ends `exportPgn(combinedRun(pack, run, branchIds))`; `combinedRun` reduces the authored spine and the played branches to `CombinedPath.moves` (bare UCI arrays), dedupes them by their joined UCI string in `uniquePaths`, and replays them through `createRun` + `commitMove`, forking at `sharedPrefix` — which itself locates a node by walking children whose `moveUci` matches. Node ids, branch ids, seqs and cursors are all regenerated; **the root-relative UCI path is preserved exactly and is unique within a tree**, so it is the only usable address. Two consequences nothing else records: `uniquePaths` collapses two branches with identical move sequences into one, so a remap is many-to-one and must dedupe; and the combined tree contains **authored nodes the run never reached**, so anything keyed on `transposeKey` can surface at a position the run never visited. First applied by `rfc/board-annotation.md` §3.1 | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D248 🐞 | **A guard test that runs below the layer its barrier is breached at cannot fail, and a guard never shown to go red is not evidence.** Both halves are demonstrated inside one RFC. (1) `board-annotation`'s criterion 2 asserts byte-identical `evidencePacket` output with and without marks — but the hazard it is cited against is a **`rest.ts` sentence splice**, which sits *above* `evidencePacket` and outside everything the criterion calls, so the test is sound and structurally blind. (2) Its proposed replacement grepped six pinned line numbers, four of which name assembly sites and two splice sites, while the natural violation is a **seventh** splice line the enumeration cannot see — and the numbers had drifted within a day. **Rule: a barrier criterion runs at the layer the barrier can be breached at, is matched by pattern rather than by line, and carries a positive control proving the assertion can report a planted violation.** Cheap, and it is the difference between a guard and a decoration | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D249 🐞 | **`reachCheckpoint` emits its checkpoint/segment pair through two separate `appendEvents` calls, so one of the three adjacency gaps is held open by nothing but the absence of an `await`.** Sharpens [[D213]] with the instance that proves it. `commitMove` does push `move.committed` and `outcome.reached` into **one** drafts array (`packages/runtime/src/runtime.ts`), which is why that gap is unconstructible — but `reachCheckpoint` calls `appendEvents(run, [checkpointDraft])`, inspects the projected result, and then calls `appendEvents(next, [segmentDraft])` against it, with straight-line synchronous code and no lock, queue or `await` between. `segmentFromEvent` compares raw adjacent array slots, so any event landing in that window throws at the append. **The invariant is enforced; its enforcement is kept off legitimate writes by a property of the source text that no test reads.** Cheap guard: assert that no function in `packages/runtime/src` calling `appendEvents` more than once is `async` or contains an `await` between the calls | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D250 🐞 | **A draft that writes a shared monotonic integer into its body has written a claim with an expiry date.** Generalizes [[D219]] past migrations to every version constant, and the evidence is one RFC's own history: `board-annotation` was drafted against `STORAGE_VERSION` 20 / pack `0.22`, cross-reviewed against 21 / `0.23`, and by its author round the pack constant already read **`0.24`** in the working tree with `0.25` and `0.26` claimed ahead of any date it could land. Each move made a previously-true sentence false, and each correction cost a review round. `rfc/README.md`'s landing-order rule fixes the migration case — claim a *position*, resolve `STORAGE_VERSION + 1` at landing — and the same discipline generalizes: **state what a document does not move, never what a number currently is.** *"Stays at 0.23"* expires; *"changes no pack document"* does not | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D251 💡 | **Ids in this runtime are path-shaped — `` `${run.id}:node:${n}` `` and `` `${run.id}:branch:${n}` `` (`createRun`, `commitMove`) — so any key built by concatenating two of them embeds the run id twice.** Measured while pricing `board-annotation`'s mark bound: a `` `${branchId}:${nodeId}` `` composite is **98 characters** against a four-field `transposeKey`'s 52, making a branch-scoped record **202 bytes** of JSON against a FEN-keyed one's 158 — the reverse of the assumption that an id key is cheaper than a position key. At 1,000 records that is 198.2 KiB against 155.3 KiB [V]. Nothing is broken; it prices every future composite key, and it means *"key it by id"* is the expensive option in this codebase, not the cheap one | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D252 🐞 | **`kind === "match"` and `boardControl === "match"` are not interchangeable, and the divergent case ships.** `live-session.ts` refuses a `match` board control on any other session kind, so `boardControl === "match"` implies `kind === "match"` — but **not** the reverse: an **imported-Arena** session is `kind: "match"` on a non-`match` board, which is exactly the combination `storage.ts` detects when it seeds `arena_legs`. Eight sites read `kind === "match"` for behaviour (four server, four web) and seven read `boardControl === "match"`, and the two sets are not aliases of each other. Any future rule phrased *"not during a contest"* must test **`kind`**: a `boardControl` test silently exempts every imported-Arena game, which is a whole session type of real contests. First bites `rfc/board-annotation.md` §4.2's relay refusal | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D234 🐞 | **"Packet membership is a speaking licence" is true of chess TOKENS only, and claude told the owner otherwise.** `voiceCheck` (`packages/runtime/src/voice.ts`) compares exactly three things against the packet: square/UCI/SAN tokens, and membership in three frozen word lists — `CHESS_LEXICON` (18 words), `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` (19), `PRESCRIPTIVE_VERBS` (24). **Prose drawn from none of those vocabularies is unconstrained.** The `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review *executed* it: against a packet whose only sentence is *"This pack declares: opening."*, `voiceCheck` returns `valid: true` for *"No reading was recorded at this position."*, *"The engine is silent here."*, and *"No measurement exists here; elsewhere on this line there was one."* — the last being the exact cross-node inference that RFC exists to forbid. A wholly invented recorded-reading sentence passes too. **Claude advised the owner (D116) that widening the packet is "law-8-legal by construction because `voiceCheck` still binds every sentence to the packet".** That is overstated: it binds the chess vocabulary, not the proposition. The advice's *direction* survives — widening the packet is still strictly safer than widening the model's licence — but it is not a construction-proof, and any RFC that leans on it needs its own constraint. **This is the SECOND instrument tonight found to guarantee tokens while being described as guaranteeing propositions**; the other is `claim-backing`-s residual sweep (D131). Same failure of description, two independent mechanisms, both claude-s | 💡 open, correction 2026-08-16 |
| D235 ✅ | **Undisclosed selection candidates are projected by allow-list.** `publicSelectionEvent` now constructs the five permitted structural/policy fields explicitly; score, WDL, and any future measurement-shaped field are absent unless disclosure opens. A regression injects a future field and proves it stays private while the opponent move remains playable. Maia `mass` remains deliberately public pending its separately ledgered permission decision. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the projection allow-list fix |
| D236 💡 | **Maia candidate WDL sums to exactly 1000 — the encoding is per-mille, measured rather than assumed.** `engine-leverage` criterion 21a existed to *report* observed sums so a later RFC could pin the encoding without guessing, and the real-Maia integration run at `b65bd4e` reports them **consistently 1000** across three passing tests. That is the criterion doing precisely its job: it produced a fact nobody had, at the cost of one assertion. **Whoever specifies a WDL consumer may now write the divisor as a measured constant with a citation**, instead of inferring it from one sample or hard-coding 1000 and hoping. Recorded here because the number lives only in a test report otherwise, and the next author will not re-run a Maia container to rediscover it | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D327 💡 | **Variants in Just Play, not only in packs — owner idea 2026-08-16. The instrument stack degrades in TIERS, and that is the useful frame.** *"what if I want in just play all these variations as well? if we're going to add packs with ie fischer random or all these other variations."* Grounded first pass, to be extended by whoever takes it: **13 files in `packages/runtime/src` import chessops**, and the single coupling point is `positionFromFen` → `Chess.fromSetup(parseFen(fen).unwrap())`. A `variant` column **already exists** in storage (`storage.ts`). `pgn-import.ts` explicitly refuses anything but `Standard`/`From Position`. **Tier 1 — Chess960**: FEN-expressible (X-FEN/Shredder), same pieces and rules, so structural reading, phase, endgame reading and **tablebase all stay valid**; the explorer carries 960 data; **Maia does not** — it is trained on standard chess, so rung 3 goes dark. Note the connection to **D193**: `UCI_Chess960` is one of the eight advertised Stockfish options with no disposition row, so the engine half is *already* partly surfaced. **Tier 2 — rule-changing variants** (atomic, three-check, crazyhouse, racing kings): each detector-s assumptions break differently and per-variant, so this is not one decision. **Tier 3 — see [[D328]].** The product already owns the vocabulary for this shape: `design/06` §2a-s *difficulty-availability* label (measured / authored / neither) is the same idea one axis over — **a variant should declare which rungs survive it**, rather than the product pretending the ladder is intact | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D328 💡 | **Westernised Xiangqi and Shogi — owner idea 2026-08-16, explicitly accepting degraded support.** *"what about a (westernized) xiangqi and shogi? those would be nice — even if we cannot offer most of the support mechanics — it might be nice to play against bot/human these 'foreign chess' variants."* The honest cost, stated so nobody discovers it late: **nothing in the detector stack survives** — different board geometry (9×10 xiangqi, 9×9 shogi), different pieces, different rules; chessops cannot represent either; no tablebase, no Maia, no explorer, no structural reading, no phase classification. **The question is therefore not "which detectors degrade" but "how coupled is the SHELL".** The branch runtime — rewind, fork, compare, checkpoints, the event log — is the actual differentiator and is in principle game-agnostic, but `Node.fen` and `transposeKey` are FEN-shaped today. **Both games have FEN-like notations** (SFEN for shogi; xiangqi FEN is a standard extension), so the coupling may sit at `positionFromFen` rather than in the runtime-s own types — **that is the measurement that decides whether this is a weekend or a second product**, and it is cheap to take. The owner has already accepted the degraded-support trade, so the open question is engineering shape, not desirability | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D329 💡 | **Packs are typed on TWO axes, not one — and a famous-game pack is expressible as prose but not as data.** Owner question 2026-08-16: *"are they all opening vs midgame vs endgame? cause what if we want packs based off of famous previous games?"* Measured: a pack declares **`phase`** (`opening | middlegame | endgame | cross_phase`) **and `mode`** (`line | plan | outcome | trajectory`), so *what kind of rehearsal* is already a separate axis from *what part of the game*. **What is missing is a third: PROVENANCE OF THE POSITION.** `$defs/provenance` requires only `reviewStatus` and offers `sources` as a bare `string[]`; there is **no `sourceGame`**, so a pack can say *"from Kasparov–Topalov, Wijk aan Zee 1999"* in free text and **nothing can read it** — no player, event, date, or move number is machine-addressable. Consequences: no surface can index or filter by game, no *"more from this game"*, no *"you have now seen three positions from this match"*, and the campaign cannot use a famous game as a run-level theme. **This is the cheapest of the three rows here** — the format already carries `variantOf` for pack-to-pack relations, so the pattern exists. Note it also gives authored prose unusually strong provenance: annotated master games are the oldest chess pedagogy there is, and a cited game is a rung-5 claim whose source is checkable in a way *"authored consensus"* never is | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D330 💡 | **There is no time control in this product at all, and `clockState` is a passthrough nothing reads.** Owner idea 2026-08-16: *"what if we want to simulate the time pressure of a GREAT move during 10+0 chess and then give actual time?"* Measured: `clockState` is typed `Record<string, unknown>` on both `DrillRun` and the start options, is set from `rest.ts`, is persisted — and **has zero readers**. No clock, no increment, no per-move budget, no display, no expiry. Separately and importantly, **`clock_zeroed` is the HALFMOVE clock** — a fifty-move-rule counter — and **`timingWindows` is MOVE-COUNT tempo, not clock tempo**; both names invite the conflation and neither is a clock. So the owner-s proposal has **no mechanism whatsoever** today, and the honest first question is not *how do we build a clock* but *what is the smallest thing that produces the felt pressure* — a countdown that ends the encounter, or a soft budget that changes what assistance can be consumed. See [[D331]], which is why this is worth more than it looks | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D331 💡 | **Time is a difficulty axis that constrains the learner WITHOUT touching the power curve — and it may be the missing lever.** `design/06` §5 rules that **what escalates is LEGIBILITY, not power**, and that the power curve is flat by construction. Every difficulty lever found so far is therefore either opponent band (measured, `[1000,2400]`), decidability tier, encounter horizon, or capability suppression. **A clock is a fifth, and it is different in kind: it constrains the learner-s own computation rather than adding or removing anything the product gives them.** It also interlocks with the coaching-versus-cheating criterion landed 2026-08-16 (*distance from the answer*): **a hint you do not have time to read is not a hint**, so time pressure degrades assistance value continuously without any of it being withheld — which is exactly the *"balance cheating to win VS coaching to win"* problem the owner posed, solved by a mechanism that touches neither honesty policy nor inventory. It is also the first candidate that makes *"you-d need proper 2000 Elo skills"* a claim about the **learner** rather than about the encounter-s configuration, which the coaching dossier established the product otherwise cannot make. **Speculative and unmeasured — recorded as the reason D330 is worth more than a missing field** **REFUTED 2026-08-16, and in the direction that matters — this row-s central claim was claude-s and it was wrong.** The interlock hypothesis was *"a hint you do not have time to read is not a hint"*, so time would degrade assistance value continuously and preferentially suppress items nearest the answer. Measured over **43,272 rendered items** from shipped renderers: distance class explains **η² = 0.201** of the variance in reading length, and **0.038** once the refused `move` class is excluded — while **which renderer printed it explains 0.984**. ρ(distance rank, words) is **−0.046**, the order of the ρ = −0.143 this repo already treats as no relationship. **And the sign is inverted at the end that matters: `move` is the CHEAPEST class the product could ever print** — median **1 word / 0.3 s** against `ranking`-s **63 / 15.9 s**. So a clock is a gradient *toward* the answer: under time pressure a learner reaches for the move precisely because it reads fastest, which makes time a **cheating amplifier** rather than the balance mechanism this row proposed. It would also make difficulty a function of copy length, incentivising the compression of evidence into verdicts. **What survives is the by-product, and it is worth more than the hypothesis was:** at a 10+0 budget of 15.0 s/move ≈ 60 words, **≈6.6 `fact` items or 0.94 `ranking` items fit** — the slot number D78 licensed and never supplied, arrived at independently and **with no clock existing**. `design/research/time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D332 ✅ | **Evidence recovery raced an older evidence page against an explicit analysis enqueue.** Coalescing overlapping timer ticks was valid hardening but not the root cause: the next full gate reproduced the exact two-leaf timeout. Its retained trace showed an evidence GET already in flight when `/analysis` enqueued the missing leaves; applying the older page then recomputed `pendingEvidence` from the run log, overwrote the explicit enqueue count, and canceled polling before the new results were read. Analysis enqueue and evidence-page application now serialize in both directions, with a deterministic regression for this exact ordering. The exact browser scenario passed 10/10 isolated zero-retry repetitions and the following full suite. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by serializing analysis enqueue with evidence-page application |
