| D345 🐞 | **The `plyHorizon` act ramp is a restatement of authored spine length, and no pack has ever used the field that would make it anything else.** Measured at HEAD over the 47 packs in `content/drafts`: `plyHorizon` equals the **deepest authored spine ply exactly** in **19 of 20** opening packs and **10 of 11** middlegame packs `[V]`. Endgame packs are the only family with real slack (12 of 14 positive, median +7). So `design/06` §5's *"median 12 ply"* and the 11 / 8 / 24 per-act medians describe how much spine an author typed, not how long an encounter is licensed to run. And **`authoredBoundary.fenPredicates` has 0 uses across all 47 packs** — the one mechanism that can grant authored territory off the move list, and the one `design/04` §0a names as transferable. Consequence for the brief that raised this: *"should Act II's horizon be longer"* has no answer in the number, because raising the cap alone grants nothing | 🐞 open, measured 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |

This deliberately does not impose the same rule on trajectory roots, whose leg boundaries make deeper spines a different contract. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the exemplar boundary correction |

Regressions cover checkpoint/boundary validation and the corpus census. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the vocabulary-wiring follow-up |
| D348 🐞 | **No expression leaf names a shape entry's TRIGGER, so every structure-keyed predicate in a pack is a hand copy of the library.** `plan_signature` resolves a **plan class**; the only trigger-shaped leaf is `named_structure`, whose id vocabulary is closed at `carlsbad`, `iqp-white`, `iqp-black` and `maroczy-bind`. **21 of the 25 shipped shape entries cannot be referred to by name in any expression** — including all three this wave used (`london-wedge`, `open-centre`, `closed-centre-chain`). A pack that wants *"authored while this structure is still on the board"* must duplicate the entry's trigger into its own file, and nothing checks the copy against the entry, so the library and its customer can silently disagree. This is precisely the re-authoring `design/04` §0a warns about, arriving through a field rather than through prose. Middlegame tabiyas in this corpus start at ply 13–21 from the game start, so 6–8 more plies lands at 19–29 — the same place `design/06` §2a's inherited *"human outcome data dies at ply ~20"* lands. **The two numbers are the same number**, and together they answer [[D345]]'s question: a longer Act II cannot be bought with more corpus-backed spine, only with territory that is not spine | 🐞 open, measured 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D350 💡 | **The one timing measurement the explorer CAN produce is availability, not outcome — and it is worth naming as a technique.** In `open-centre-french-exchange-black`, `...Re8` — the objective's own move — occurs **339 of 2695** at the root and **48 of 822** six plies later on the same pack's other branch, i.e. above the 100-game floor at one depth and below it at the other. It is not a substitute for [[D155]]'s per-game traversal and does not pretend to be; it is the shape a timing claim can take **today**, and this is the sixth wave to arrive at that missing instrument from a new direction | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D351 🐞 | **A pack's most concrete-looking claim was wrong in both directions until the evaluator was run — attack counts in prose must be computed, and no target computes them.** The first draft of `closed-centre-chain-black-base-strike` asserted that d4 is attacked **three** times (c5 pawn, c6 knight, b6 queen) and defended **two** (c3 pawn, f3 knight). Sibling of [[D110]], [[D161]] and [[D119]] — a plausible cardinal that no instrument had been pointed at — and the remedy is the same shape as D119's: **a count is a property of what the evaluator returns and may only be asserted after running it**. These are the **first three uses of `fenPredicates` in the corpus** ([[D345]]). The argument is that a shape-library entry is authored guidance valid wherever its trigger fires, so authored territory keyed to that trigger is honest in a way that territory keyed to how far the author typed is not — and it is the only way to lengthen Act II that [[D349]] does not refute. | D354 💡 | **The 1400–1799 opponent band is now 5 packs instead of 2, and the step function survives — the rest is a re-band, not more authoring.** Re-measured at HEAD with the same script shape that found the hole: `targetElo` **< 1400: 6 · 1400–1799: 5 · ≥ 1800: 37 · none: 2**, over 50 packs. The three added are all middlegame — `design/06` §5's **authored** act, the thinnest by decidability — all three put the learner in the **received** structure, the one the opponent chose, which applies `design/04` §2c's anti-opening whitespace to Act II for the first time, and all three are the **first customer for a shape entry's unserved chair**: `london-wedge`'s five Black plans, `open-centre`'s two White plans and `closed-centre-chain`'s two Black plans had **zero middlegame customers between them** before this wave. | D355 💡 | **A game clock is NOT the refused pursuit clock — the discriminator is whether the budget survives a rewind, and it is one field's altitude.** `design/06` §5 refuses *"a pursuit clock is a retry price by another name"*, and §2c's own decomposition says only **how often** you may retry conflicts with `00` §76's *"experimentation without cost"*. Tested: a **run-pooled** clock makes the *k*-th retry cost more than the first — it is a rewind budget with a real-valued counter and the refusal is exactly right. An **attempt-scoped** clock that resets at the fork prices *deliberation inside one attempt* and touches none of §2c's three separable ideas. **The shipped placeholder already picked the safe altitude** `[V]`: `clockState` is declared on **`Node`** (`packages/runtime/src/types.ts:111`, alongside `branchId` at `:107`; mirrored at `schemas/drill_run.schema.json:265`) and written only from `CommitMoveOptions.clockState` (`runtime.ts:57`, applied `:341`) — nodes are path- and branch-scoped, so a clock built on the reserved field **rewinds with the board automatically**, and the refused version needs a **new run-level field**. That makes the ruling checkable in a diff rather than in prose. Two counter-arguments given in the dossier: a resetting clock has no teeth unless flag-fall rides `06` §5's *submitted-branch* verb (it can, and needs no new refusal class); and `06` §5's power/legibility dichotomy has **no slot** for a device that shrinks the learner's own capacity. `design/research/time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §2 | 💡 open, answered 2026-08-16, owner ruling pending — see [[D364]] |
| D356 💡 | **Reading cost does NOT track distance to the answer — the interlock hypothesis is refuted, and the number is not close.** D331 proposed that time pressure would degrade assistance *preferentially near the answer*, solving coaching-vs-cheating without touching honesty policy or inventory. Measured over **43,272 rendered items** from the shipped renderers (47 packs / 721 transitions / 609 positions, 25 shape entries, 43 real cached explorer responses, 105 recorded Maia candidate counts) `[V]`: variance in log(words) explained by **distance class** is **η² = 0.201** over four classes and **0.038** over the three the product may ship, while variance explained by **which renderer printed it** is **η² = 0.984**. Spearman ρ(distance rank, words) = **−0.046** over all items, **+0.125** excluding `move` — the same order as the ρ = −0.143 this repo already treats as *no relationship*. **Reading cost is a property of renderer verbosity, not of distance from the answer.** The within-class spread swamps the between-class difference: `kind` runs **6 words** (phase reading) to **298** (full shape panel) — a **50×** range; `ranking` runs **12** (the `human_divergence` marker) to **230** (the corpus page) — **19×**. So a clock would make an encounter's difficulty a function of **copy length**, and its standing incentive would be to compress evidence into verdicts — a move *down* the distance axis, i.e. toward the eval-bar trainer `00-thesis.md` exists to replace. The one point for the other side, stated: excluding `move`, the class medians do order correctly (6 < 9 < 63) — but η² = 0.038, and the effect is carried almost entirely by **one family**, the corpus page | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16, `time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §3, `tools/d355-reading-cost-harness/` |
| D357 💡 | **The answer is the cheapest thing this product could ever print, so a clock is a gradient TOWARD cheating.** Measured per-item medians `[V]`: `move` **1 word / 0.3 s**, `kind` **6 / 1.5 s**, `fact` **9 / 2.3 s**, `ranking` **63 / 15.9 s**. Inside one 10+0 move budget (15 s) a learner can read the answer **50 times over**, or **0.94** of one human-model split. Under time pressure the rational learner reads the shortest item that resolves the most of the live decision — which is precisely the item `coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` calls cheating. **This interlocks directly with that dossier's open owner question:** under its option (a) `move` stays refused and a clock weakly favours the honest end (weakly — η² = 0.038); under option (c) `move` becomes purchasable pre-commit and **a clock becomes a cheating amplifier**. So the clock ruling should not be taken before the hint-ladder ruling | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16, blocks on the D317–D326 owner question |
| D358 💡 | **The 10+0 arithmetic: the shipped all-on reading costs 16.4× a whole move budget, and it derives the slot number D78 could not.** 600 s over a **40-move reference game** — *Lichess's own* classifier convention (`initial + 40 × increment`), so the denominator is the platform's, not ours — is **15.0 s per learner move**, which at **238 wpm** (Brysbaert 2019 meta-analysis, 190 studies, adult silent non-fiction reading) is **60 readable words** `[V]`. Against that: the all-on rung-0 structural reading at **one** node is a median **978 words / 247 s** = **16.4×** the entire per-move budget (p95 1,273 / 321 s); reading it at **two** of a median encounter's five nodes costs **494 s — 82% of the whole 600-second game**; even handing the **whole 600 s to one median-horizon encounter** (120 s/move) leaves it over budget at a single node. Per item against a 15 s budget: corpus page **3.9×**, full shape panel **5.0×**, human split **1.06×**, guided shape block 0.81×, `bestmove` **0.02×**. **The durable result is a derived number:** the readable loadout is **≈6.6 `fact` items or 0.94 `ranking` items**, and the campaign's chosen five-slot loadout costs **45 words / 11.3 s** — so D78's measured readability floor and a 10+0 move budget land on the same order (5–7 items) by independent routes. **The clock is not needed to collect this**; a slot budget already implements it. Fairness note the numbers require: the shipped default is `SILENT_ASSISTANCE` and costs **0 words**, so these figures describe the opted-in states, which is exactly where the campaign's loadout lives | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16, `time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §4 |

The distribution is **bimodal by phase** and the median sits in its gap — observation-count deciles **27 · 31 · 33 · 37 · 78 · 85 · 87 · 88 · 92**; words per position by declared phase: **endgame 256 (65 s)**, cross-phase 985 (248 s), **opening 1,188 (299 s)**, **middlegame 1,199 (302 s)**. Fix is cheap and mechanical: wherever *"median 58 observations per position"* is cited (`06` §2c, `06` §5, the campaign dossiers, D78 itself), cite the per-phase figures, and quote the all-on state's real headline — **978 words, 247 seconds, one node** | 🐞 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D360 🐞 | **`clock_zeroed` renders a shipped sentence the detector never emits — the only clock-named renderable string in the product is dead code.** `move_irreversibility` declares the subkind and `apps/web/src/lib/transition-sentences.ts:9` renders *"The move was a pawn move or capture, so the halfmove clock resets."* — but `irreversibility()` (`packages/runtime/src/transition.ts:245-265`) returns only `castled`, `last_of_role` and `pawn_break`, and **never** `clock_zeroed`. Measured over **721 spine transitions**: `pawn_break` 52, `castled` 20, `last_of_role` 21, **`clock_zeroed` 0** `[V]`. Either delete the subkind and its sentence, or emit it — but it should not sit in a type, a renderer and a schema while being unreachable, **especially with a name that invites exactly the conflation D330 warns about** (it is the fifty-move-rule counter, not a clock) | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D361 🐞 | **`clockState` accepts and persists literally anything, and nothing reads it.** Exactly **six** non-test references in `apps/` and `packages/`, all declarations or opaque passthrough — `packages/runtime/src/types.ts:111`, `runtime.ts:57` and `:341`, `apps/web/src/lib/api.ts:468`, `apps/server/src/rest.ts:471-477` and `:1314-1316` — **no `.svelte` file ever sends it** and **no logic consumes it** `[V]`. Its schema definition is `{"description": "Reserved until clock semantics are specified by a later RFC.", "type": "object", "additionalProperties": true}` (`schemas/drill_run.schema.json:206-210`), so the run log will today accept an arbitrary untyped object per node from any client and persist it inside *"the sole source of chess truth"* (`05` §1). Whichever way [[D364]] is ruled, this should not stay open-ended: delete the field, or give it a closed shape in the same commit that gives it a reader | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D362 💡 | **The owner's sentence literally asks for a DEPICTED clock, and it is one retained field away.** *"simulate the time pressure of a GREAT move during 10+0 chess **and then give actual time**"* reads most naturally as: state the recorded time the original decision was made under, and let the learner take as long as they like. That version touches **no invariant, no honesty gate, no inventory and no refused-list entry**, prices nothing, and is a recorded fact so law 8 is clean. **But `05` §1's *"absence is stated, never simulated"* makes grounding a hard constraint, and it currently bites** `[V]`: `parsePgnMainline` keeps **headers whole** (`Object.fromEntries(game.headers)`, `apps/server/src/pgn-import.ts:63`) so `TimeControl` already survives into `ImportedGameRecord.headers`, but each move is reduced to `{san, uci}` at `:54`, **dropping the per-move `[%clk]` comments Lichess emits on every move** — which is exactly the number the owner's sentence is about. So: the game's declared control is importable today; the clock *at the moment of the move* is one retained field plus a renderer. Scope the abstention path first (see the dossier's F6) — if a material share of imports carry no clocks, this is an abstention, not a feature | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D363 💡 | **"A GREAT move" is not expressible, and the nearest honest object is already shipped.** Nothing in this product grades a move (`coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` §3; law 8). The closest shipped selector is `storyMoments`, which fires on a **≥150 cp recorded swing** (`STORY_PIVOT_CP`, `packages/runtime/src/story.ts:30`, applied `:86`) and renders *"The recorded evaluation moved +X cp across this move"* — a **change**, not a verdict `[V]`. So the honest encounter D362 would depict is *"the move at which the recorded evaluation moved 150 cp, played with 24 seconds left"*: **two recorded facts, no chess claim** — and on this evidence a considerably more interesting object than a countdown. Worth its own row because it is reusable well beyond the clock: it is the general shape for *"replay a famous decision"* content without inventing chess truth, and it connects to [[D329]]'s famous-game pack question | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D364 💡 | **`DESIGN-GAP:` `06` §5's refused list is one word too broad and its escalation dichotomy is one axis too narrow — both are owner-tier amendments, proposed not written.** (1) *"a pursuit clock is a retry price by another name"* is true of the **run-pooled** clock and false of the **attempt-scoped** one, and the shipped `clockState` field cannot express the refused form ([[D355]]). (2) *"what escalates is LEGIBILITY, not power"* is a complete dichotomy only while nothing constrains the learner's own computation; a clock is a **third kind** — it changes neither what the opponent can do nor what the product will say — and `06` §5 has no verdict on it either way. **The owner question the dossier ends on: is time nothing, a decoration, or a rule?** (a) **nothing** — close the cluster and delete the reserved field, banking the derived ≈6-item loadout as a slot number; (b) **a decoration** — the depicted clock of [[D362]], recommended and cheapest; (c) **a rule** — the enforced attempt-scoped clock, admissible but amending §5 twice, and **a run-pooled budget must be refused in the same ruling** or the design drifts back into the retry price by accident, because the difference between the permitted and the refused object is one field's altitude and nothing today would catch it | 💡 open, owner ruling requested 2026-08-16, `design/research/time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §8 |
| D297 📊 | **The three devices that produce fun WITHOUT a power curve — and we own runnable versions of all three, six of them already shipped with no consumer.** 2026-08-16, `design/research/fun-mechanics-outside-roguelikes.md`, the wide sweep past the roguelike frame (15 genres). Outside the roguelike set — where *every* game has a power curve — fun-without-power is produced by exactly three devices: **(1) score the SOLUTION, not the outcome** (Zachtronics' three-axis histogram; Barth: *"the only thing a global leaderboard manages to tell you is that you suck"*); **(2) make KNOWLEDGE the key** (Outer Wilds' ship log, Tunic's manual, La-Mulana items that *"do not grant any abilities, but open up a new area"*); **(3) buy variety with SETUP SELECTION** (Dominion's 10-of-26 kingdom ⇒ ~5.3M configurations with no unlock system; Really Bad Chess' randomised army). **Six shipped objects with zero consumers carry them**: `RETRY_VARIANT_KINDS` (D299), `CheckpointInteraction.prediction`, `AssistancePermission.locked_off` (D298), `shapeRecommendations`, `attempt_concepts` (D300) and `ObjectiveState.degraded`. **The campaign's binding constraint is delivery and sequencing, not content** — the same verdict `feedback-versus-the-dashboard.md` reached for the feedback layer, reached independently for the campaign layer. **Fact refresh that discharges two standing cost arguments** `[V]` at `c55b9cf`: the corpus is **47 packs, 11 of them middlegame** (commit `aee7c64`), so `06` §5's *"Act II is impossible today"* and this ledger's *"Plan Drill is essentially UNAUTHORED"* are both **stale**; `plyHorizon` 46/47 (median 10.5); deviations/checkpoints **330/189**; shapes attested **21 of 25** (D44 shrinks from 9 orphans to 4); `timingWindows` 4 and `variantOf` 2 (both were 0). Also new: **reward branch BREADTH** (Hitman's mastery challenges — *"there is never a 'wrong' way"*, and unconventional approaches unlock options) is the direct answer to *"make experimentation rewarding"*, computable as a census over the learner's own graph via `attempts.countable` + first-divergent-move, with the anti-farming guard being the shipped `plyHorizon` | campaign, D78, D298–D306 |
| D298 📊 | **The capability-suppressing boss is not "architecturally the same shape" — the whole suppression path ships end to end.** `AssistancePermission` is `"free" \| "locked_off" \| "sight" \| "evidence"` (`packages/runtime/src/assistance.ts:20`), with all three layers behind it: the producer (`permittedAssistance` returns `locked_off` on `role`/`deliveryOpen`, `:29`), a **named server refusal** `ASSISTANCE_WITHHELD` (`rest.ts:1090`, `:1107`, `service.ts:826`), and an honest UI sentence rendering the control disabled (`DrillScreen.svelte:717-720`) `[V]`. `AssistanceContext` has exactly three fields, so a boss suppression is one more input. **But it must NOT be a fourth field on `permittedAssistance`** — that function is the *honesty* gate (`06` §3 law 1, honesty outer / inventory inner), and putting a game rule inside it would make a campaign decision indistinguishable from an honesty decision. Required form: an **inner-gate mask** that composes with `permittedAssistance`, reuses the `locked_off` value and the error code, and carries a **different sentence** — *"this encounter withholds X"* is a game statement, *"X is not honestly available here"* is an honesty statement, and one screen must never render them identically | campaign, `06` §3 law 1, `05` §3 |
| D299 💡 | **`RETRY_VARIANT_KINDS` is a five-kind variety vocabulary that is authored, validated, counted — and consumed by nothing.** `["same_root_new_defense", "alternate_plan_class", "related_position_same_idea", "opposite_side", "different_material_details"]` (`packages/schema/src/drill-pack/types.ts:27-33`), **9 variants across 7 of 47 packs**; the only repo-wide consumers are the schema barrel and a `pack-check` counter (`pack-check.ts:24`, `:83`) `[V]`. Those five strings are **Dominion's kingdom sitting in our schema** — *the same board asked a different question*, which is the fun-without-power device that costs zero authoring. `same_root_new_defense` is DDR's difficulty chart (same song, different chart) built from shipped opponent modes; `alternate_plan_class` and `related_position_same_idea` are Chessarama's trick (keep the pieces, swap the goal) over our own nine-value `objective.type`; `different_material_details` is Really Bad Chess, and it is **honest only in the endgame**, where R4's κ = 1.000 makes the modified position exactly assessable | campaign, D297, Dominion, R4 |
| D300 🐞 | **The cross-pack collection axis is authored, stored, indexed — and namespaced apart by one injectable class.** Packs declare `concepts`: **186 tags, 156 distinct across 47 packs, only 24 appearing in ≥2 packs** (`advance-chain-base` in 6) `[V]`. They reach a real table with an index (`attempt_concepts`, `storage.ts:2529-2538`) and two live queries. **But the default resolver is `PackScopedConceptResolver`, whose key is literally ``pack:${packId}#${raw}``** (`progress.ts:56-59`), so the same string in six packs is six keys, and the related-attempt query is additionally scoped `AND a.pack_id = ?` (`storage.ts:1388-1393`). `ConceptResolver` is an **injectable interface** (`progress.ts:52-54`), so a corpus-global resolver is a one-class change. **Two things follow:** the Outer-Wilds-shaped "what you have seen" log is an assembly of two shipped producers (`concepts` = map mode, `shapeRecommendations` = rumor mode); and **it will not be a collection until the vocabulary is one** — 132 of 156 concepts are singletons, so today the honest collection is the 25-entry shape library | campaign, D297, `shapeRecommendations` |
| D301 💡 | **One shared position a day, and a spoiler-free share artifact — the cheapest complete feature in the campaign cluster, and it answers a row that has had no mechanism since it was filed.** Wordle: one puzzle per day identical for everyone, 2,309 curated words, and Wardle's stated posture *"people have an appetite for things that transparently don't want anything from you"* and *"Would I send you a push notification?… Why don't you just forget about Wordle for a little bit?"* — **and the emoji grid was invented by a player (Elizabeth S), not designed.** Chess already has two precedents: **Chessle** (guess an opening, 3 or 5 moves per side, 6 guesses) and **Chessguessr** (guess the five moves *actually played* in a real game, daily at 00:00 UTC, four-band legend) — **Chessguessr is our thesis in miniature and someone else shipped it**, grading against what a human played rather than what an engine prefers, the same choice R9 validated. **Every input ships**: 47 packs + **43 mined candidates in `content/candidates/`**, the run graph, and the `story_read` scoped anonymous token. The share artifact must be the **shape of your branch set**, never a score. Answers the standing *"why open it on Tuesday?"* gap in the skill/progress + return-loop row. **Killers:** a branch set has no fixed shape, so the glyph is the hard part and could become a score by accident; a daily is a cadence promise and a missed day is worse than never having one | campaign, return loop, Q1b |
| D302 💡 | **A three-axis histogram over the learner's OWN submissions — the one device that gives a flat power curve a real progression — with a pre-test that could kill it before any build.** From Zachtronics (Opus Magnum ranks *"speed in cycles, cost in G, and area in tiles"*; Barth chose a histogram over a leaderboard because a leaderboard *"tells you that you suck (and not even by how much)"* and *"is a fantastic incentive for cheating"*). Three axes exist that are about the **learner's process** and not the position's truth: **plies to the objective on the submitted branch** (bounded by `plyHorizon`, 46/47), **assistance rungs switched on at the moment of submission** (the axis **D78 licenses** — all-on is the measured *unreadable* state), and **the sealed objective state** (authored, not graded). **Chess.com already ships axis 2**: bot crowns are 3 for a win with no help, 2 with 1–3 hints/undos, 1 with 4+ — so this is adoption, not invention. **Two hard constraints:** (a) **no count of rewinds, forks or attempts may ever be an axis** — that is C1 in scoring clothes and the most likely accidental violation in the whole campaign; (b) the population is the learner's **own** prior submissions, never other learners (the ChessMonitor finding). **The pre-test that kills it: correlate rungs-on with plies-to-objective on the existing corpus. If they are one axis, the histogram is a disguised single score** | campaign, D78, D297, ChessMonitor |
| D303 🐞 | **`DESIGN-GAP:` `04` §1's Pack row describes an object that does not exist — and the campaign's unit is the RUN, not the pack.** `04` §1 defines a Pack as *"5–15 roots teaching one claim"*. The shipped format has **no `roots` array**: across all 47 canonical packs, `start` is singular and present 47/47 and `roots` appears **0/47** `[V]`. **What ships as a "pack" is `04`'s Root**, so the taxonomy has a shipped level 1 and level 4 (trajectory `legs`, 3 packs) and **nothing at levels 2, 3 or 5** — `family`, `track`, `curriculum`, `campaign`, `run_set`, `playlist` are **0 hits repo-wide**. Meanwhile `RunSessionKind = "pack" \| "position" \| "imported"` (`packages/runtime/src/types.ts:36`) and two of three kinds carry no pack (Just Play, `/start/fen`, flip-sides derivation, **`createRepertoireGapRun` builds a session from a gap key with no pack at all**, `service.ts:585-587`). **The rule that follows is sharp and shipped:** `projectAttempts` forces a pack-less run's verdict to `"open"` (`progress.ts:90`, `:127`), so a pack-less node **cannot seal**. Therefore **intermediate nodes may be pack-less; boss nodes may not** — and the campaign's content bill is bounded by act bosses (3), not by nodes (9). Verdict: the owner's *"packs are micro-DLC"* read is **right about the role, one level off on the grain**; a pack is a content contribution to a node, and the missing container is `04` §1's **Track**. By-product: the community channel is **less unexercised than reported** — `shape-studio.ts:33-34` ships draft→validate→register→export with four named refusals and registers every community shape with `channel: "community"`; what has **no** path is a third-party *Track* | campaign unit, `04` §1, ADR-0007, D297 |
| D304 💡 | **ADR-0007 wants a twin clause: unlocked by playing, never by WINNING.** ⚠️ **owner-facing.** The most consistent finding across the whole genre sweep, in four unrelated systems: *My City* drips on game count (24 games / 8 chapters / 3 per chapter); *Pandemic Legacy* advances after two failed attempts regardless; *Dicey Dungeons* unlocks *"regardless if you win or not"*; **Balatro's Mr. Bones unlocks on *"Lose 5 runs"*** and ships **105 of 150 jokers available on the first run**; *Dungeon World* makes a 6-or-lower the **XP trigger**. **Losing is a legitimate unlock currency in almost every system surveyed.** This matters *now* because `06` §5's accepted ruling gives the campaign a failure state, and the natural next step — gate the next lens on clearing the boss — is exactly the mistake all four avoided, and that WoW paid to undo (attunement: *"This strategy has largely been phased out"*, removed across four patches). Proposed form: **the run can be lost; the catalogue can never be locked.** Costs nothing today, expensive later | ADR-0007, campaign, `06` §5 |
| D305 💡 | **OWNER QUESTION: what is the campaign's progression denominated in, now that the power curve is flat by construction?** ⚠️ **owner-facing**, `design/research/fun-mechanics-outside-roguelikes.md` §10. Three answers, all law-8-legal, all about the same cost, producing **very different products**. **(a) Nothing — cadence and completion** (Wordle + Lichess Practice: a daily position, per-unit mastery marks over a named vocabulary, no number about the learner anywhere). Cheapest and most honest; also the weakest hook for a *run*, and close to the option `roguelike-run-design.md` §4d called *"strictly weaker"*. **(b) The learner's own history** (D302's three-axis histogram). The only option that gives a flat-power product a progression the learner can feel, with an honest basis nobody else has (D78) — but it introduces **the first number this product has ever shown a learner about themselves**, and two findings here say numbers outrun their basis (ChessMonitor's FIDE estimate; Celeste's preamble rewrite, where identical mechanics read differently on a sentence). **(c) The catalogue — a collection** (GW2 + Stardew: shapes met, structures played, the *what's-missing* mark living on the pack card rather than a progress screen). Most chess-shaped, and it turns `04`'s breadth into the reward — but our collection vocabulary is not yet a collection (D300). **(a) is a floor the others build on; (b) and (c) genuinely compete for the same screen** — a histogram of how you played and a map of what you have seen are two different answers to *"how am I doing"*. Picking one first is the ruling; the other can arrive later without being erased | campaign, D297, D300, D302, `06` §5 |
| D306 ⛔ | **Three mechanics whose transformation collapses the invariant — and in two of the three, HALF survives.** Per `design/02`'s amendment the refusal set should be near-empty, and it is. **(1) The prestige multiplier** (Cookie Clicker's Heavenly Chips = cube root of lifetime cookies; AdVenture Capitalist's angels ≈ 150·√lifetime; Kittens' paragon + a whole post-reset tech tree). Refused because a multiplier needs a quantity that measures how far you got *and* multiplies the next attempt's version of it — R4+R9 say no such quantity exists across a run, so it would multiply an invented number (C4 + law 8). **Adopted instead: the reset**, which is Gloomhaven's retirement (*"When a personal quest is completed, the character will retire"* — mandatory, at full power, unlocking a class for anyone) and is the answer to our read-once content decay. **(2) Rarity tiers** (Genshin: 0.6% base, hard pity at 90, 50/50; FEH: 3% + 0.25% every 5, guaranteed at 120, Spark at 40). Refused by `06` §3 law 5 and by ρ = **−0.143** — pricing a lens by scarcity prices noise. **Adopted instead: the pity GUARANTEE**, which is a fairness model over the offer stream, not a rarity model, and composes with the offered-choice draft at zero cost. **(3) The stamina gate** (Genshin Resin cap 200 at 1/8 min; HSR 300 at 1/6 min). **Refused entire** — it prices *playing*, which is strictly worse than pricing retrying (C1), and it is FTL's pursuit clock with the sign flipped. Its only adoptable fragment is its apology (HSR's Reserved Trailblaze Power). **Near-miss resolved by transformation, not refusal: literal chess variants** — Fog of War (*"Players can see only the squares where their pieces can legally move"*) is the capability-suppressing boss as a rule of chess, but `UCI_Chess960` is a **published capability refusal** (`capabilities.ts:105`) and every grounded instrument we own is a standard-chess instrument, so the variant is adopted **in the assistance layer** and refused **in the move generator** | campaign, law 8, ADR-0007, C1/C4, D297 |

Measured: (a) **`permittedAssistance` takes `sessionKind` and never reads it** — the body of `packages/runtime/src/assistance.ts:27-30` uses only `role` and `deliveryOpen`, so `pack`, `position` and `imported` receive byte-identical permissions and **there is no lever to make Just Play more permissive than a drill even if the owner rules that it should be**; (b) `loadAssistance` returns `SILENT_ASSISTANCE` for **every** unset profile (`assistance-preference.ts:30-33`), so **all six contexts default identically** — the six profiles buy six empty localStorage slots the learner must fill by hand, six times. D82 is correctly closed and its closing sentence *"profiles select defaults and never permission"* is half true: the permission half ships, the defaults half does not. `DESIGN-GAP:` — owner tier | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §3.3 |

| D316 🐞 | **"It steers you by classifying openings, strategies, endgames" is aspirational: classification describes, and exactly one instrument steers.** Censused, `classifyPhase`/`structuralReading`/`endgameReading`/`shapeFirings`/`pivotalMarkers` have **four** non-test consumers and all four are renderers — `DrillScreen.svelte` (sentences), `guidance.ts:30-46` (voice packet), `story.ts:97-122` (post-hoc slides), `pivotal.ts:47` (passive markers). **Nothing selects an opponent, a band, a position, an objective, a pack, a next node or an assistance rung from a classification.** Three specific breaks: **`shapeRecommendations` computes a `packIds` list and the client discards it** — `onclick={() => navigate("/play")}` labelled *"Find {packId}"* (`App.svelte:667`), dropping the learner on an unfiltered grid; **`PackList.svelte` renders `phase` as a chip with no filter, sort or grouping** (`grep -c 'filter\|sort\|select'` → 0) against `design/03:70-72`'s *"first-class navigation and filters"*; and **`/learn` contains the words opening/middlegame/endgame zero times**. The one genuine steer is **repertoire gap finding** (`/learn` → *Go to biggest gap* → `createRepertoireGapRun` at a gap FEN, at the repertoire's band) — and even it exposes only the **top-ranked** gap (`{#if index===0}`, `App.svelte:694`) while the server's `enter` accepts any `gapKey`, and its write-back verb `chooseRepertoireAnswer` has **zero client callers**, so scanning never converges. Also blocks `design/06` §1's encounter unlock, whose detector this is | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §4 |
| D332 💡 | **Measure the learner-s Elo — the owner-s answer 2026-08-16, and it is LAW-8-LEGAL, which nobody had noticed.** Offered three denominators for progression (own-history histogram / catalogue / cadence) the owner declined the fork: *"we need history of learner too… and maybe catalogue progress… but the idea is again: go from elo 1000-2000 so maybe we need to just measure player ELO properly."* **The apparent collision dissolves on inspection.** `design/research/coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` established the product can say neither *"played well"* nor *"run succeeded"* — no learner number exists anywhere, prediction grading was deleted at schema 0.8→0.9, and law 8 forbids grading moves. **But an Elo is not a claim about a move.** It is arithmetic over win/loss/draw against a known-strength opponent — structurally the same object as the explorer result splits the owner ruled admissible as `corpus_observed` (rung 4) on 2026-08-15: it says *what happened*, never *what was good*. **So Elo is the one learner measurement that requires grading nothing**, and it is the denominator the campaign has been missing since the power curve was ruled flat. It also makes *"you-d need proper 2000 Elo skills"* a claim about the **learner** rather than about the encounter-s configuration — the exact gap the coaching dossier identified and could not close. Both other denominators stay wanted (*"we need history of learner too… and maybe catalogue progress"*); this is the spine they hang on | 💡 open, owner ruling 2026-08-16 |
| D333 🐞 | **[[D332]] has one hard prerequisite and it is already ledgered as [[D324]]: nobody has measured that Maia-s band moves the RESULT.** R10 measured that the band moves the **distribution** — the policy vector differs by band, bit-identically reproducible. **That is not the same claim.** An Elo computed against Maia bands is meaningless unless Maia at 1500 actually *loses more often* than Maia at 1800 over played games. Evidence now points at the gap rather than closing it: `design/research/maia-wdl-versus-human-outcome.md` found band-to-band WDL movement correlates with the human population-s at **Pearson 0.02–0.04, sign agreement 47–52%**, against a real human movement of 1.2–2.2 pp after sampling noise — *"doesn-t settle D324, but it points."* **This is now the highest-value unrun experiment in the repo**, because it gates the campaign-s only viable progression denominator. Its shape is simple and it is the one thing R10 did not do: play games, band against band, and count results | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D334 💡 | **Long-term unlocks are an ideation topic, deliberately not decided now — and ADR-0007-s twin clause is refused as a HARD rule.** Offered *"progression is never unlocked by winning"* as a companion to ADR-0007-s *never purchased*, the owner declined it as an absolute: *"not as a HARD rule… IE reaching a high checkpoint, maybe you want to start at the latest level vs 2000 ELO or w/e… or a weird variant run… we don-t have to have all this answered immediately, we can ideate on long term rewards/unlocks that are fun and motivate more experimentation or playing."* **The distinction that survives, and it is the useful one: winning may unlock CONVENIENCE and VARIETY, never CONTENT.** Starting a later run at a higher band, or opening a variant run, costs the learner nothing they have already earned — whereas locking a structure, a technique or a pack behind a win would make losing subtract from what you have learned, which cuts against the thesis and against the submitted-branch ruling. Candidates to ideate on, none decided: skip-ahead starts, variant runs (see [[D327]]), modifier runs, cosmetic or titular marks, and a New Game+ that changes the *rules* rather than the *numbers* | 💡 open, owner ruling 2026-08-16 |
| D365 💡 | **Rating systems already solve [[D332]], and the one property that matters here is UNCERTAINTY.** Owner extension 2026-08-16: *"we can think of other ways to gauge progression… make it configurable in the profile or when opponents win/lose against bots readjust… there must be measures for this."* There are, and they are standard: **Elo** (K-factor, no uncertainty term), **Glicko/Glicko-2** (Lichess-s own system — carries a **rating deviation** and a **volatility** alongside the number), **TrueSkill** (μ and σ). **Glicko-2 is the obvious candidate and the reason is not accuracy, it is honesty.** RD lets the system say *"1450 ± 180"* and, more importantly, lets it **refuse to say anything** until RD narrows — which is the same discipline as the tablebase abstaining outside range, the explorer abstaining below 100 games, and the `unmeasured` disposition. A product that refuses to manufacture chess truth should not manufacture a rating either, and every alternative here either asserts a bare number or has no term for its own ignorance. **The owner-s two mechanisms map exactly onto it**: *"configurable in the profile"* is a self-declared seed with a wide provisional RD (Lichess-s own onboarding), and *"readjust when they win/lose against bots"* is the update step. Neither needs invention. **Both are gated on [[D333]] in the same way** — a rating derived from games against Maia bands is only as meaningful as the bands are real, and a Glicko update against an opponent whose declared rating does not predict its results produces a confidently wrong number faster than Elo would. Note also the encoding fact from `design/research/maia-wdl-versus-human-outcome.md`: Maia already emits an expected score directly, `0.5 + cp/2000` exactly, which is the same quantity a rating system predicts — so there is a cross-check available that most implementations never get | 💡 open, owner extension 2026-08-16 |
| D99 🐞 | **D64 would have compounded into certified costs.** `engine-leverage` §2.3's tablebase path and its criterion 4 would have derived and **digest-certified** `cost` values from the 135 manufactured syzygy records — turning fabricated provenance into cryptographically-stamped authored content. Every entry carries `origin.kind: "http"`, `status: 200`, a `tablebase.lichess.org` URL, and a `sha256` **of the local fixture body**. **The deferral's claim was already false when written** — all 135 landed a day earlier in `2fd82be`. **Nothing shipped catches them**, and the one honest trace (`"offline": true` in seven `job.json` files) is **written and never read**, confirmed by three independent tells that agree exactly (the timestamp formula; the fixture-payload digest, with identical match sets and *zero* partial matches; and `origin.bytes` of 117–123 against 190–8339 for real fetches). **Claude verified the mechanism and the offline flag directly: seven packs record `"offline": true` in their `job.json`.** And `offlineQuery` does not merely lack provenance — **it asserts `status: 200` from a URL no process contacted.** *Original:* **`offlineQuery` manufactures the provenance it records.** For `verify-draft.json` it synthesizes `retrievedAt` from a FEN hash and takes a digest of the bytes it just read — a record that attests to **the fixture**, not to the tablebase. | D61 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-15 by `client-surface-floor`.** Compact projections now assert board-to-viewport and board-to-ancestor containment plus `.drill-region` containment; the surviving document-scroll guard is explicitly desktop/tablet-only and annotated with its compact-tier limitation. *Original:* **The phone-viewport browser assertion cannot fail.** With the shipped `html,body,#app{height:100%;overflow:hidden}` plus `#app{position:fixed;inset:0}`, a 5000 px child still measures `scrollHeight` 844 = `clientHeight` 844 — so `drill.spec.ts:893-894` is vacuous, and unlike the desktop test it carries **no board bounding-box check**. `docs/app-shell.md`'s promise that *"the board remains visible"* is therefore **unasserted on phones**. Measured 2026-08-15 (`design/research/mobile-scope.md`). Third member of the tests-that-cannot-fail family, after D56's tidy fixtures and D54's single-file scanner — this one asserts against a property the CSS makes constant. **MECHANISM CORRECTED TWICE — final form 2026-08-15, established by driving the running app rather than a synthetic DOM.** The vacuity is **not** "unconditional `overflow:hidden`, constant at every width" — that intermediate claim was wrong and claude repeated it. The incumbent assertion **fails today** at 992×768, 900×700, 834×1194, 768×1024 and 720×1024. There are **three regimes**: `#app{position:fixed;inset:0}` makes it structural at ≤719 px, containment makes it constant at ≥1024 px, and it propagates genuinely in the 720–992 band. The defect is real and is demonstrated by **injection** — a 3000 px child moves the assertion by zero pixels on `/settings` at four viewports and on the drill route at 390×844 and 1280×720. **And the above-the-fold reversal is reversed back: at 360×640 and 360×740 the board IS below the fold today, on every tab.** `mobile-scope.md` was right; the `client-surface-floor` draft's "correction" was wrong, and claude relayed that wrong correction to the owner | ✅ closed 2026-08-15 by `client-surface-floor` |

| D30 ✅ | **CLOSED — verified 2026-08-15 by two independent passes (the reconciliation gate flagged it un-flipped; claude re-checked the code): `outcome.ts` now reports the fifty-move draw. *Original:* **The fifty-move rule and threefold repetition are not terminal outcomes.** `terminalOutcome` (`outcome.ts:5-11`) reports only under chessops' `isEnd()` = insufficient material or no legal moves. So a **Hold drill saved by repetition — one of the most common real defensive resources in chess — never fires `outcome.reached`**, and the automatic outcome rules cannot grade it. Consequence is wider than the cursed-win case that surfaced it: it silently constrains every defensive objective we ship. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-15: this is a defect, not a question.** (*"we're making drill packs — what do you think should happen if someone achieves the goal that would ALWAYS resolve in a draw in GM games? DRAW IS A COMMON OUTCOME IN TOURNAMENTS."*) Claude framed claim-vs-automatic as an open chess-rules question; it is not one. In a drill, a position repeated three times or a fifty-move counter run out **is a draw** — both sides in any real game take it — so the outcome fires and grading resolves it by objective: success under `hold`/`save`, failure under `win`, exactly as `pack-orchestrator.ts:243` already treats draws. No new vocabulary is needed. Fix: `terminalOutcome` reports a draw on threefold repetition and on the fifty-move count, not only under chessops' `isEnd()`. This also removes the reason `authoring-frictions` §8 grades through an authored condition — that workaround exists only because of this defect | 🔨 shipped by `authoring-frictions` §9 |

Hits endgame and Outcome Drill packs hardest, since those are the runs that actually end. Fixing it means producing `outcome.reached` on terminal detection and treating it as a reveal event | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by the second sourcing review |
| D12 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-12 by `outcome-drill-grading`.** *Pack C had never been playable — verified by execution.** Its `still-holding` checkpoint triggers on `materialBalance {black, atLeast, -1}`, and Black is *already* a pawn down at the root (White e4/f2/g2/h2 vs Black f7/g7/h6, balance −1), so the condition is true before anyone moves. The opponent's first move fires it, the objective grades `achieved`, `achieved` is absorbing, and `commitMove` throws `RUN_TERMINATED` before the learner ever moves. The one real `hold` pack we authored is a drill you cannot play | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by the Outcome Drill draft |
| D12a ✅ | **CLOSED — now a build failure, not a warning.** *No lint caught a trigger already true at the root.* Pack C passes `pack-check` green. A condition satisfied by `start.fen` is always an authoring error, and nothing detects it | 🐞 |
| D12b ✅ | **CLOSED by the monotone law + `resolveAt` → `preserved`.** *`achieved` is absorbing, so grading a hold as achieved ended the drill.* A defensive objective that succeeds *by continuing* cannot use a state that stops play. The proposed law: a state that stops play may only be entered where play has actually ended | 🐞 |
| D12c ✅ | **CLOSED with D12a** — the scenario is rejected at validation. *The disclosure barrier opened at ply 1* in the same scenario — `delayed_checkpoint` discloses on the first `checkpoint.reached`, so a checkpoint that fires immediately reveals everything before the learner has made a decision. Anti-contamination defeated by an authoring slip | 🐞 |
| D13 ✅ | **CLOSED.** *Zero-length segments were emitted.* `reachCheckpoint` pairs each checkpoint with the previous one, so two checkpoints firing at the same node produce a segment spanning nothing | 🐞 |
| D14 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-12 — diagnosed, not masked.** Root cause: the tests targeted Chessground's private `<cg-board>` during transient relayout; they now synchronize on the stable public `aria-label="Chessboard"` wrapper and require visible geometry. `retries` remains unset (default 0), so the fix is verified rather than hidden — ten consecutive zero-retry suites passed, five of them run independently. *The gate had failed once in five runs on an unchanged tree* `served Najdorf pack plays, rewinds, branches, compares, and exports` failed once in five consecutive runs on an unchanged tree, then passed four times. `playwright.config.ts` sets no `retries` (Playwright defaults to 0) with `workers: 1`, so a single flake fails the whole suite — and a gate that can report either result on identical code cannot be evidence for anything. Needs the flake diagnosed (the Chessground stale-bounds fix in `d1b26db` suggests timing sensitivity in that area), not `retries` raised to paper over it | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by independent verification |
| D26 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-14 by `structural-reading`** — dispatch is now an exhaustive `switch` with a `never`-typed refusal, so an unhandled variant is a compile error. **`matchesFenPredicate` fell through, so a new predicate variant silently evaluates as a pawn-structure match.** `packages/runtime/src/objective.ts:145-167` dispatches `transposeKey`, then `pieceOnSquare`, then **unconditionally** falls into the pawn-structure branch — there is no exhaustive switch and no default refusal. Any variant added to the `FenPredicate` union therefore reaches that branch with `predicate.white`/`predicate.black` undefined and is evaluated as a pawn comparison rather than rejected. Found by the structural-reading draft, which would have been the first to add one. A predicate language whose dispatch fails open is the never-silent contract at the layer that decides whether an objective fires | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D25 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `pack-studio §13c`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **`provenance` is a passthrough, so any future trust field can be forged in the document.** `provenance.additionalProperties` is `true` (`schemas/drill_pack.schema.json:598`) and `projectPackDocument` returns the object whole (`apps/server/src/pack-registry.ts:70`) — so an author writing `"channel": "official"` or `"reviewedBy": "…"` gets it validated, registered **and served to the browser**. The publication channel is safe only because it is server-derived and never read from the document; this row records that the *general* hazard remains for anything added later. `pack-studio.md` §13c closes it with a rendering allow-list rather than a schema narrowing, which would invalidate every committed pack | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D22 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-14 by `defect-batch-2`.** `opponentPolicy` now rejects unknown fields under pack schema 0.12, with an exact-path negative fixture and full committed-corpus validation. *Original defect:* an author could write a policy key that nothing reads and validation blessed it — D3's never-silent shape surviving in the pack format | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D23 ⛔ | **FILED IN ERROR; residual closed 2026-08-14 by `defect-batch-2`.** The emitter has emitted `phase` since its first commit (`608d80a`), which predates this row; all four candidates carry it and emitter tests already pinned honest omission for ambiguity. The added browser regression proves a null-phase pack remains visible as `unclassified`. Kept for the record per the rejected-rows convention. *Original claim:* `position-seeds` emits packs without `phase` | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D27 ✅ | **CLOSED by `shape-library` (`7cf2e32`); docs residual completed by `defect-batch-2`.** The structural envelope records finite samples without gating them; `docs/structural-reading.md` now carries quiet/load observations, the 100 ms investigation tripwire, and the visible-output command. *Original defect:* a wall-clock envelope asserted inside the unit gate could report either answer on identical code under load | 🐞 found 2026-08-14 |
| D24 ✅ | **CLOSED by `defect-sweep`; regression completed by `defect-batch-2`.** Secure cookies remain the default unless the exact string `false` opts out; both the environment encoding and omitted-option behavior are tested. *Original defect:* the light release profile needed the F3 auth boundary's transport default pinned | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D21 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-14 by `defect-batch-2`.** A segment now exists iff a validated `segment.completed` event exists. Coincident checkpoints emit none; genuine pre-guard zero-length records remain readable; forged branch/node/order/adjacency scope is rejected before it can widen authored reveal. *Original defect:* the producer refused coincident segments while `deriveSegments` invented them | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D20 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `pack-studio`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **A pack-digest mismatch silently disables orchestration while the run keeps accepting moves.** `#registeredPack` (`apps/server/src/service.ts:621-625`) returns `undefined` when `pack.digest !== run.packDigest`, and the four call sites then skip `orchestratePackMove` entirely — so checkpoints stop firing, objectives stop grading, and every authored disclosure stops, **with the run still accepting moves and reporting success**. The learner plays on inside a drill that has quietly stopped being one. Unreachable today only because a pack id resolves to one document per process; **pack versioning makes it live for every in-flight run of every pack that gets a new version**. `rfc/pack-studio.md` §3 makes run→pack resolution digest-addressed *before* the write path exists. **Review 2026-08-13 found digest-addressing alone is not total** — an official pack's bytes are git-mutable, a playtest run is orphaned by the author's next save, and a restored database misses rows; in all three the run kept accepting moves silently. §3a adds `PACK_UNRESOLVABLE` thrown from all four call sites *before* `commitMove` | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 by the studio draft |
| D17 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `live-session-platform`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **The lease claim has no compare-and-swap and no transaction — any write-capable learner can seize the board.** `claimLease` (`apps/server/src/storage.ts:758-777`) runs `UPDATE drill_runs SET active_writer_id = ?, active_writer_learner_id = ? WHERE id = ?` with no predicate on the current holder, and the role check is not in the same transaction. So a `participant` can take the board from a host mid-session, and two simultaneous claimants both pass the check, both update, and both believe they hold it. F3 delivered *who may write*; it never delivered **policy over possession**. **Scope corrected 2026-08-13 by adversarial review: the hole spans *every* run carrying a `participant` grant, not only coached sessions.** `mayWrite` returns true for `participant` (`apps/server/src/authorization.ts:21`) and grants are issued on any run through the shipped path, so a board-control mode keyed on session existence would have left every ordinary run exposed. Fixed in `live-session-platform.md` by deriving board control from the write-capable grantee count when no session exists, plus the CAS the claim never had | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D18 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `live-session-platform`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **A withheld spectator's board freezes with no signal.** `publicEvents` truncates at the barrier and pins `nextSeq` to `sinceSeq` (`apps/server/src/feedback-policy.ts:45-50`), so a polling reader asks for the same sequence forever. A player eventually reaches a checkpoint and unblocks; a spectator cannot, and nothing distinguishes "nothing new" from "withheld" — the honest-absence problem on a surface where the viewer has no way to resolve it. Fixed additively with `withheld: true` | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D19 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `live-session-platform`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **A multi-writer run cannot say who played a given ply.** `Node.actor` is `"user" \| "opponent" \| "system"` with no identity. It must **not** widen: in an Arena match both legs' reference-side moves are legitimately `"user"` though different humans played them, so putting a learner id there would break the very comparison the mode exists for. Attribution belongs beside the node, not inside it — `live-session-platform.md` derives it from a `board.granted` possession journal by interval lookup, with no run-schema change. **Additional gap found by review:** `deleteLearner` moves possession to `__legacy` (`apps/server/src/storage.ts:630-636`) and journals **nothing**, so a deleted learner's plies have no attribution at all | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D16 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by owner ruling: branch switch worries at 100 ms and intervenes at 200 ms**, superseding the unsourced `<50 ms`. Measured 45–53 ms sits comfortably inside. The ruling also fixed the shape — a worry/intervene band instead of a single pass/fail line — and `design/02` now records that the budgets are a tripwire for when to go look, not the criterion itself. *One operation carried two different budgets, and I had escalated an imperceptible delta against the stricter one* |
| D15 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13** — `policyModeApplied` records the applied mode, and the off-spine fallback records `human_common` by construction. *The `theory_strict` fallback was invisible to the product.* When a position leaves the authored spine the selector silently falls back to `human_common` and emits only a `console.warn` (`apps/server/src/opponent-selector.ts:455-459`) — it reaches no event, no run, no response and no screen. Consistent with `DEGRADED_POLICY_MASS` (`:469-471`), but consistency with an invisible signal is not a defence for **Line Drill**, a mode whose entire subject is theory: the opponent can stop playing theory mid-drill and nothing the learner or the run can see records it. **Owner ruling 2026-08-12: closed inside the Line Drill RFC, and it blocks acceptance.** `policyModeApplied` (`human_common | strong_engine | theory_strict | unknown`) is added to `opponent.move_selected.selection`; on-spine `theory_strict` records `theory_strict`, off-spine fallback records `human_common`, historical selections migrate to `unknown` and are **never inferred**. Run schema v0.7, migration 5. This reverses `outcome-drill-grading`'s refusal of the field, deliberately: that RFC declined it to preserve no-migration scope, and the owner ruled the migration is worth paying to turn a disclaimer into traceable run evidence | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 reviewing the Line Drill draft |
| D10 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **Both shipped Stockfish specs report `version: "unknown"`.** `parseIdentity` fills `version` from the UCI `id name` line *only* when `spec.name` is unset (`apps/server/src/engine-supervisor.ts:116-126`), and both specs set it — so engine provenance recorded against any evidence is anonymous. B6b works around it by omitting `name` in its authoring spec, which is a workaround, not a fix | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by the sourcing revision |
| D9 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **`start.side` is schema-optional, and the real consequence is an inverted grading verdict — not the crash originally ledgered.** Re-verified 2026-08-13 while drafting the defect sweep. The stated symptom (`packStartSide` throwing) is now unreachable: `apps/server/src/service.ts:183-185` 400s a side-less pack before any screen renders. Underneath it is worse. `apps/server/src/pack-validation.ts:369` silently coerces a missing side to white (`=== "black" ? "black" : "white"`), and `:346-348` then evaluates `learner === sideToMove` with `learner` **undefined** — always false — so the declared Syzygy category is passed through `opposite()` unconditionally. A `save` pack (tablebase loss) reads as a win. **It passes `pack-check` green and cannot be run**, so the wrong verdict is latent rather than observed — which is why nobody caught it. A silent inversion in the one place the product claims machine-exact grounding | 🐞 raised in severity 2026-08-13 |
| D8 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **The JSON Schema and the runtime validator disagree on two declared values.** `perfect_tablebase` (opponent mode) and `immediate_blunder_guard` (feedback policy) both validate against `schemas/drill_pack.schema.json` and are then rejected at load by `apps/server/src/pack-validation.ts:104-129`. Consequence beyond tidiness: the **on-ramp layer's declared feedback knob** (`design/00-thesis.md` §Target player specifies the 1000–1400 lane on exactly three knobs, one of them this) and the **endgame perfect-play opponent** are both currently unencodable. Same divergence class as D4 — two sources of truth for one vocabulary | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by the sourcing draft |
| D7 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `line-drill-theory-grading`.** *`deviations` were not linted at all.* The new `AUTHORED_PROSE_AFTER_LAST_CHECKPOINT` warning covers `annotations` only, so unreachable deviation prose passes silently — but Pack C found the gap is wider than reveal: **no lint checks that a deviation's `moveUci` is even legal in its anchor position, or that it belongs to the side to move.** An author can ship an illegal deviation, or one for the wrong colour, and `pack-check` passes. Both pack authors resorted to hand-auditing every deviation with chessops (10 and 12 respectively) | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by authoring |
| D6 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **`phase` never reaches the client.** `PackSummary` omits it (`pack-registry.ts:16-24`); `grep -rn "phase" apps/web/src` finds two unrelated prose strings. The Learn IA is organized on this axis | 🐞 |

## Authoring-format friction (💡 — raised by authors while writing real packs)

The point of authoring one pack per phase is to find these. Each is a concrete
proposal from someone who hit the limitation with content in hand, which is the
evidence the withdrawn contracts RFCs never had.

| Friction | Proposal | Raised by |
|---|---|---|
| A plan drill's objective cannot be expressed. Success is relative to the **captured intent** — did you execute the plan you committed to — but `successConditions` supports only intent-blind `reach_checkpoint`, so plan packs ship with no objective at all | Intent-relative success conditions. **Path found 2026-08-13 (`05` §5c): grade the plan by its structural consequence.** The predicate machinery ships and is authorable; only feature-level vocabulary (Q4b) is missing. Still connected to `intent_capture` being inert and to the objective-type widening in program #4 | Pack B (Carlsbad), 2026-08-12 |
| The on-ramp's blunder-guard knob has no encoding. `defect-sweep` removes `immediate_blunder_guard` from the schema because `feedbackPolicy` has no capability publication, no applied-policy record and no honest degradation — so `design/00-thesis.md`'s 1000–1400 lane loses one of the three knobs that define it. Re-add it as a *real* policy with those three properties, not as an enum value. **SHIPPED 2026-08-14 (`onramp-guard`): `immediate_guard` at pack 0.14, post-commit, two rung-0 tiers + recorded-eval tier. Scheduled note kept for history: v1 form is the post-commit guard — the move commits, the guard offers play-on-or-rewind — per the Dr. Wolf transformation (their pre-commit retract inverts commit-before-learning). Pre-commit form is invariant-review material if the owner's session says beginners need it** | 💡 raised by the defect sweep, 2026-08-13 |
| **For a middlegame pack the two authoring rules fight each other, and no instrument sees the fight.** "Follow the most-played move at the band" is what makes a spine corpus-grounded; "the referenced shape must keep firing" is what makes it a *structure* pack. Measured 2026-08-15: from the French Advance family root the most-played walk liquidates the d4 pawn and `closed-centre-chain` stops firing within three plies; the Grünfeld's most-played reply is a central lever that ends the fianchetto race; the Maroczy's most-played line survives intact. Authors resolved it by hand, one extra walk at a time (~12 min each), and each stated the departure in a SPINE SELECTION RULE provenance line. A walker that reports, per node, whether a named expression still holds after each candidate move would make the conflict visible instead of archaeological | Middlegame wave (10 packs), 2026-08-15 |
| Deviations have no link to a plan class. 11.a3 and 11.Rab1 are literally the *same plan*, and `accepted_alternative` says nothing about that | Optional `planClassId` on a deviation | Pack B, 2026-08-12 |
| `concept_violation` does two different jobs — a **timing** error (right plan, wrong moment) and a **plan-coherence** error (a move that contradicts the committed plan). Collapsing them loses the distinction the feedback most needs to make | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-15 by `deviation-classes`.** Optional multi-valued `mistake` separates `plan`, `timing`, and `tactical` from the unchanged objective-relative class; no machine reclassifies the author's judgment. | Pack B, 2026-08-12 |

| Comparison column ceiling | 💡 **owner ruling wanted.** `n-way-comparison.md` caps a comparison set at 8 branches and originally cited `design/03-product-breadth.md:288` for it — **that citation was fabricated; no such statement exists** (the only "eight" there is eight program items). The RFC now asserts 8 on its own grounds. Design should either ratify the number or set its own, because it is a product constraint on the surface the whole rewind-and-compare loop leans on | `03-product-breadth.md`, Q9 |
| Prediction distribution delivery | 💡 the withholding barrier **truncates** the event stream at the first engine-feedback event, so a synchronous response is a legitimate and distinct delivery path. Worth stating in design, since "it isn't gated" and "it is delivered" were conflated once already | `explanation-grounds`, ADR-0006 |

## In-run assistance surfaces (💡 batch, owner ideas 2026-08-13)

Three ideas from the owner, ledgered on utterance. They compose: (1) says *what
kind of position this is*, (2) says *what evidence applies to that kind*, and (3)
is a distinct thing — deterministic sight, not evidence at all.

**Design home: `05-in-run-experience.md`**, written the same day so these do not
sit as loose candidates. All three are generic in-run features — true whether or
not a pack is loaded — and that document places them: the classifier is what
makes the assistance rail selectable (§4), configuration per session context is
one of its four varying axes (§4), and discovered-threat sight is **rung 0** of
the assistance ladder (§3), the only rung that cannot manufacture chess truth.

| Idea | Take | Home |
|---|---|---|
| **Phase classifier with an in-run sidebar** | Early/middle/endgame surfaced live during play, not just as a catalogue filter. Partly covered already: Q4c owns automatic recognition, D6 is `phase` never reaching the client, and the Learn IA is organized on it — but all of that is *navigation*. A live in-run classifier is new, and it is what makes (2) selectable. Must stay non-authoritative over curated pack boundaries (standing rule) and must abstain honestly rather than guess | Q4c, D6, `03-product-breadth.md` |
| **Configurable engine/assistance sidebar** | Run simulations, ask for best move or a move *set*, from whichever source is applicable — Stockfish for evaluation, Maia for what humans at a level actually play, Lichess explorer for frequency, Syzygy where the material allows. **The new part is not the sources — B4 already owns those — it is that assistance becomes configurable per session context.** Just Play, streamer mode and a curated drill want different defaults, and "what assistance is available here" is currently implicit everywhere. Includes structural classifiers for middlegame ideas (the Dragon bishop wanting an open long diagonal), which is the deterministic-features work in Q4b rather than an engine question | B4, Q4b, Q8, `03-product-breadth.md` §Intelligence |
| **Discovered-threat visualisation ("shift to see two moves of consequence")** | 💡 **transition arithmetic shipped 2026-08-15; the visual/hypothetical surface remains open.** `transition-primitives` now answers “what changed on the committed move?” on request with rules-derived attack, defence, line, flight-square, duty and irreversibility facts. R3 refused an unasked live tier. Holding a modifier over a candidate move or projecting two plies remains a distinct anti-contamination/form decision and is not claimed by that RFC. | ADR-0005, ADR-0006, opponent-intent row, Q4b, Q9, `transition-primitives` |

## Strategic reading and pivotal detection (💡 batch, owner 2026-08-13)

Specified in `05-in-run-experience.md` §5/§5a/§5b; rows kept so none is lost.

| Idea | Take | Home |
|---|---|---|
| **Prophylaxis / denial as a first-class readable** | "After a4, Black's knight can never use b5 again" is rules arithmetic — rung 0, cannot be wrong. A denial move is **invisible to every eval-first tool** because nothing happened, which makes it the clearest demonstration that rung 0 sees things an evaluation cannot explain. Mirror of the ledgered opponent-intent prompt | `05` §5, ADR-0005 |
| **Structural readables: outposts, diagonals, pressure balance** | All three are computable without an engine — an outpost is a pawn-defended square in enemy territory unattackable by enemy pawns; a diagonal's blockers can be named; attackers and defenders can be counted. **Detection is free; significance is rungs 2–5 and must be attributed.** Q4b's deterministic-feature work is this | `05` §5, Q4b |
| **Tactical build-up ("what is each side aiming at")** | The one part of the owner's list that is *not* deterministic. Rung 2/3 territory, and the place where a strategic classifier would be tempted to manufacture truth. Keep separate from the structural readables above rather than bundling them into one "threat classifier" | `05` §5, ADR-0005, Q8 |
| **Auto-detected pivotal moments for pack-less play** | The gem, per the thesis amendment. Honest detectors: irreversibility (rung 0), phase change, **human divergence** — the Maia distribution at the learner's level splitting several ways, which is a fact about the distribution rather than a claim about chess and uses a model already shipped — and option collapse. Engine eval swing is deliberately *excluded* as a primary detector: it finds where someone erred, which is the post-mortem framing this product replaces, and it cannot fire before the error | `05` §5a, Q4c, B2 |
| **Content transfer test** | 📐 owner test 2026-08-13, audited in `04` §0a: *does authoring this make Just Play better?* Result for the shipped format — **no**. Every field is anchored to the pack's own move tree except four, and the three that carry reusable knowledge cannot escape: `planClasses` are inlined per pack, `concepts` have no cross-pack identity, `feedbackClaims` have no trigger so they can never fire. Authoring a pack today improves exactly one drill and contributes nothing to an unauthored game. **Rule: content earns its cost by how much of it fires in a game nobody authored** — not a ban on line content (opening theory *is* a move sequence) but a budget and a direction, and the reusable half currently rounds to zero. Minimal fix: packs **reference** shape entries instead of inlining them; `fenPredicate`/`materialBalance` are already the two shipped triggers that key on the position rather than the tree, so the machinery exists and the authoring habit does not | `04` §0/§0a, B11, Q4b |
| **Feature-level structural predicates (the unification)** | ✅ The shared structural-expression grammar is executable in pack objectives and shape entries. Predicate waves 1–3 supply eighteen detector kinds, and `plan_consequence` now binds an authored plan class to its reusable shape-plan success signature without claiming intent. Pack B exercises the binding on its authored line. | `05` §5c, Q4b, `predicate-wave-3` |
| **Game story — a finished game as ~8 pivotal slides** | ✅ **SHIPPED — verified 2026-08-15**, `docs/game-import-and-story.md` §Grounded moments: `GET /runs/:id/story` is a disclosure-gated derived projection over shipped deterministic detectors (irreversible moves, phase changes, option collapse, eval pivots, first endgame entry, shape spans); the payload returns all moments with a deterministic rank and **the client displays up to eight**, ordered by ply. Voice may phrase the packet, never add chess claims. Was carried as open in claude's 2026-08-15 inventory — an error caught the same day by checking `docs/` instead of the ledger — original row: owner 2026-08-14: *"we have our pivotal moments... maybe we can summarize a game with states and how it influenced the game? a set of 8 slides."* **Everything it needs is the backward half of machinery we already specified or shipped**: retrospective pivotal detection (`05` §3a — eval swing is *permitted* backward, plus objective-state transitions and last-point-the-result-was-available), phase classification (B10, in flight), structural naming (B9, shipped), and the outcome grade. A slide = position + what kind of moment it was (detector-attributed) + what changed after it. **Post-game, so ADR-0006 does not bite** — the game is over, nothing left to contaminate; this is the one surface where the full ladder may speak at once. Natural tie-ins: the share/spectate links (a story is the shareable artifact a run link never was), session-to-pack distillation (a slide's moment is a candidate drill root), and Just Play's review path. Law-8 rule: each slide *names* its detector and evidence; the connective narrative is LLM-as-voice territory (packet-bound, §3b-i). Slide count is presentation, not contract | `05` §3a/§5a, B10, share links, teardown-taketaketake (research in flight) |
| **Own-game review — import one game into the rehearsal frame** | ✅ **SHIPPED — verified 2026-08-15**, `docs/game-import-and-story.md`: `imported` is a third session kind; one pasted PGN or one public lichess game URL (chess.com refused with guidance, no public per-game contract); selecting a story moment rewinds to its `entryNodeId` and creates a `story-reentry` branch, so the game opens back into live play rather than a report — which is exactly the line that keeps it out of the rejected v1 identity. **Export ships too** (all branches, rehearsal branches as legal PGN variations). Browser-tested end to end — original row: owner 2026-08-14: *"we said it's not a game review, but we have all the pieces (+the LLM) — in context of our rewind / classifiers."* **The v1 boundary, pinned:** what stays rejected is mine-games→detect-weaknesses→generate-lessons as identity and mandatory entry (AGENTS.md, ADR-0003). What this is: **one game, imported by choice, as an entry context** — backward pivotal detection finds the moments (`05` §3a), game-story renders them as slides, tapping a slide rewinds *there*, branch, replay vs human-like resistance with structural reading on. The review IS the rehearsal entry — the anti-version of the engine-review screen, not a return to it. **Shipped pieces:** PGN export w/ variations (B3), Arena leg PGN import on a request path (live-sessions — verify reusability as the parser seam), backward detectors (B10), structural naming (B9), position runs (F2 + B11's player). **Missing:** a first-class import endpoint creating an imported run from PGN or a lichess/chess.com game URL (external fetch = ToS/licence note needed), and the review surface itself. Composes with game-story into one RFC — both consume B10's backward detection. Account-level fetch-all-my-games stays out | `05` §3a, game-story row, ADR-0003, B2 entry contexts |

Both solved it locally (recovery via `/analysis` + honest absence; a `ready` gate). If a third consumer hits it, the fix is structural: evidence jobs for *historical/batch* analysis should not ride the live-play queue's pruning rules — a batch class exempt from `onRewound` | evidence-queue, branch-groups §, game-import § |
