| **Just Play difficulty: what is the opponent, and what can "fair but challenging" mean?** | 💡 owner 2026-08-15: *"if you just play, what is the difficulty of the opponent? do we track elo and slide it from 1000 to 2000? or configurable? or IM/GM level? we have so many sources for moves — we can make it fair but challenging?"* **As shipped:** Just Play runs `human_common` — Maia sampling its policy head at a `targetElo` the session may set — so an Elo slider already exists, but unbounded (D60) and un-inherited-safely (D58). Five modes ship: `human_common`, `strong_engine`, `theory_strict`, `perfect_tablebase`, `practical_resistance`. **The honest ceiling:** Maia is a human-behaviour model trained at bands ≈1100–1900. **There is no IM/GM-like option and cannot cheaply be one** — above the band the only shipped opponent is `strong_engine`, which is not a strong human but a different species, and weakening it is rejected doctrine. **The honest floor on "fair":** *adaptive* difficulty needs to measure how hard a position is for this learner, and R4+R9 jointly say that is impossible in the middlegame — engines are silent on undecided positions, humans past ply ~20. So mid-game difficulty can be **selected** (a band) or **authored**, never **measured**. **What that leaves, and it is more interesting than a slider:** a *phase-aware* opponent — theory in the opening, Maia at band through the middlegame, tablebase-perfect or `practical_resistance` once the endgame is decided. The sources the owner names are real but they cover different phases, not different strengths | Just Play, R4, R9, resistance spectrum |
| **Q3 answered: tolerate mobile, and the TABLET floor is the unmet one** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/mobile-scope.md` — verdict **tolerate, responsive-only**, with a two-tier floor: phone = the run loop, read surfaces and 2-column compare; full surface at 768 px and up; **mobile-native stays a non-goal**. Not phone-shaped, named: 4–8-column compare, branch groups ≥4, live session detail, pack studio. **The finding that reframes it:** the **720–992 px band (iPad portrait) is worse than the phone** — it loses the fitted grid at ≤62 rem without gaining the compact tabs at ≤719 px, which is exactly the *"drill scrolls like a document"* shape the owner rejected, and **no browser projection covers that band**. The tablet floor is currently **unmet**; that is the one thing worth fixing. Market corroboration from the repo's own dossiers: Lichess shipped its phone client **without** studies, learn-from-mistakes or analysis and absorbed the complaints — phones win where the unit is one board, never where it is a tree | Q3, E5, `branch-set-scale` |
| **Move primitives: what a move DOES (the transition vocabulary)** | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-15 by `transition-primitives`.** Pack 0.22 adds a sibling transition grammar with exact attack/defence target changes, surviving slider-ray changes, geometric destination changes, defensive-duty threshold crossings, move irreversibility and before/after structural composition. Three real packs grade it and Just Play renders committed-edge facts on request. R3 removed the live tier; routing, prophylaxis, forcing claims and multi-move history remain refused by name rather than smuggled into the census. | question shapes, predicate wave, `docs/transition-primitives.md` |
| **R1 answered: the transition census is cheap** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/move-primitive-computability.md` — 593 spine transitions replayed from all 35 packs. The **entire** census (attacks, defences, lines, control delta, escape squares, overload, tempo proxies, irreversibility, routing) costs **29 µs/ply** from two FEN strings: 0.58 ms for a 20-ply branch, 4.7 ms for an 8-branch comparison. **Cost is not an argument against the category in either direction.** ~70 of 290 implementation lines are verbatim copies of code already in the tree but private (`pivotal.ts:32-57`, `structure.ts:401-410`). Measured selectivity, which nobody had — **now stated as UPPER BOUNDS**: the `transition-primitives` draft read the harness and found it keys attack/defence relations by `(attackerSquare, targetSquare)`, so a rook that moves and still defends c1 is scored as a *lost* defence. Both readings are coarsenings, so the true firing rates are lower and require re-measurement against the target-keyed definition. Overload **6.7%** < check 7.1% < irreversibility 13.2% ≪ attacks 50.6% < lines 52.6% < escape squares 61.2% < defences 74.9%. **The owner's "overload" instinct picked the sharpest instrument in the set** | question shapes, predicate wave |
| **The engine request contract (promote to design tier)** | 💡 named 2026-08-15 by `rfc/engine-request-contract.md`, and it is the sibling of the declared-vs-executable law rather than an instance of it: **that law governs what a document may SAY; this one governs what a call must DO.** *An engine request must close over the instrument state its answer depends on* — **state** (every option whose value changes the answer is sent by that request, never inherited), **clear** (every accumulated search state the request does not want is cleared), **bind** (the clear and the search it protects are ONE queued exchange — independently violable, and the `position-seeds` reset is a pure *bind* failure with correct clearing), **bound** (every value sent lies inside a published range, or the request is refused by name), **record** (every value applied and the answer taken appear in the persisted record). Its one-line form: ***state a request does not state is state the previous request chose.*** Four ledgered defects are each exactly one missing obligation — D35 is *clear*, D58 is *state* + *record*, D59 is *state* + *record* (the RFC's own preferred remedy widens the window, which is *state*; the original *record*-only mapping was retrofitted), D60 is *bound* — and the two laws overlap on **two obligations** (*bound* wholly, *record* partly) at **one ledger row**, D60 — the draft's "exactly one point" was contradicted two sentences later by its own text — which is why that row cites the older law correctly and is silent on the other three. Belongs in design tier once the RFC lands | declared-vs-executable law, `engine-request-contract` |
| **`piece_distance` — the attestation base is weaker than the ruling was told** | ⚠️ **owner-facing correction, 2026-08-15.** The owner ruled to absorb `piece_distance` into 0.18 on the stated ground that it "meets the wave's own bar — two attestations for non-king roles". Measurement afterwards showed **both** of those attestations (`knight-vs-bishop`, `up-an-exchange`) live in shape entries **no pack references** (D44), against `piece_count`'s 143 corpus leaves. The bar is about what authors hit, and they did hit it, so this does not overturn the ruling — but the owner decided on a base materially thinner than presented and should see it stated. Two further measured limits argue for scoping: **rook, bishop and queen take only the values {1,2}** across all 615 corpus positions against a king target, so slider-distance leaves are near-useless; and the **vacuity trap bites where authors would reach for it** — 85 of 440 positions have every white bishop off-shade from the black king, concentrated in `mate-bishop-knight`, a bishop-and-knight mate pack. Under an ∞ convention `atLeast n` would have been *true* across the wave's own demonstration packs | `predicate-wave-3`, D43, D44 |

Two packs now authored. **The process lesson generalizes past this row: a deferral justified by an instrument reading needs the instrument checked, exactly as a claim does** | wave 4b, sourcing |

Same family as the tablebase walker (4 attestations) and 'no repo command evaluates a draft pack' (5 attestations) — authors keep rebuilding the verification tooling the repo does not ship | authoring frictions |
| **R4 answered: decidedness, not piece count, is the gate for measured difficulty** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/practical-difficulty-outside-tablebase.md` — **the answer is no.** Practical difficulty is measurable exactly where positions have determinate outcomes and only there: inside 7 pieces that is 88.3% of the corpus with perfect agreement; outside it only **10.2%** of 284 positions are decided (5.8% at depth 16), and the metric silently becomes an unstable magnitude filter wearing the same name — concession sets reproduce across depth on 99.4–100% in range but **29–54%** out of it. Consequence for the campaign cluster: **bosses whose difficulty is a *measured quantity* are decided-position-shaped**, which in the committed corpus means endgame-shaped. A `practical_resistance` that probes once and refuses by name when |eval| is inside the threshold would extend honestly to decided middlegames — that is the variant worth building | R4, campaign cluster, `resistance-spectrum` |
| **R9 answered: the human oracle DISCRIMINATES but does not REACH** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/human-outcome-coverage-depth.md`. **The discrimination half is a yes with force:** at band 1600, 124 positions Stockfish calls level (|eval| < 50 cp) span human scores **0.405–0.600**, and **Pearson(cp, score) = −0.079** — the engine explains **under 1%** of the variance. Move-level is sharper: among **2,814 move pairs Stockfish cannot separate** (|Δcp| < 30), **475 (16.9%) are separated by ≥5 pp significantly**, max 22.3 pp. Human data sees what the engine cannot, exactly as R4 predicted. **The reach half is a no:** usable coverage (n ≥ 400, a threshold stated before measuring) ends at **ply ~20** at position level and thins from **ply 12** at move level — mean moves clearing 400 games falls 10.1 at ply 4–7 to **0.17** at ply 20–23 — with literally zero games by ply 27 | R4, campaign cluster |
| **The middlegame has NO difficulty oracle of either kind** | 🐞 **the structural consequence, 2026-08-15.** Engines are silent where positions are undecided (R4: 10.2% decided out of range, median |eval| 43 cp). Humans are silent past ply ~20 (R9). Measured difficulty therefore covers **endgames and the first ten moves**, and **the middlegame between them has no oracle at all**. **And it is not a data-collection problem.** R9 measured every population lever: whole-database window ×3.51, all six speeds ×1.35, three merged rating buckets ×3.10, **all three at once ×23.4** — and a 23× larger population moves choice-level coverage only from ply 19–21 to **ply 23**. No population setting reaches the middlegame. Any design that assumes measured difficulty mid-game — bosses, `practical_resistance` outside endgames, adaptive scaling — must instead use **authored** difficulty there, and say so | R4, R9, campaign cluster, `resistance-spectrum` |
| **On-ramp grounding is worse than the ledger recorded** | 🐞 2026-08-15 (R9): band **1000 has 45–100× fewer games** at plies 12–19 than 1400 and dies ~5 plies earlier, and **all 26 puzzle-derived on-ramp roots return 0–5 games at every band**. The earlier row said only that our artifacts cover 1400/1600/1800 while the on-ramp targets 1000–1400; the measurement says the on-ramp lane's positions are **largely ungroundable by corpus evidence at all**, not merely mis-banded | on-ramp lane, sourcing |
| **The shipped 100-game floor is exactly the 60/40 line** | 💡 2026-08-15 (R9): `explorer.ts:91` floors at 100 games. At per-game variance ≤ 0.25 that resolves 60/40 and nothing finer — while the measured signal is a **2.6 pp mean** deviation (4.9 pp p90), which needs **n ≥ 400**. Defensible as shipped, defensible for nothing finer, and it silently under-powers every claim built on it | sourcing, evidence |
| **Explorer coverage into middlegames is the highest-value follow-up** | ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-15 (R9, `design/research/human-outcome-coverage-depth.md`) — and the answer creates a named gap.** *Original: *2026-08-15, R4's closing finding and the sharpest lead it produced: human win/draw/loss counts are **the only oracle that exists where engines have no result**. Where a position is undecided (|eval| 43 cp median), Stockfish cannot say who is in trouble — but a large enough human sample can say who *loses from here*. **The blocker is coverage, not compute**, which is a very different and much cheaper problem than the one we assumed | R4, Q6, sourcing |
| **Maia advertises `Elo`** | ✅ 2026-08-15 (`design/research/practical-difficulty-outside-tablebase.md`) — closes `resistance-spectrum` open question 2 affirmatively. Maia advertises `Elo`, `SelfElo`, `OppoElo`, `Temperature`, `TopP`, `MultiPV`, so §3's `eloHonored` seam is a check that will **pass**, not a refusal. Also: Maia caps its candidate list at 20 regardless of requested MultiPV (median 35 legal moves out of range), but those 20 carry a median **99.99%** of the policy mass — the absent mass is real and small | resistance spectrum |
| **R2 REFUTED: routing is a renderer, not a detector** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/move-primitive-computability.md` — **claude's hypothesis, tested and failed.** The claim was that distance-to-target-square makes the knight-reposition case a census rather than an opinion. The arithmetic is exact and the recall is perfect (**9/9** authored arrivals register, all the same 2→1 event) — but the **false-positive rate is 98.7%**: it fires on 38.4% of all transitions and **49.6% of quiet piece moves**, with **0.0% precision** under the sharpest filter across 48 firings, dominated by endgame king walks. Even with the target supplied it does not pick the move — **52.8%** of the piece's own legal alternatives also close the distance. And routing explains only **9 of 17** author-labelled repositions; the rest are vacation, attack-set coverage, safety and later-move — different primitives. **The correction: the census is real, but the TARGET SQUARE SET is the judgment, and that is the uncomputable half.** Corroborated by six shape-library notes naming distance as a gap — all six name the *static* quantity, not the delta | question shapes, ADR-0005 |
| **`structuralDelta`'s cost is a defect distinct from its deadness** | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-15 by `transition-primitives`.** `evictionChanges` now parses the before/after FEN once and reuses those positions across the 128 square/colour probes. The exported function remains unconsumed; fixing its latent cost did not make it a transition dependency. | move primitives, dead vocabulary, `docs/transition-primitives.md` |
| **Intent grading has a measured, content-dependent ceiling** | ✅ **RULED 2026-08-15: grade what has an authored structural consequence; refuse the rest BY NAME.** `predicate-wave-3` ships capability publication, a named learner-facing refusal, and the applied plan-class record. The parallel signature pass moved the corpus to 96 non-null and 21 null signatures out of 117; that is content coverage, not a format guarantee, and future passes must remeasure rather than preserve the old ~45% title. | question shapes, ADR-0005, `predicate-wave-3` |
| **`structuralDelta` and `vacationReading` ship and are dead** | 🐞 2026-08-15: `structure.ts:425-447`, exported at `index.ts:52,55`, consumed by **nothing but tests**. This is transition arithmetic — the exact machinery the owner's move-primitives question needs — already written, exported and unused. Third member of the dead-vocabulary family after `preserve_plan_window` and the dead `mate-two-bishops` condition | move primitives, validator integrity |
| **Sunset rule for zero-use vocabulary** | 💡 amended 2026-08-15: usage is measured across packs and shape entries, never packs alone. Predicate wave 3 deprecates `pawn_count` and `piece_reach_count(scope: every)` with warnings and assigns their removal to wave 4; a future zero-use element still needs an explicit keep/remove ruling so the vocabulary cannot grow monotonically by accident. | predicate waves, admission rules |
| **Vocabulary audit: the reassuring half** | ✅ recorded 2026-08-15 (`planning/pack-vocabulary-audit/`, `6c7a579`) so the negative result is not lost: **no remaining constant authored conditions** — 27 positional conditions varied across probes and all seven checkpoint conditions fired on replay, so `mate-two-bishops` was the only dead one. Sixteen shape expressions lack a positive probe witness but **none is proven constant** — an honest "unknown", not a defect. **Coverage follow-up completed 2026-08-15:** the post-wave emitter set had grown to 107 fixed literals with 41 still unpinned; constructed refusal fixtures, explicit schema-shadowed dispositions, and a drift guard now leave zero without a test disposition | validator integrity, test coverage |
| **Build your coach — assistance as unlockable inventory (the campaign core)** | 💡 owner 2026-08-15, and it is the first gamification idea with a **mechanical** core rather than a cosmetic one: *"in campaign mode we are strict in where you can rewind and how often and what kind of hints/help/theory you can get (or have to 'buy' like a potion)… what if you have built the right combination of theory/classification/hints which basically allows a noob to play against an IM/GM boss, and still win because it has the right help? You basically build your coach."* Unlocks come from clearing (parts of) drill packs **in the run**, and a boss is declared: *"on this phase the end boss will play as white and apply this attack, or play as black and prefers defense Y"*, with theory supplying the counter. **Why this fits rather than fights the architecture:** it splits the assistance ladder into two independent axes that we have been conflating — **policy** (what may honestly be shown, and when — `05` §3/§3a, unchanged) and **inventory** (what this learner currently *has access to*). Deck-building operates only on inventory; every honesty rule survives untouched. A "boss" is expressible from shipped primitives today: a pack with a declared `opponentPolicy` plus a theory spine whose counter-plan is gated behind an unlock. And ADR-0007 holds by construction — you unlock by playing, never by paying. **Post-session by the standing ruling**, but this is the design that should be written first when that gate opens | gamification cluster, ADR-0007, `05` §3, encounter unlocks, Spire map |
| **Roguelike as the frame, not the decoration (owner posture 2026-08-15)** | 💡 **Research owed before design — see `planning/campaign-research-queue.md` (R1–R8), owner ruling 2026-08-15: *"we need to do more research before we can turn that into design and RFC"*.** *"we can gamify the shit out of chess, make it a roguelike game."* Recorded as a **posture**, because the parts have now independently converged into a coherent roguelike architecture and it is worth seeing them as one design rather than five rows: **map** = the Spire path (career mode row) · **encounters** = drills, puzzles, bot matches, continue-from-here (encounter-unlocks row) · **inventory/deck** = assistance primitives you unlock and combine (build-your-coach row) · **constraints** = rewind budget and hint economy · **resources** = time controls as something spent or stolen · **bosses** = packs with a declared opponent policy whose counter-theory is gated behind unlocks. The synergy claim is the interesting one and it is testable: *"what if you built the right combination of theory/classification/hints, which basically allows a noob to play against an IM/GM boss and still win because it has the right help?"* — i.e. the win condition is **the quality of the coach you assembled**, not raw playing strength. **The standing sequencing ruling still holds and matters more as this grows:** the campaign wraps the core loop, so if the loop is wrong the wrapper is wasted work. Post-session — but this cluster has earned a real design doc the moment that gate opens, rather than being reassembled from six rows | gamification cluster, ADR-0007, `05` §3 |
| **Rewind budget as a difficulty axis** | 💡 owner 2026-08-15, inside the campaign idea and worth its own row because it touches an invariant: campaign mode restricts *where* you may rewind and *how often*. Rewind is currently unlimited and is one of the six in-run invariants — *"the first attempt is never erased"* is preservation, not unlimited retry, so a budget may be compatible. But it must be checked against `05` §1 rather than assumed: a scarce rewind changes the punishment-free-experimentation claim, which is a ledgered thesis-level property. Ruling wanted when gamification opens | `05` §1, punishment-free experimentation |
| **Practical difficulty has no learner-side grade** | 💡 2026-08-15, handed off by `resistance-spectrum` §7a: that RFC defines `humanConcessionMass` ONCE as a shared primitive (Maia policy mass over moves a grounded classifier calls conceding) and deliberately does **not** invent a `successCondition` kind for it, because it may not claim the pack-schema lane. The learner-side consumer belongs to the vocabulary wave, whose exact shape §7a specifies. This is the other half of the owner's *"how can I annoy my opponent"* question. **R4 (2026-08-15, `design/research/practical-difficulty-outside-tablebase.md`): the metric is exactly gradable only in DECIDED positions, so the §7a `successCondition` inherits a *decidedness* gate, not a piece-count gate.** | question shapes, predicate wave |
| **`resist` is graded by an authored ply proxy** | 💡 2026-08-15: `pack-orchestrator.ts:244-256` grades `resist` as "terminal loss + authored checkpoint reached" — i.e. *survived to ply N*. `01-training-model.md` defines it as **maximize practical difficulty**. Nothing measures difficulty, so the grade is a proxy standing in for the definition. Closes when the row above ships. **R4: it cannot be replaced by a measurement in undecided positions — median |eval| 43 cp, only 10.2% decided — so authored proxies remain CORRECT there, not merely tolerated.** | `01` §Outcome types |
| **Maia policy-scalar stability is unmeasured** | ✅ **measured 2026-08-15** by the `resistance-spectrum` tagged integration probe: 20/20 identical requests at target Elo 1800 returned byte-identical policy vectors. This strengthens but does not replace determinism by record: the shipped selection cache and logged selection remain authoritative on replay, and Maia still advertises no seed option. Earlier partial: `design/research/practical-difficulty-outside-tablebase.md` §8 measured 60/60 repeat pairs at n=2 and policy mass on 120/120 probes | Q5, engine workers |
| **Branch-set scale: pruning, collapse and eval budget (RULED)** | ✅ **OWNER RULING 2026-08-15**: *"if you make 9 branches it becomes cumbersome… if 4 are completely lost that is obvious pruning so it can be hidden from main views but if you expand or w/e ez prune… we don't want to overload the entire machine with 99 branches of eval AND the user ux."* **This is pruning and scale, NOT ranking-as-verdict — the doctrine refusal stands untouched.** Three parts: (a) **collapse decided-lost branches** from main views, expandable on demand; (b) **bound the eval work** — 99 branches must not mean 99 evaluations; (c) easy manual prune. **The honesty key, and it is the same one R4 found:** *"completely lost" is a DECIDED position.* `design/research/practical-difficulty-outside-tablebase.md` measured that outcome class is exact where a position is decided and meaningless where it is not (median |eval| 43 cp, only 10.2% decided out of range). So collapsing a decided-lost branch states a settled fact; an undecided branch can never be auto-collapsed, because nothing can honestly say it is worse. Decidedness does the work that ranking was being asked to do — with no verdict. *Original row:* found 2026-08-15 while verifying the open-work inventory. The owner asked (2026-08-13) for *"good UX on ranking all the branches… rank with Stockfish right?"*. But `docs/n-way-comparison.md:11-12` states as shipped doctrine: **"The payload never ranks branches, computes an eval delta, or recommends a winner."** That refusal is not an oversight — it is law 8 / ADR-0005 applied to the compare surface, since a ranked column list is the "dashboard, not a drill" anti-pattern named in AGENTS.md. **The resolution is probably a distinction the ledger has not drawn:** ranking-as-verdict (which branch was *better* — refused, correctly) versus ranking-as-management (which of my twelve branches do I keep, fold or discard — a pruning affordance, no chess claim required). If the owner wants pruning, it can ship without touching the verdict refusal; if the owner wants Stockfish ordering, that is a deliberate doctrine change and should be ruled as one. Do not build either until ruled | `n-way-comparison.md`, ADR-0005 |
| **The 99-branch eval worry was aimed at the wrong cost** | ✅ measured 2026-08-15 by `branch-set-scale`: **compare already spends ZERO engine work** — the overlay is derived only from durable `evidence.attached` events (`compare.ts:179-196`) and `Service.compare` enqueues nothing. The real shipped bill is one **100 ms** movetime job per committed ply, paid at commit time during play (`service.ts:605,643`), at concurrency 2. So 99 branches never meant 99 evaluations; they mean plies already paid for. The genuine scaling problem is the **UX** half — the O(B·N) rail (D42). Worth recording because the owner's instinct was right about the symptom and the cost lived somewhere else | branch-set scale |
| **Collapse must check SHORTFALL, not admission** | 🐞 caught 2026-08-15 in cross-review, and it is the sharpest near-miss of the branch work. The draft's rule was `decided ∧ ¬admitted`, reusing the shipped admission check. But `¬admitted` is **symmetric**: under a `hold` objective it folds a **won** branch, and under `save`/`resist` it folds every `win`, `draw` and `cursed-win` — each with a grammatically correct sentence explaining why. It would have hidden the branches that *beat* the objective, confidently. Not what was ruled (the ruling says collapse decided-**lost** branches). Fixed with a shortfall rule over the shipped `CATEGORY_RANK`, with the consequence stated as a feature: **under `save`/`resist` the tablebase ground collapses nothing, ever.** A second, independent near-miss in the same section: syzygy returns the category **for the side to move**, which on a leaf is the opponent about half the time, and the draft never converted — so collapse would have folded winning branches on roughly a coin flip | branch-set scale |
| **Bind `/deviations/{i}/cost` to engine evidence** | 💡 2026-08-15 — created deliberately so an unowned hand-off becomes a visible gap. `deviation-classes` (0.21) adds a per-deviation `cost` and delegated its evidence admission to `opening-evidence-path` (0.20); that RFC declined it. **Closed by claude as coordinator: `cost` ships author-declared and UNBACKED, no capability claims it is verified, and no surface may render it as engine-confirmed.** The binding is real work neither wave scoped: 0.20's engine record attaches to the pack ROOT (`assessedBy`) while `cost` is per-deviation, so per-move evidence linkage is its own design question. Until it lands, a declared `cost` is exactly as grounded as any other authored number — which is to say not at all | `deviation-classes`, `opening-evidence-path`, declared-vs-executable law |
| **Authored contexts declare; unauthored contexts default (owner ruling 2026-08-15)** | ✅ **A general principle, ruled on the `outpaced` case and worth applying past it.** The owner: *"drill packs add primitives, then use them in tailored experiences, so they must have FULL flexibility. But in Just Play, one would argue that entering an outpaced race means you made a mistake."* So: **a pack may declare per window whether `outpaced` grades** (full authorial control, no default imposed on authored content), and **Just Play grades it as a failure by default**, because entering a race a tempo behind IS a mistake when nobody authored the position. The generalisation: wherever the product must choose a semantic, an authored context supplies it and an unauthored context needs a stated default — the two should never be collapsed into one global answer. Applies to `outpaced` now; candidate for every future grading-honesty fork | `tempo-vocabulary`, Just Play, grading honesty |

Answers whether measured difficulty, bosses and resistance work outside endgames at all — and the Scandinavian lesson applies directly: check the instrument, not just the reading | R4, Q6, campaign cluster |

| **Encounter unlocks — content discovered by meeting it in your own games** | 💡 owner 2026-08-14, the standout of the progression batch: *"people unlock packs through finding the patterns in their games."* The machinery is entirely shipped: the classifier detects a Carlsbad in your Just Play game or imported story → the moment becomes an **encounter** → the matching shape entry + packs unlock with the provenance shown ("you met this structure on move 12 against X — now rehearse it"). It transforms the lootbox drop into grounded discovery: the reward is REAL (a moment from your own play), the unlock is an event fact (honest-progress compatible), and it makes the library feel earned instead of assigned. Composes with: shape detection (B9/B10), own-game import, milestones, the return loop | `04` tracks, return-and-progression, game-import |
| **Career mode — the Spire map** | 💡 owner 2026-08-14, concretized same day: *"a Slay-the-Spire-esque game where you walk paths, and the fights are a drill or puzzle or match against bot or a continue-from-here-and-achieve-X."* **Every node type already exists as an episode kind**: fight = pack drill · skirmish = puzzle-consequence seed (on-ramp) · duel = position run vs Maia at your band · **achieve-X = a position + an outcome objective (convert/hold/save)** — the achieve-X node IS the shipped outcome grading · elite = anti-opening pack or a save-from-worse · **boss = a trajectory** (opening→middlegame→endgame, the thesis as a boss fight). Path branching is thematically chess-native (the aggressive route vs the solid route); relics/card-unlocks = encounter-unlocked shape entries (row above); a lost fight is not game-over — it becomes a **save node** (recovery-as-skill as level design). Map generation can be seeded from tracks (`04` §1) + priority data. No skill numbers needed anywhere — advancement is event facts. **RULED 2026-08-14: the map gates its own rewards, the library stays open** — a chosen mode over an always-open catalog | `04` §1 Track, encounters row, outcome grading, trajectories |

| **Branch groups — play N candidates in parallel** | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-14 by `branch-groups`.** A durable group records 2–8 ordinary branches from one decision, sourced by hand, authored theory, Maia replies, or engine top-N. Fixed resistance uses a position-keyed reply journal because Maia does not honor seeds; per-branch is the explicit opposite experiment. The live explorer remains an operator-side sourcing tool rather than a runtime seed source. *Original idea:* a third multi-branch capability distinct from simulate and compare, where the learner plays every candidate as a set | `03` §Branch groups, B3, `05` §3, `docs/branch-groups.md` |
| **Multi-branch view shapes (tree / carousel / stack / grid / fractal subdivision)** | 💡 owner 2026-08-13. **The view follows the shape being examined, and there are three** — a *run over time* (sequential forks; the interesting relation is ancestry, so a thread or tree), a *group at one moment* (N parallel candidates; the relation is sibling difference, so a grid), and a *single branch deep* (one board plus timeline). Treating those as one problem is why "which view" feels unanswerable. The owner's **fractal subdivision** — a rectangle that splits on each fork — fits the second shape well and the first poorly, since it shows containment rather than sequence. **Correction 2026-08-13:** an earlier version of this row computed a "depth budget" from how many boards fit legibly in a fixed viewport (27 at 200px, 81 unreadable at 114px) and called it a ceiling. **That was the wrong question** — the owner pointed out pan/zoom removes it, and they are right: a canvas can hold an arbitrarily deep grid and you navigate it. What actually survives is not a pixel limit but an **overview-versus-detail tradeoff**: at any zoom you can see the whole structure or read a board, not both. The answer to that is **semantic zoom** — cells render *different content* by scale, not the same content smaller: at far zoom a cell shows result, structure name and objective state; at near zoom it shows the board. A pan/zoom canvas inside a region is also compatible with the viewport-contained shell, since it is not document scroll. Also interacts with: the walkthrough finding that compare *selection* is cumbersome (a branch group makes selection disappear — the group **is** the selection), and the branch-race row as the two-board special case | Q9, `03` §Branch groups, comparison-column-ceiling row |
| **Branch ranking for pruning** | 💡 owner 2026-08-13: *"we need good UX on ranking all the branches… rank with Stockfish right? we need to prune and drop stupid branches."* **The need is real and the engine is the wrong ranker.** Three reasons. (1) Ranking branches by evaluation *is* "an engine review screen with a rewind button" — the failure shape `AGENTS.md` names as the thing this product must not become. (2) It breaks ADR-0006 on the comparison surface: telling a learner branch B is worse **before they have played it out** hands them the answer, which is the same contamination the whole withholding barrier exists to prevent. (3) It is usually answering the wrong question — *"stupid branch"* in practice means *"I abandoned it after two moves"*, not *"the engine dislikes it"*. **Rank for attention, not for quality.** The signals that decide which branches deserve screen space are already computed and are all rung 0 — `branchCards` (`apps/web/src/lib/screen-model.ts:135-152`) carries `objectiveState`, `terminal`, `intent`, `firstMove`, `forkNodeId`, `origin`; add depth, and structural divergence from siblings (a branch that transposed back teaches nothing a sibling does not). A branch you committed an intent to and played to a terminal outweighs a two-ply stub, and no evaluation was consulted. **Engine evaluation is legitimate as one column in review, after the outcome — never as the sort key.** And **prune must mean collapse, never delete**: *an attempt is never destroyed* is an invariant (`05` §1), because the product's one original claim is comparison of two preserved attempts. Note the tension to keep straight: `rfc/archive/n-way-comparison.md` normatively forbids ranking fields and comparative sentences — that prohibition is about *asserting chess quality*, and ordering a rail by completeness and divergence does not assert any | Q9, `05` §1/§3, ADR-0006, `rfc/archive/n-way-comparison.md` |
| **Silence as the assistance default** | 📐owner 2026-08-13, and it corrects a default `05` §3 got wrong: rung 0 being the *safest* thing to show is not an argument for it being **on**. Proactive assistance is in tension with the thesis — telling a learner their knight has no outpost before they move coaches them past the mistake that would have taught them. Default during committed play is **silence**; the rail exists and the learner may open it. `05` §3a | `05` §3a, ADR-0006, `00-thesis.md` |
| **Guided ("clippy") mode** | 📐owner 2026-08-13. Live tips while you play — and it is **not** an exception to the silence default but the **shape library rendered live**, i.e. what B9+B10+B11 produce with assistance turned up. **One line carries the design: a tip may name what *kind* of position this is and what that kind is generally about; it may not say what is good *here*.** "This is a Carlsbad; the standard plans are minority attack, central break, kingside" is shape content, true independent of this game. "Play the minority attack" is an evaluation, and during committed play that is the ADR-0006 violation. Not the Clippy people hated: rung-0/authored so it cannot be casually wrong, structure-keyed so it is not generic, delivered as the already-ruled **passive marker the player may open** rather than a modal, and it is a chosen mode. **Real risk, distinct from all of those: tips that never stop remove the need to look** — rung 0 exists to teach seeing, and naming the outpost every time does the seeing for the learner. So it is band-shaped: natural default for the 1000–1400 on-ramp where naming a concept precedes rehearsing it, off by default above, with an explicit intent that it fades | `05` §3b, B9/B10/B11, `00-thesis.md` §Target player |
| **LLM as the guided-mode voice** | 💡 owner 2026-08-13 (*"we don't have the LLM for nothing… the clippy can be beautifully annoying"*). Correct division and squarely inside ADR-0005, which forbids the LLM **inventing** chess truth and has never forbidden it having a voice: **the classifier is the source, the shape entry is the claim, the LLM is the mouth.** Contract is checkable — an evidence packet (detected structure, plan classes, rung-0 facts that fired, phase) is assembled first, the model gets the packet plus a persona and may choose wording/order/tone, and **may not introduce a chess noun, square, move or judgement absent from the packet**. `docs/explanation-grounds.md` already ships grounded rendering of this shape, so this extends a mechanism rather than inventing one. "Beautifully annoying" is a real property: personality makes a concept memorable, and what must be annoyed is the learner's *complacency*, not their play — hence the passive-marker delivery, opinionated inside a panel they opened, never seizing the board. **Cost/degradation under hosted multi-user:** per-move generation is a per-user bill and a per-ply latency budget, and is unnecessary — tips fire on detected shapes, which are sparse, and the shape entry already carries canonical authored prose. Default is the authored text with the LLM re-voicing on request; provider absent means the learner loses the personality and keeps the identical claim | `05` §3b-i, ADR-0005, B4, B10 |
| **Declared honest target as the motivator** | 💡 owner 2026-08-13, recorded at thesis level. Being worse feels like prolonging a defeat, which is why people bail out of exactly the endgames that teach most. Declaring the true target up front — *drawn with correct defence, hold it* — makes being worse the **premise** rather than the failure, and gives a winnable goal from a losing position. Binding constraint: **the target is only settable where the result is assessable** — tablebase below eight pieces, authored or engine judgement above and labelled as such. Declaring "this is held" wrongly is real damage: the learner grinds a defence of a position that was already lost | `00-thesis.md`, `01` §Outcome types, B9 |
| **Punishment-free experimentation** | 💡 owner 2026-08-13. Human-like resistance makes a game worth playing; preserved branches make trying something free — play the dubious sacrifice, see what a real opponent does, still have the position you left. **Guard rail:** the consequence stays mandatory and only the retry is free. *Rewind is an experiment, not an undo* (`05` §1) — frictionless rewind during play would mean nothing is ever committed and the rehearsal collapses. **Play it out, then go back; never take it back.** Also implies the rewind should be *offered* after a failed recovery, not merely available | `00-thesis.md`, `05` §1/§3a |
| **Recovery as a first-class skill** | 📐owner 2026-08-13: *"the fuck up, then recover, and if recovery fails grind out the draw."* That sequence is most of real chess at 1400–2000 and every trainer skips it because they all optimise for finding the best move. Vocabulary already exists — `01` §Outcome types has **save** = rescue a worse position — but it has been a drill variant rather than the normal path through a run. Adds a fourth thing a run can be about: not *what should I have played* but **what can I still do from here** | `05` §3a, `01` §Outcome types, B2 |
| **Retrospective pivotal detection** | 📐owner 2026-08-13 (*"or go back to that pivotal point"*). Resolves an apparent contradiction: `05` §5a excludes engine eval swing as a **live** detector because it finds where someone erred and cannot fire before the error — that stands. But *going back* is a backward-looking question, and there eval swing is exactly right. **Forward** (is a real decision arriving?) → irreversibility, phase change, Maia divergence, option collapse. **Backward** (where did it turn?) → eval swing, objective-state transitions, the last point the result was still available. Using the backward detector forward is the post-mortem framing this product replaces | `05` §3a, §5a |
| **Endgame steering as named technique** | Recognize the type, name the technique, let the learner execute, grade the result — never "play Rc8". Most tractable phase for honest guidance: types are structurally recognizable, techniques are finite and named, and ≤7 pieces has tablebase ground truth | `05` §5b, `04-content-architecture.md` §4 |
