| Target learner/coach problem value | Competitive whitespace is insufficient; interview and concept-test the recurring job and preference over simpler workflows | exploration **Q1b**, `planning/exploration/plan.md` |
| Learning effect versus simpler training | Test transfer and delayed retention only after a slice exists; cannot gate construction of its own test instrument | exploration **Q1c**, H1–H4 |
| Mobile (PWA vs native vs non-goal) | Brief excludes native from v0; whether the loop is a strong mobile fit was never examined | exploration **Q3**, `02-product-shape.md` |
| Runnable human-opponent policies (Maia-3 variants, ChessMimic if reproducible, corpus baseline, future released models) | Benchmark identical positions/seeds; Chessformer is Maia-3's architecture, not a separate engine | exploration **Q5**, `arch/research/source_index.md` R41–R42 |
| Branch growth and compare comprehension | Owner n=1 walkthrough 2026-08-11: fork/rewind is quick and promising, but manual compare selection is cumbersome and the current comparison lacks enough instruction to judge learning value. Test default-all selection, 2/4/8-branch overview, grouping/cleanup, and phone separately | exploration **Q9**, `02-product-shape.md`, `planning/drill-client/log.md` |
| Branch race UX (two boards, alternating moves) | Tangible divergence but high cognitive load — experimental, optional | `arch/10 §Board swapping` |
| LLM as renderer of validated evidence (never source of truth) | Bounded by ADR-0005; wording/summarization only. Owner walkthrough confirmed its absence is conspicuous: the next feedback slice must actually render validated authored/engine/Maia/corpus evidence instead of stopping at generic evidence chips. **Correction 2026-08-12:** Maia is not an unintegrated source — policy mass is parsed (`opponent-selector.ts:225`), persisted in the v0.4 selection payload, and already reaching the browser inside `opponent.move_selected` (the barrier gates only `evidence.attached` and engine-referenced objective changes). It is a rendering gap, not an integration gap, and `docs/explanation-grounds.md` grouping it with corpus/Syzygy is a category error | Q8, `arch/09`, `arch/rfcs/RFC-0006`, `planning/drill-client/log.md` |
| LLM concept-anchoring agent (self-hosted LLM/provider + search + embeddings) | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-10: when a run derails off the trajectory spine, classify the resulting structure, retrieve the nearest curated concept/pack, switch objective, narrate the transition. Retrieval + deterministic features + engine validation pick the anchor; the LLM only words it — stays inside ADR-0005. Also candidate authoring assistant (drafts stay unpublished until review, per ADR-0001) | Q4b, Q8, `arch/07 §related-position jumps` |
| Position/structure embeddings for related-position retrieval | 💡 the retrieval layer the agent above needs; compare against pawn-structure signatures (Q4b's deterministic features) before adopting learned embeddings | Q4b, Q6 |
| Opponent-intent prompts ("what does their move want; what is the moved piece no longer doing?") | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-10: extend intent capture (currently own-move only, `arch/03 §Commit`) to reading the opponent — prophylaxis/threat-scan checkpoints. Drillable with curated claims + features; strong fit for the sub-1400 blunder-stopping leg | `01-training-model.md`, Q8 |
| Deployment packaging — root compose.yaml (server + healthchecked Maia sidecar), GHCR multi-arch images pulled by digest, compose profiles (light vs engines), devcontainer sharing the same toolchain (kills the machine-lacks-stockfish class of gate failures) | 📐 ruled 2026-08-12 alongside the docker-required ruling; scheduled for the client/vertical-slice era | rulings in `planning/archive/engine-workers/log.md` |
| Browser-run engines (stockfish WASM; small Maia via web runtime) | 💡 owner posture 2026-08-10: pushes compute to the client, hosting cost → near-static + agent inference; Chess Endgame Training [V] proves the PWA/WASM pattern. Complements ADR-0004, doesn't replace the server workers **Proven in the wild 2026-08-14:** ChessMind AI ships Maia-2 as in-browser ONNX (`maia2_rapid.onnx`, elo conditioning, policy sampling, six bands ~1100–2000+) — a competitor demonstrating the exact deployment this row proposed (`teardown-chessmindai-desk.md`, Q5 datapoint) | Q2, `arch/12`, `research/competitor-value-props.md` |
| On-ramp pack lane (1000–1400) | 📐 designed 2026-08-10 at thesis level: same pack object, three knobs (2–8-ply branches, pack-declared immediate blunder-guard, principle/threat objectives). Adds a per-pack feedback-policy field to the pack format — note for the future drill-pack RFC | `00-thesis.md §Target player` |
| Content architecture — full-breadth map (both colours, chosen + faced openings, 10 structure families, 7 endgame families, trajectories, on-ramp) | 📐 designed 2026-08-11 on owner ruling: content is planned at product breadth, not one defense | `04-content-architecture.md` |
| Anti-opening packs — drill *facing* an opening you don't play | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-10, from lived pain: White vs the Caro-Kann — 3.e5 (Advance) is enticing but hard to handle (c5/f6 breaks, Bf5 placement, Tal Variation 4.h4 sharpness). You chose 1.e4; the *opponent* chose the resulting board states and threats you must know. Repertoire trainers drill your side of your openings; nothing drills the defender's/anti side. Strong candidate first pack — owner can dogfood it. Demand signals: R46–R49 | `01-training-model.md`, Q7 candidate pack, `research/source-index.md` R46–R49 |
| Authored explanation vocabulary — claim `when` triggers, timing-window semantics (planMoves/opponentArrival/luxury accounting), authored-boundary provenanceMode, feedback-packet abstraction, non-Stockfish evidence refs | 📐 **revived 2026-08-12 — the withdrawal's root-cause diagnosis was partly wrong.** Withdrawn 2026-08-11 after three failed reviews as "an authored vocabulary with no authored content to design against". The alignment pass falsified the load-bearing premise: `authoring-contracts-v03.md` §1 argued claim `when:` triggers cannot reuse the runtime's `ObjectivePredicate` "without duplicating a second vocabulary", but `pack-orchestrator.ts:39-59` **already** translates the pack's key-discriminated `SimpleTrigger` into exactly that runtime type and `evaluateObjectivePredicate` (`objective.ts:195`) evaluates it — the seam is in production use for checkpoints. Six of nine contracts pin against merged code today; only the timing move-set, non-Stockfish payload widening, and the LLM provider genuinely lack a pin. Scheduled under program #2. The reviews' salvage is preserved in the withdrawn RFCs: boundary must be "plyHorizon caps, does not grant"; `pack:` must split per id space; comparison scopes by {branchId,startSeq,endSeq}; recorded-claims requires a real recording site or it is off-cursor re-evaluation renamed | `rfc/authoring-contracts-v03.md`, `rfc/evidence-composer.md` (both withdrawn), Q7 |
| Breadth #2 split history | 📜 RFC 2026-08-11. The composer review found claim triggers, executable window semantics, a boundary combinator, and a multi-source ref grammar all assumed-but-absent; they became a prerequisite amendment RFC rather than implementer improvisation | `rfc/authoring-contracts-v03.md`, `rfc/evidence-composer.md` |
| Off-spine graceful-degradation contract — how feedback honestly thins out as a run deviates from authored content | 💡 2026-08-11 from owner's deviation question. Deviation handling itself is designed (move classification incl. "interesting deviation — continue and compare"; off-objective branches saved not marked wrong; objective transformation; opponent deviations required; agent re-anchoring). The unwritten part: the UI must visibly downgrade from authored coach-voice to instruments-only (engine/corpus/tablebase) at the authored boundary, and the LLM must not paper over the gap (ADR-0005 pressure is highest off-spine). Needs an explicit section in the future drill-pack RFC | Q4a, Q8, `arch/04 §Stage B`, `arch/09 §off-objective` |
| Forward-branching "simulate" — at a spine node with N variations, auto-fork N branches, walk each authored line to its end, render a grid of mini-boards showing the resulting structures | 📐 scheduled under program #5. Owner idea 2026-08-11. Cheap by construction: spine playout is deterministic (no engine), previews are REAL branches in the run graph (enter one and keep playing vs the opponent; compare; export all free). Pairs with the explain-sidebar: sidebar explains, simulate shows. **Two verified costs (2026-08-12), neither fatal:** authored plies must commit as `actor: "system"` — an `opponent` commit demands an engine selection and corrupts read-back replay (`runtime.ts:196-204`, `replay.ts:68-74`) — and every move currently enqueues a Stockfish job (`service.ts:405-411`), so an unguarded 4×12 simulate submits 48 unwanted jobs. Depends on N-way comparison (see the N-way row) | `01-training-model.md` Stage A, `rfc/archive/drill-client.md`-follow-up, Q9 |
| Step-indexed reasoning transcript (steal from ChessMotive) | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-14 by `open-answer-grading`.** The implemented closed three-row form captures candidates, plan, and fears and diffs the learner against their own prior attempt; shortlist and concrete-line rows remain deliberately out of scope | `docs/open-answer-grading.md`, `01-training-model.md`, Q8 |
| Category-scan scaffold for the on-ramp (steal #2 from ChessMotive) | 💡 2026-08-12. Their Foundation track walks a fixed scan — **checks → captures → threats → piece improvement → pawn moves last**, auto-advancing per category. Cheap structured intent capture, no engine, and it is the natural vehicle for the owner's "what does their move want / what is the moved piece no longer doing" prompt at 1000–1400. Pairs with the reasoning-transcript row: the scan produces the transcript's first row (candidate generation) | `04-content-architecture.md` §6 on-ramp, `01-training-model.md`, Q8 |
| Feedback-timing convergence (ChessMotive) | 🔬 external validation 2026-08-12: an independent product in our band withholds authored explanation until after commit — the same rule as ADR-0006, reached independently. Not a change; recorded so the decision has outside support rather than only principle | `arch/adrs/ADR-0006`, `teardown-chessmotive-desk.md` |
| Prediction checkpoints — flip the board at pivotal moments, predict the opponent's reply, then explain why/why not | 📜 **authored half already ships** (verified 2026-08-12): `CheckpointInteraction.prediction` with `grading{source,topK,minMass}` and `flipBoard` is in the v0.2 schema, and the sparsity constraint below is *mechanically enforced* — `lint.ts:115-138`, max 2 predictions per segment. What is missing is delivery (`pack-registry.ts:66-71` projects checkpoints to `{id,label,actions}` and drops `interaction`, with a regression test asserting it), a durable interaction record, and a board-orientation parameter. `grading.source: "engine"` is not backed — the strong-engine profile ships `multiPv: 1`. Scheduled under program #5. Origin: owner idea 2026-08-10. The active form of opponent-intent prompts; solitaire-chess/"guess the move" pedigree with a twist no competitor has: grade the prediction against the **Maia distribution at the opponent's level** ("42% of 1500s play Qb6; the engine's c4 is found by 8%") plus engine validation — three-way honesty (what's likely, what's best, what you feared). Constraints: a checkpoint *interaction*, not a mode; pack-authored, sparse (pivotal moments only — interrupting mid-segment fights the uninterrupted-consequence stage of the episode, `01-training-model.md §3`); explanation obeys ADR-0005 (claims + frequencies rendered, never freestyle LLM). Board-flip optional per checkpoint — perspective-taking gain vs disorientation cost is a Q9 prototype question | `01-training-model.md`, Q8, Q9 |
| Checkpoint/compare explanation sidebar with selectable evidence layers | 💡 owner walkthrough 2026-08-11: reveal the authored theory claim and objective change, Stockfish best line/evaluation, Maia chosen move plus plausible alternatives/policy mass, and historical-game evidence after commitment; let the user control layers. An LLM may connect and phrase only this validated bundle. This is the missing theory tie-in—anti-contamination governs timing, not permanent absence | Q8, ADR-0005/0006, `research/competitor-value-props.md` §WhyThisMove, future feedback RFC |
| Compare defaults and multi-branch overview | 📐 scheduled under program #5, and the **first** thing in it. Owner walkthrough 2026-08-11: eligible branches should be checked by default; repeated manual pair selection is cumbersome. Ordering rule from `03-product-breadth.md` stands — manual N-branch inclusion is correct first, default selection and branch scoring optimize it afterwards | Q9, N-way comparison row, forward-branching simulate row |
| N-way branch comparison (the runtime change under every multi-branch surface) | 📐 scheduled under program #5, **precedes** simulate/branch-race/overview. Verified 2026-08-12: comparison is pairwise at the *runtime type* level — `BranchComparison` hard-codes `{a,b}` on every collection (`compare.ts:49-64`) and REST/transport/controller inherit it; the UI checkboxes are theater (`compareIds.slice(-1)` evicts the oldest). **It cannot be composed from N−1 pairwise calls**: each pair's `plyOffset` is relative to that pair's own fork (`compare.ts:198-211`), so branches forked at different nodes would land on different axes and align unrelated plies. PGN export is already N-way; the UI just never passes branch ids | `planning/breadth/review-branching.md`, Q9 |
| Viewport-contained desktop app shell | ✅ shipped (program #1) — `docs/app-shell.md`. Origin: owner walkthrough 2026-08-11 ("the drill scrolls like a document"). **Residuals belong to the foundation edge, not to a later item:** `/settings` contains no form control at all (only `<dl>`/`<ul>`), so the opponent/feedback/engines/accessibility IA row is nominal; `phase` is never projected to the client; the drill-address grammar has no route | Q9, `02-product-shape.md`, `planning/breadth/live-and-platform.md` |

## Untracked-until-now gaps (💡 batch, 2026-08-11 — surfaced by the "what else is open?" sweep)

| Idea | Take | Home |
|---|---|---|
| Skill/progress model + return loop | The product has no answer to "what does it believe you know?" or "why open it on Tuesday?" — per-concept mastery tracking, the varied-repetition scheduler's *trigger*, and progress display. "Voluntary return to the same concept" is a success metric with no designed mechanism behind it. For a solo OSS tool this decides shelfware vs habit | 💡 → needs a design pass; feeds Q1b |
| Time-pressure dimension | A top-3 loss cause at 1000–2000 (clock panic, time-trouble blunders) and the brief only gestures at "relevant clock or move budget." Are drills ever timed? Is there a time-scramble Outcome Drill variant? Undesigned | 💡 | `01-training-model.md` | **Extended 2026-08-15 (owner):** *"imagine time controls… another layer in campaign mode you can build out in a run or maybe play against with abilities."* Time stops being only a realism dimension and becomes a **campaign resource** — something you spend, extend, or have taken from you, and something a boss can hold as an ability. Note the honesty constraint that already applies: a clock changes what assistance can mean (you cannot deliberate under a ladder you have no time to read), so time interacts with the inventory axis, not just with difficulty.
| Pack interop: import Lichess studies / existing repertoires as pack seeds | 📐 scheduled under program #6. **K10 framing SUPERSEDED 2026-08-15** by `design/research/pack-authoring-cost.md` (33 packs, nine waves): the single 105-min / 43%-friction data point below was pack A alone; corpus-wide cost is **43.5 min/pack at 11.6% friction**, under the threshold. `owner-review` logged 0 for its whole life before retirement, so the earlier "no K10 verdict supportable" clause is discharged — import is still the long-tail lever, now with a measured baseline to beat. *Historical record follows:* measured authoring cost is 105 min, 45 of it tooling friction (43%, past the ~25% threshold that fires the build-tooling rule) — but all 45 minutes were *playtest/run-assembly* friction, which import does not touch. Import attacks the other 55% (research + encoding), so it is the lever for the long tail (`04-content-architecture.md` §8 order item 5), not the first one. The lever that has actually fired is a **pack playtest harness** (server-derived run id/policyConfig/seed). And `owner-review` is still 0 — the clock `content-era/plan.md` §1b calls decisive — so no K10 verdict is supportable in either direction yet. Still the community on-ramp for contributed content | Q7, `planning/breadth/create-and-return.md` |
| Open pack format as the ecosystem contribution | The value-to-chess-ecosystem may be the openly-specified drill-pack format + runtime (what PGN/EPD did for games), not our app's user count. Reframes "low expected usage" from weakness to irrelevance: others embed/extend the format. Affects Q2 content-rights axis and the novelty story | 💡 | Q2, `00-thesis.md` candidate amendment |
| Small-n evaluation methodology | The validation design (H1–H4, C2–C4) assumes user cohorts we will not have ("I don't expect much usage"). Honest alternatives: n-of-1 self-experiments with preregistered protocols, coach expert review as the primary quality gate, delayed self-testing. Without this, Q1c/C2–C4 are unfalsifiable theater | 💡 → must be settled before E-gate thresholds are preregistered | `gates.md`, Q1c |

## Depth beyond the minimal-real version (the surface itself is scheduled)

These are not postponed features. In each case the *surface* is scheduled under
a program item above, and the row records how much depth the minimal-real
version carries — the boundary `03-product-breadth.md` already fixes when it
says native matchmaking "can deepen later without erasing the surface".
Deepening one of these never re-opens whether the surface exists.

| Topic | Minimal-real scope | Home |
|---|---|---|
| Position Arena native matchmaking/clocks/moderation | Program #8 ships the external invitation + PGN-return handoff as a real workflow. Native matchmaking adds depth to a surface that already works | `arch/rfcs/RFC-0007` sketch, `arch/11`, `arch/schemas/position_arena.schema.json` |
| Corpus ingestion beyond Stage 0 (streamed months, historical slices) | Program #6 ships the explorer-API/curated-PGN path and one mined unpublished candidate through the real pipeline. Bulk volume is throughput on a working pipeline, and bulk-ingestion-*first* stays rejected | `arch/08`, `arch/13` |

## Provisional decisions (the archive ADRs — held, with revisit triggers)

Not accepted RFC-tier decisions; they predate validation. Treated as standing defaults
until their trigger fires or an owner ruling changes them.

| ADR | Decision | Revisit trigger |
|---|---|---|
| ADR-0001 | Curated-first content: **authored** packs before automatic lesson generation | ⚠️ **superseded in part 2026-08-13 (owner ruling): the *reviewed* half is struck.** There is no review workflow; packs ship from the repo with honest provenance. Curated-first survives as authored-not-generated, which is the half ADR-0005 also protects. Revisit trigger for the surviving half: C6 met and mining quality proven |
| ADR-0002 | Stockfish is judge, not default opponent | H5 fails in *both* directions (weakened SF also beats Maia/corpus on believability) |
| ADR-0003 | Personal history optional; core drills work without imports | Standing — protects against the v1 identity error |
| ADR-0004 | Local-first modular monolith + workers | 🔔 **TRIGGER FIRED 2026-08-12** — owner ruled hosted multi-user deployment. Needs an explicit re-decision; a fired trigger is not itself a reversal, and nothing yet says the monolith is the wrong architecture for a hosted deployment |
| ADR-0005 | No LLM as chess source of truth | Standing law (AGENTS.md §7); owner override only |
| ADR-0007 (new, 2026-08-14) | **Progression is never monetized.** No unlock, encounter, path, or ceremony is purchasable; no progression state is for sale — binding on whatever Q2 ever decides. The satirical pack-opening ceremony depends on this being law, not posture | Owner override only |
| ADR-0006 | Delayed feedback in Plan Drills (until checkpoint/segment end). **Two shipped amendments, both consistent with the rationale:** `attempt_end` as a third, re-closing boundary (F2), and `immediate_guard` as a pack-declared post-commit exception for the on-ramp (2026-08-14) | H3/K4 evidence shows delayed feedback hurts rather than helps |

## Rejected (⛔ — kept for the record; see also AGENTS.md §Rejected)

| Idea | Why |
|---|---|
| v1 identity: personal game-analysis AI coach (mine games → weaknesses → episodes) | `arch/CHANGE_FROM_V1.md` — legitimate adjacent product, not this one; caused the v1 brief's comparison and kill-signal errors |
| Mandatory game import as entry point | Same source; drifts back into personalized analysis |
| Bulk-ingestion-first data strategy | `arch/08` — Stage 0 (explorer API + curated PGNs) suffices; billions of games are not a prerequisite |
| LLM-generated strategic lessons as content | ADR-0005 rationale: fluent but shallow/hallucinated coaching |
| Weakened Stockfish as default opponent | `arch/08` — samples weaker engine moves; does not model human choice |
| "Paid products exist" as a kill signal | `arch/02` — v1's calibration error; real kill criteria live in `gates.md` |
| **No instrument answers "where does this expression fire?"** | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-15 by `expression-census`.** `make expression-census` produces deterministic coverage plus three-valued satisfiability for all six host sites, played witnesses, degeneracy labels, and evaluator-fault isolation. Coverage never errors; only a sound R1–R8 refutation does. | authoring frictions, D43, D49, `docs/expression-census.md` |
| **Census measured: 43 of 64 in-shape signatures fire zero times in-shape** | 📊 2026-08-15, measured with the shipped evaluator over 43 packs / 694 spine positions: **8 of 25 shape triggers fire on 0 of 694**; **28 of 96 signatures fire on 0**; and of the 64 signatures with a non-empty in-shape denominator, **43 fire zero times in-shape while 30 of those fire elsewhere in the corpus** — D43's exact pattern, thirty instances. ✅ **TRIAGED 2026-08-15 by the shipped instrument (`planning/expression-census-triage.md`) — and the answer is the good one: ZERO genuinely dead expressions.** All 36 zero-firing subjects are coverage facts or unknowns; **1** is correct-but-uncovered with a witness, **35** are `unknown`. Three independent negative tests: no refutation rule fires anywhere; the refutation arm run over `all[trigger, signature]` for all 96 signatures returns **21 satisfiable, 75 unknown, 0 unsatisfiable**, so no signature contradicts its own shape; and all 38 pack-hosted subjects fire ≥1 corpus-wide. Every headline figure reproduced **exactly**, determinism confirmed across two independent builds. The scare number was a coverage fact throughout — which is precisely what the coverage-vs-satisfiability distinction was built to establish, now demonstrated rather than argued. `FIRES_ONLY_OUTSIDE_SHAPE` is the one comparative form that does carry a defect | expression census, D43 |
| **R3 answered: census hints are true and mostly useless** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/census-hint-false-positives.md` — 634 transitions, 15,989 enumerated alternatives. Whole-surface: **6.18 observations per ply, 0.68 clearing the gate — an 89.0% false-positive rate at the observation level.** Per leaf, FP of firings: defences **95.8%**, attacks **94.1%**, irreversibility 89.1%, escape squares 86.1%, duties 53.2%, lines 41.7%. **Two methodological results outlive the numbers.** (1) **Selectivity does not predict usefulness** — Spearman ρ = **−0.143**, the cleanest leaf is the second-commonest — which invalidates the reasoning every draft so far has used to pick what to surface. (2) The **alternatives axis is load-bearing**: `slider_lines_changed` looks like the best leaf until you ask whether the moves *not* played also signal, and its lift is **1.05×**. Without that axis the analysis would have recommended shipping it live. R2's lesson, second instance | R3, `transition-primitives`, assistance ladder |
| **Q8 answered: the honesty machinery is the differentiator; the content layer is not delivered** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/feedback-versus-the-dashboard.md` — the quality question the product rests on, answered with a measured instrument rather than an argument. **YES, structurally, on three axes:** consequence before verdict (disclosure gated on commitment, `attempt_end` re-closing on the next committed move), preserved alternatives that rewind never deletes, and prose anchored to **the move you did not play** (235 deliverable deviation notes, each bound to a named non-spine move). No dashboard can do those; they are properties of the loop, not of the text. **NOT on specificity, and FORFEIT on the claim layer** — see D77/D78. The uncomfortable half is that what we *deliver* today is a census that discriminates the played move at **1.01×**, i.e. barely better than chance, while the authored material that would discriminate cannot reach anyone. **The verdict is therefore actionable rather than damning: the remedy is delivery, not authoring** | Q8, K6, R3 |
| **Plan Drill is designed, implemented, and essentially UNAUTHORED** | 🐞 **the largest half-built element in the product, measured 2026-08-15.** `01-training-model.md` names four modes; the middlegame one has **one standalone pack**. Its objective family across 37 authored packs: `reach_structure` **1** (and that one is a *mate trajectory*, not a middlegame plan), `execute_break` **4** (one browser fixture, three inside trajectories — leaving `carlsbad-minority-attack` as the only standalone), `prevent_opponent_plan` **0**, `transition_to_endgame` **0**, `preserve_plan_window` **0**. **And it compounds with the measurement gap:** R4 and R9 jointly establish the middlegame has **no difficulty oracle of either kind**. So the one phase we cannot measure is also the one phase we have not authored — the product's least-evidenced surface by both tests at once. Line Drill (20 packs), Outcome Drill (17) and Trajectory (4) all have real content; Plan Drill has one | `01` §modes, R4, R9, content scale |
| **The campaign has 1 build and 17 run states — and the fix costs zero authoring** | 📊 **2026-08-15, `design/research/campaign-effect-vocabulary.md` — the measured answer to the owner's depth worry.** **Builds over the nine `AssistanceConfig` axes: 1.** All nine are **monotone**, so the lattice has a single maximal element; 2,304 nominal states collapse to 64 content-bearing, the honesty gate contributes 2 vectors, and *a deck requires opportunity cost that monotone axes do not have*. **Distinct run states today: 17** — 37 packs collapse to 17 encounter tuples, two tuples cover 17 packs, and **every middle act is `carlsbad-minority-attack`**. **The fix that beats content, lenses and an economy:** a per-lens loadout with a slot budget over the **34 attested lenses** → **278,256** five-slot builds, zero authoring minutes, no honesty rule touched. **And the opportunity cost does not need inventing — it is already measured:** all-on is the *unreadable* state (median 58 observations/position; compare strip 1.01× lift), so a slot budget is the product **refusing to print noise** rather than withholding a reward. Second-cheapest: **9 middlegame packs ≈ 9.75 agent-hours**, the minimum that makes a phase-shaped map possible at all. For scale: Spire parity is **56 hours, 64% of it middlegame** — the phase with no oracle and the highest cost per pack | campaign, D78, Plan Drill |
| **Capability-suppressing bosses make the assistance lattice non-monotone** | 💡 **2026-08-15, `design/research/roguelike-run-design.md` — the highest-value campaign mechanism and nobody in the repo had proposed it.** Balatro's boss-blind shape: an encounter that *removes a capability* for its duration. **Law-8-legal by construction** — it speaks about the learner's *information*, never about chess — and it is the thing that breaks the monotone lattice that reduced the deck to one build. The loadout gives choices; the suppressor gives those choices **consequences**. Zero authoring minutes | campaign, D78 |
| **A run that cannot be lost is a playlist — owner ruling wanted** | 💡 2026-08-15: **no resource refusal exists anywhere in the runtime**, and no loadout mechanism creates one, so the campaign as designed has **no failure state**. Proposed resolution: **price *declaring done*, not retrying** — rewind stays free inside an encounter and the *submitted* attempt decides the run. That yields a failure state while leaving *experimentation without cost* (`00-thesis.md`) untouched, which every scarcity proposal so far violated. Needs a ruling before the map is designed | campaign, `06` §2c, thesis |
| **The escalation is legibility, not power** | 📊 2026-08-15 (`design/research/roguelike-run-design.md`) — recorded as design-tier framing because it decides what may be copied. A lens changes what you can *see*; a position does not care what you know, so **the power curve is flat by construction** and every compounding device is on the refused list. Because marginal value goes **negative** past D78's noise floor, the optimisation is **the smallest sufficient set, not the strongest** — which is **Into the Breach's** shape, not Spire's. Take ITB as the structural model, Balatro as the boss model, Spire for the map and draft only | campaign, D78 |
| **Vocabulary verdict: content CAN scale, after two packs of work** | ✅ **2026-08-15, `design/research/authoring-vocabulary-completeness.md` — the answer to the owner's lock-the-primitives-first sequencing.** The **format is not the bottleneck**: `pack-check` passes 37/37 with zero warnings, zero unsatisfiable expressions, and no vocabulary in flight. **But the two things that would cost most to re-author are not missing from the vocabulary — they are shipped, executable, and never once used**: the tempo layer (`04` §2d *requires* a timing window per opening root; **0 of 20** opening packs, 0 of 145 checkpoints, all 7 verdicts with zero users, while `tempo.ts` now consumes every field) and the engine-condition surface (D87). **Good news worth banking, and it validates the deprecate-and-replace loop:** shape `signature: null` is **21/117 (17.9%)**, not the 73% an earlier dossier recorded; `piece_reach_count`'s 143 existence-encoding leaves are now **0**; only **3 of 18** structural feature kinds are unused anywhere, down from 7 of 15. Fourteen not-expressible-and-unclaimed rows remain, five of which touch how a pack grades or explains | content scale, tempo, D87–D90 |
| **Two of four outcome types have zero real content** | 🐞 2026-08-15: `save` is authored **0** times and the only `resist` pack is **`outcome-resist.browser.json`, a test fixture**. Both are defined in `01-training-model.md` and both were shown by `resistance-spectrum` to be undrillable without opponent modes that do not exist — an opponent that never errs makes *save* unwinnable by construction, and one that simplifies happily makes *resist* meaningless. So the content absence is a **consequence**, not an oversight, and it closes only when the opponent side does | resistance spectrum, `01` §Outcome types |
| **Four declared vocabularies have zero content usage** | 🐞 measured 2026-08-15 across 37 authored packs: **`timingWindows` 0** (the known E3 blocker — `tempo-vocabulary` shipped its evaluator today, so this is now *expected-but-watch*), **`variantOf` 0** (shipped today by `authoring-frictions`, same status), **`prediction` 0** and **`reasoningKeyPoint` 0** — the last two are **older and have no in-flight RFC**, which makes them the genuine unknowns. `stated_reasoning` is declared by 1 pack and used by **0 of 145 checkpoints** (Q8). The standing pattern this repo keeps re-learning: **a shipped mechanism with no content is indistinguishable from a mechanism that does not exist** | Q8, predicate waves |
| **A firing rate measured against the wrong population is a definitional artefact** | 🐞 2026-08-15, and it corrects our own celebrated instrument. `live-marker-quality` admitted `last_of_role` partly on its **"0.0% of quiet alternatives"** — but `last_of_role` is **capture-only** and R3's quiet population is **non-capture by definition**, so that 0.0% is arithmetic, not evidence. The real numbers are **0.2% of all alternatives and 9.9% co-signal on n = 17**. Generalised as a rule rather than a special case: **a structurally-zero denominator makes lift *undefined*, not infinite** — otherwise any capture-gated or check-gated marker clears the very axis that killed `slider_lines_changed`, by construction and without the axis ever being applied. Third instance of the same family, after selectivity-does-not-predict-usefulness (R3) and compare-on-a-spine-of-endorsed-moves: **the population you measure against decides the answer before the instrument does** | R3, live-marker quality |
| **A corroboration can be a selection effect** | 🐞 2026-08-15, the methodology lesson behind D64 and the reason a wrong number was *believed*. The deferral did not merely mis-measure — it **corroborated** itself by observing that the timestamps looked like *"sequential live-fetch clusters ~32 ms apart."* That observation is **true of exactly the 206 entries it sampled and false of the 135 it missed**: real fetches cluster at a **30 ms** median gap, manufactured ones at **2,232,578 ms**, scattered by a modulo. The corroborating evidence was drawn from the population the error had already excluded. **Fourth instance of one family today** — after selectivity-not-predicting-usefulness, a mistake-detector measured on endorsed moves, and a capture-gated marker scored against a non-capture denominator. The rule now has four attestations: ***the population you measure against decides the answer before the instrument does*** — and a corroboration drawn from the same population corroborates nothing | D64, R3, live-marker quality |
| **A measurement can smuggle a verdict — the threshold must sit off the optimality boundary** | 🐞 **2026-08-15, and it sharpens law 8 itself.** `engine-leverage` drew the honest line as *a scalar about a position is a measurement (`cp`, `wdl`, `dtz`, policy); the instrument's choice among moves is a verdict (`bestmove`, MultiPV rank, `bestline`)* — and its cross-review broke it with one condition. Inside a category-preserved endgame **|dtz| decreases under optimal play**, so `tablebase_dtz_regression` at `byAtLeast: 1` means *"you left the shortest conversion path"* — which **is** `bestmove`, the arm the rule refuses by name, reached through a scalar the same clause calls a measurement. **The general form, now the rule: the subject being a measurement is NOT sufficient — the threshold must sit off the instrument's optimality boundary.** Only `engine_eval_swing` demonstrably does (its shipped `minimum: 50` sits far off; the DTZ floor was 1). Every future engine-referencing condition must clear this, not merely name a scalar | law 8, ADR-0005, engine leverage |
| **The advertised range was the interpolation domain, not a training claim** | 📊 2026-08-15, R10 (`design/research/maia-band-calibrated-range.md`) — the finding that explains why the engine's own advertisement was useless. `interpolate_elo` clamps to `[0,5000]` and returns a **linear blend of exactly two learned vectors**, so `min 0 max 5000` describes the *interpolation domain*; the artifact holds no structure recording where training data lay. **Two edges, and they fail for different reasons.** The **high** edge ≈2400–2600 fails on *ordering*, and the shape is striking: the trajectory **doubles back**, so bands 0 and 5000 are closer to each other than either is to 2500 (TV 0.409 vs 0.702/0.787). The **low** edge ≈800–1000 fails on *readability* — median listed mass falls to 0.833 at band 0, so **the contract fails before the model does**. **Methodology lesson worth keeping:** the definition claude proposed (*is the distribution distinguishable between adjacent bands?*) holds over the **entire** advertised range and fails **totally** outside it — it would have found nothing. The edges only appear under *ordering* and *readability*, neither of which claude thought to ask for | R10, engine request contract |
| **Overload is what bad moves create** | 💡 2026-08-15, the sharpest single sentence R3 produced and it generalises past the leaf: a primitive measured on an authored spine is measured on **endorsed moves**, so anything that fires on mistakes will look *rarer* on a spine than on the alternatives — `defended_duty_acquired` signals on 2.1% of played moves vs 3.4% of quiet alternatives, **0.61×**. Any future "is this primitive informative" measurement must compare against alternatives, never against spine frequency alone, or it will systematically prefer the wrong instruments | R3, predicate waves |
| **The coverage/satisfiability conflation already shipped, twice** | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-15 by `expression-census`.** Both predicate-wave-3 fixtures now use satisfiable-but-absent expressions for pack-local coverage refusals; `piece_count(king) equal 0` is pinned separately as the R2 unsatisfiable case. | expression census |
| **Two of six expression host sites are never linted** | 🐞 2026-08-15: `structuralIssuesInPack` (`pack-validation.ts:280-291`) matches only `kind: "structural_feature"` / `type: "structuralFeature"`, so `windowClosing.kind: "position"` and `reasoningKeyPoint.ground.kind: "structural"` **never reach `structuralIssues`** although both are evaluated at runtime. Zero instances in content today, so latent rather than live — and latent is how D32 started | expression census, validator integrity |
| **The intermediate-node consequence already ships, and it is called `degraded`** | 📊 **2026-08-15, `design/research/campaign-intermediate-consequence.md` — the answer to the owner's "all the intermediate is without consequence" worry, found in shipped code rather than invented.** `ObjectiveState` has **6** values and only **3** are absorbing (`trajectory.ts:6`), so the runtime already distinguishes *this node went badly and play continues* from *this node ended things*. `degraded` is **one-way by validator rule** (`OBJECTIVE_DEGRADED_IS_ONE_WAY`, `pack-validation.ts:469`), **produced by authored content and not by any grader** (76 of 275 deviations carry `offObjective`, compiled at `pack-orchestrator.ts:367-385`), and **sealed forward across node boundaries** — a leg transition resets the objective to `active` *only* from `preserved`/`degraded` and stores the outgoing verdict as `sealedState` (`pack-orchestrator.ts:556-575`, `trajectory.ts:83-92`). Node-skipping is recorded too (`skippedLegIds`). **The one thing missing is a scope, not a mechanism** | campaign, `06` §5, M2 |
| **The intermediate/boss split is a lint rule, and the act shape is already authored** | 📊 2026-08-15, same dossier: `THEORY_ABSORBING_UNSUPPORTED` (`pack-validation.ts:457`) means **`follow_theory` cannot end a run — 17 of 37 packs**, while `win`/`hold` (13 packs) can end one only from an outcome condition (`:463`). So the catalogue is *already* partitioned into cannot-be-lost and can-be-lost, and the partition is nearly `06` §5's intermediate/boss split. **All three canonical trajectory packs have exactly 3 legs and in all three only the last leg carries an absorbing objective** — `06` §5's "2 encounters + 1 act boss" is the shape three authors independently wrote | campaign, `06` §5, trajectory legs |
| **Which path does a node remember? — owner ruling wanted** | 💡 2026-08-15, and it is the *whole* remaining design space for intermediate consequence. Every seal is **path-scoped** (`objectiveState` is per node, `types.ts:102`; `historyFrom` walks the current path), so a rewind to a clean line erases it — which is the thesis working, not a bug. Three answers with different costs: **(a) the branch you're standing on when you leave** — no new verb, but the node then prices *diligence* (did you go back and fix it), a real and thin stake; **(b) the branch you submit** — the same verb `06` §5 already needs for the boss, so **one ruling buys both holes**, and `reveal` is close enough to extend rather than invent; **(c) the worst branch you produced** — prices retrying, contradicts `00-thesis.md:76-79`, listed only so it is refused on purpose | campaign, `06` §5, thesis, R6/R7 |
| **`DESIGN-GAP:` `06` §5 conflates "no resource refusal" with "no failure state"** | 🐞 2026-08-15: the first clause is **correct** — `SIMULATE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` is declared (`errors.ts:42`) and never thrown, no table has a balance/quota column, and the only refusal blocking a solo learner from rewinding is `MATCH_LIVE`. But the conclusion *"the campaign as designed has no failure state"* **does not follow**: the absorbing objective states are a failure state, and `degraded` is a validated non-fatal one beneath it. Conflating the two is what made every proposal so far reach for invented scarcity and collide with *experimentation without cost* every time. Escalation owed to `planning/exploration/log.md` | campaign, `06` §5, thesis |
| **Carried board state — the trajectory pack IS an act** | 💡 2026-08-15 (M1): the strongest and most chess-native intermediate consequence is **the position handed to the next node** — self-evident, needs no vocabulary, no grading, no number, so law 8 is satisfied vacuously. It is Into the Breach's Power Grid (5 starting, max 7, lost per destroyed building, *"persists between Islands"*) and it already ships as trajectory legs. **What kills it: content, and it kills the map** — a positionally-chained run must be authored as a chain (3 such packs today, 1 middlegame pack, 65.0 min/pack) and is linear by construction, spending the 278,256 loadout builds to buy narrative continuity. Recommendation: **the thing the design grows into, not the thing it ships first** | campaign, ITB, `06` §5 |
| **Unspent reveals convert forward — Balatro's cash-out** | 💡 2026-08-15 (M3): Balatro pays *"$1 for each remaining hand"* at round end, so unspent information-gathering becomes economy — it prices **looking, not retrying**, which is the one shape the thesis permits. Here the `attempt_end` window already is that consumable (`feedback.ts:22-30`); the new half is **carrying the unspent remainder to the next node**, so a node you read cleanly funds the boss. **The strike against it: D78 licenses the *slot* budget because all-on was measured unreadable, and licenses nothing about reveals** — the reveals-per-node number would be invented, and a learner arriving at the boss empty has been punished for finding a node hard. R6/R7 gate it, and the cheapest pre-test is one owner run counting reveals | campaign, R6, R7, D78 |
| **Boss selected by census, not adapted — the Nemesis shape** | 💡 2026-08-15 (M7): Shadow of Mordor's orcs *remember*, and the system manufactures no stat — it is pure **selection and framing**. Here: the boss node is chosen from its candidate pool by a census of the structures the intermediate nodes actually produced. **Selection is not grading, so law 8 holds.** The detector already exists and gates nothing — `shapeRecommendations` (`service.ts:758-777`) computes shapes met but never drilled over the last 50 runs, sorted and `.slice(0,10)`, and its only consequence is a sentence plus a button that navigates to `/play`. Costs content at the rank-4 rate (2 packs per node slot), and risks self-confirming curation | campaign, `shapeRecommendations`, `06` §5 |
| **A bounded reconfiguration window between nodes — the rally service park** | 💡 2026-08-15 (M8): DiRT Rally carries damage between stages and allows repairs only in a **time-boxed service park every two stages**, so you triage what you arrive with. Here: loadout changes permitted only between nodes and only N times per run. Prices neither retrying nor looking. **Weak survivor** — the budget is invented (D78 licenses loadout *size*, not *churn*), and denominating it in time rather than count turns it into the pursuit clock already refused | campaign, `06` §5 |
| **⛔ Lens fatigue — killed by a measured number, twice over** | ⛔ 2026-08-15 (M5): the XCOM/Darkest-Dungeon shape (a lens you leaned on is suppressed at the next node, i.e. Balatro's Ox applied to lenses). **Dies to standing law 5**: "most-used" is a frequency, and ρ(firing rate, usefulness) = **−0.143**, so targeting the lens you leaned on hardest is targeting *noise* — faintly negatively correlated, as likely to remove the lens that was carrying you as one that was not. Beneath that it is a hint tax: it prices asking for help, which is *experimentation without cost* read one level up from moves. **Kept because it is the mechanism the genre most obviously suggests and the one a Spire intuition will keep reaching for** | campaign, R3, ρ = −0.143 |
| **⛔ Two more intermediate-consequence mechanisms that die to law 8** | ⛔ 2026-08-15: **(M4) Hanabi's clue-token economy** — 8 tokens, spend to ask, and *"the only way to return a clock token is by discarding a card"*. The spend half is honest; the **refill** half needs the discard to be risky, and our equivalent risk is a chess judgement about an unaided move. Strip the risk and the economy is decorative. **(M6) Dishonored's Chaos drift** — high chaos is not harder, it is a *less legible world*, which is the closest commercial analogue to "legibility, not power". Dies on the accumulator: Dishonored counts bodies, and our only mechanical accumulators are censuses measured at **89.0% FP** and **1.01× lift**, so the drift would drift on noise | campaign, law 8, R3, D78 |
| **No run-level verdict exists — `attempts` is per branch** | 🐞 2026-08-15: `attempts` is keyed `(run_id, branch_id)` and `projectAttempts` emits one row per branch (`progress.ts:91`), so a run with four retry branches yields four independent verdicts and **no roll-up**; `verdict` is additionally forced to `"open"` unless the pack has objective rules (`:127`, `:90`). *"Did this run succeed"* is computed nowhere. It is the **precondition for every campaign proposal** and the smallest new code any of them needs | campaign, progress, `06` §5 |
| **The compare surface bypasses the honesty gate** | 🐞 2026-08-15, found while measuring: `comparisonStrips` calls `pivotalMarkers` directly (`compare-strips.ts:38`), **not** `liveMarkers`, so the permission filter at `pivotal.ts:96` never runs; `CompareView.svelte` imports no assistance symbol and `:119-121` renders the entire unfiltered `structuralReading` of the leaf node. `06` §3 law 1 makes the honesty gate the **outer** gate — on the surface with the most disclosure it is currently not a gate at all, and any campaign inner gate built on top inherits the hole | honesty gate, `06` §3 law 1, compare |
| **D277 📊 The conjunction hypothesis is refuted — loadouts are additive, not synergistic** | 📊 **2026-08-16, `design/research/conjunction-hypothesis.md` (R11) — the measured answer to the `[M]` claim the whole deck argument rested on.** Over **721 spine transitions / 47 packs** and **19,099 alternatives** on the unmodified R3 harness: best single leaf:direction **69.4%** precision / **12.64×** axis-D lift; best of **55 conjunctions** **35.7%** / **2.73×**; only **7 of 55** produce enough co-signalling witnesses to measure discrimination at all, **median lift 0.66×** (worse than a random quiet move), **0 of 7** beat even their own two components on both axes, **0** clear the global bars. **The arithmetic:** given both leaves fired the two *signals* are near-independent (observed precision a median **1.18×** the independence prediction), so **the false positives multiply at exactly the rate the specificity does** — §4 assumed one and got both. Firing is not independent either (median lift **1.136**, max **5.26×**, φ 0.456): six leaves computed off one attack map are six views of one object. **Consequence, pre-named by `roguelike-run-design.md` §3 rank 6:** do not ship synergy discovery, combination-payoff unlocks, or any deck framing that promises the value is in the intersection. The lens pool stays; the intersection does not | campaign, R3, R11, `06` §1/§3 |
| **D278 📊 A lens read BEFORE the move is discrimination-inert by construction** | 📊 **2026-08-16, same dossier §7a — analytic, not statistical, and the finding with the widest reach.** A predicate evaluated on the parent position takes the **same value for the played move and for every alternative from that position**, so conjoining a transition primitive with it cannot change *which move* is singled out — only *at which positions the surface speaks*. Measured over **161** leaf ∧ shipped-structural-lens combinations: precision moves a median **+0.1 pp**, and the large gains are population effects (`slider_lines_changed:closed ∧ phase:endgame` +42.3 pp) with the within-position also-signalling share **unchanged at 51.7%**. **This reclassifies three shipped/proposed things as *selectors* rather than synergies**: all three of `campaign-effect-vocabulary.md` §4's worked triples, the shape library's 96 signed signatures, and the boss-by-census mechanism (the *Boss selected by census, not adapted — the Nemesis shape* row). Selection is genuinely useful and is what targeting means; it is not "the value is in the intersection". **Rule for the assistance ladder: when a lens is read decides what it can do** | R11, assistance ladder, shape library, campaign |
| **D279 💡 Post-move lenses are the one arm that discriminates, and nobody proposed them** | 💡 **2026-08-16, `conjunction-hypothesis.md` §7b.** Reading the *same* shipped structural lenses on the position **after** the move makes them vary across alternatives, so they can discriminate: **max 8.77×** (`defended_duties_changed:released ∧ after:isolated_pawn`), **25 of 72** combinations beating their own leaf, and `escape_squares_changed:gained` — a leaf at **0.60×**, which R3 would condemn — reaching **6.54×** when the child position has a passed pawn. Honest caveats in the dossier: median is still **0.79×**, witness counts are 12–30, and "passed pawn after a pawn move" is partly a restatement of the move (axis T's own concern). **Worth an instrument, not worth shipping on these numbers.** No surface in the tree conjoins a transition primitive with a post-move structural reading today | R11, B4, `05` §5a, structural reading |
| **D280 💡 `move_irreversibility(last_of_role)` is the most discriminating primitive measured — and the shipped marker renders the other three subkinds** | 💡 **2026-08-16, `conjunction-hypothesis.md` §5b.** Against **all 19,099 alternatives** it lifts **12.64×** (2.91% played vs 0.23%) — the highest discrimination number anywhere in the R3/R11 family, five times the best conjunction. R3 could only record *"— (no quiet firings)"* because the subkind requires a capture and is therefore excluded by the quiet filter by construction; R11 bounds it (rule of three) and measures it against the full population. This is the number `census-hint-false-positives.md` §7c's proposed narrowing was missing, and it points the same way: **the value is in a rare single primitive, not an intersection.** The shipped live marker still admits only `last_of_role` (`pivotal.ts:69-73`) — correct — while `renderPivotalMarker` and the on-request census carry `castled`/`pawn_break`/`clock_zeroed`, which restate the move | R11, R3 §7c, `transition-primitives`, `05` §5a |
| **D281 📊 A build of three or more transition primitives is arithmetically empty** | 📊 **2026-08-16, `conjunction-hypothesis.md` §6.** Of all possible triples of the eleven leaf:directions, **115** have all three *firing* ≥10 times, but only **38 ever produce three simultaneous signals anywhere in the corpus**, the most frequent does so **6 times in 721 transitions**, and **none reaches 10**. §4's own mechanism arriving at its destination: specificity really does multiply, and at the third factor there is nothing left to be specific about. **Design consequence: a slot budget cannot be denominated in transition primitives.** Position lenses co-occur freely and are the only pool a multi-slot loadout can come from — which is what D78's noise-floor argument already implied and now has a second reason | campaign, R11, D78, slot budget |
| **D282 📊 R3's middlegame limit is discharged, and the census works best where no oracle exists** | 📊 **2026-08-16, `conjunction-hypothesis.md` §8a.** R3 stated as limit 3 that *"the corpus's middlegame sample is 18 plies, which is far too small to carry a rate"*. It is now **105 plies from 11 packs** and carries one: the middlegame is the corpus's **least** false-positive phase (**86.0%** vs 91.4% opening, 89.3% endgame) and the phase where the most leaves signal (**65.7%**). Worth recording plainly because it is the phase R4 and R9 jointly proved has **neither** an engine nor a human-outcome oracle — the census layer is at its best exactly where the measured layers are at their worst, and still at 86% false positives. Also: the endgame prints **2.60** observations per ply against the opening's **8.74**, so any headline observation-volume figure is a property of the corpus's phase mix, not of the census | R11, R3, R4, R9, `06` §2a difficulty-availability |
| **D283 💡 The interesting Maia question is per-move policy mass, not `human_divergence`** | 💡 **2026-08-16, `conjunction-hypothesis.md` §9.** §4's Triple 3 (`move_irreversibility(pawn_break)` ∧ `human_divergence`) is the strongest form of the conjunction hypothesis and the only one with a genuinely independent rung-3 half. It is cheap to probe (**721** parent probes, not 19,099 — R5 measured the policy vector bit-identical across 20 repeats) **but §7a predicts its outcome before the first probe**: `human_divergence` as shipped classifies the *decision point* — no candidate >0.5, ≥3 candidates ≥0.15 (`pivotal.ts:30-40`) — so it is a pre-move lens and can only select where to speak. The discriminating predicate would be **per-move policy mass** (*"the move you played is one the band rarely chooses"*), which is a different object and already ships as `humanConcessionMass` with no reader-facing consumer. **That is the successor question; it is not R11** | R11, R5, Maia, rung 3, `humanConcessionMass` |
| **D284 🐞 R11 confirms [[D257]] from a second direction: three harnesses re-derive "the corpus" independently** | 🐞 **2026-08-16.** [[D257]] recorded that *"47 authored packs"* is computed by no code. R11 hit the same thing from the research tier: `content/drafts/` holds **149** JSON documents — **47** packs, **6** `.browser.json` fixtures and **96** `.evidence`/`.job`/`.sources` sidecars `[V]` — and the exclusion is performed by a **regex literal duplicated in at least three places** with three different spellings (`tools/r1r2-primitives-harness/corpus.ts:27`, `apps/server/src/opening-evidence.test.ts:31`, and `apps/server/src/expression-census.ts:239` which alone names the category, as `fixturePacks`). Every R1–R11 dossier's corpus count therefore depends on which harness wrote it. The fix [[D257]] proposes (export the exclusion as a shared predicate) would also make every research corpus figure a single derived number instead of eleven independent re-derivations | corpus definition, D257, harness family |
| **D285 💡 An infinite lift is what a keying bug looks like** | 💡 **2026-08-16, `conjunction-hypothesis.md` §10.6 — recorded because the trap is reusable, not because the bug mattered.** The first R11 run keyed conjunctions **alphabetically** on the alternative population and **by leaf order** on the spine population, so seven pairs found a zero denominator and reported **infinite** axis-D lift. That is exactly the shape of result R11 was hoping for, and it would have been the headline. Two cheap guards, both now in the harness: canonicalise combination keys through one function, and **never render an infinite ratio** — substitute the rule-of-three upper bound (3/N) so a zero denominator prints as a *bound* and stays visibly suspicious. Applies to any future pass that divides one population's rate by another's | research method, harness family, R11 |
| **D286 🐞 R11 had no ledger row and no queue row — the only campaign research question with neither** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, found while locating the question.** R11 is stated in `design/research/campaign-effect-vocabulary.md` §7, elaborated in §4, widened in `roguelike-run-design.md` §3/§7, and named in `planning/work-register.md:139` — and grep returns **zero** hits for `R11` in `planning/exploration/plan.md`, `design/BACKLOG.md` and `planning/campaign-research-queue.md` `[V]`. R1–R9 all have queue rows. Law 4 says an idea missing from the ledger is a process bug, and the specific cost here was real: the question's *statement* had to be reconstructed from two dossiers that ask subtly different questions (§2b of the R11 dossier records the drift and which reading was tested). **Registered by this pass** — R11 added to `planning/campaign-research-queue.md` §Now and to `planning/work-register.md` §5. The general form: a research question born inside a dossier does not get registered, because dossiers are not registers | process, law 4, campaign research queue |
| **D317 💡 The coaching/cheating line is a THIRD axis: how much of the live decision an item resolves** | 💡 **2026-08-16, `design/research/coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` — the encodable criterion.** Rung is refuted as the criterion (the owner's win condition *requires* rung 5 authored counter-theory, while rung-0 all-on is measured unreadable at 1.01× lift) and timing is refuted as *sufficient* (§D319). What survives is **distance from the answer**, four syntactic values read off what an item *says*: **`kind`** (names the kind of position), **`fact`** (a fact about this position, no ordering), **`ranking`** (orders this position's options), **`move`** (names the move). **Criterion: an item is cheating iff `distance === "move"` while the learner still has a committing decision that depends on it; `kind`/`fact` are coaching at any time; `ranking` is the contested band.** It is **orthogonal to both shipped axes** — `permittedAssistance` selects sources by role and disclosure, `AssistanceConfig` selects preference; neither can carry a property of a rendered *item*. Deliberately syntactic ("does it name a specific legal move here"), never "does it agree with the engine", so `05` §3 clause 2 is satisfied. **It is D113 discovered from the honesty side**: the owner's hint-distance idea and the campaign's loadout currency are the same object. Corroboration nobody designed: all four of `05` §5a's honest forward detectors are residue-preserving, and `pivotal.ts:78` already gates `human_divergence` markers separately from the split itself | D113, `05` §3/§3b/§5a, campaign, `06` §1 |
| **D318 🐞 `capabilities.ts:96` refuses the top of the hint ladder product-wide, on a reason narrower than its scope** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier.** The shipped capability register publishes **36 dispositions — 14 `reached`, 15 `refused`, 6 `unmeasured`, 1 `impossible`** — and its recurring predicate is *recording vs grading*: Maia per-move `score cp` is `reached` *"Recorded without grading"*, explorer per-move W/D/L is `reached` *"attached to each move without grading"*, and **`Stockfish · bestmove / MultiPV rank / bestline` is `refused`, reason "Move verdicts are not condition measurements"**. Read as a distance register that is **`ranking` reached, `move` refused, permanently**. Two consequences: (1) **the campaign cannot ever hand a learner the move as a reward, even after the game** — D113's ladder tops out at exactly the refused value, and **0 of 764 committed evidence records are `bestline`** although the plumbing is end-to-end; (2) the stated reason answers whether a `bestmove` may **fire a condition** (`05` §3 clause 1) and is being used to refuse **display**, a different question the disclosure model already answers. Either widen the reason deliberately or split the disposition — this is the owner question in the dossier | D113, `05` §3, `06` §5, campaign |
| **D319 🐞 Timing is necessary and NOT sufficient — and the shipped code is the proof** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier.** `feedbackDeliveryOpen` re-closes the reveal window on `move.committed` (`packages/runtime/src/feedback.ts:22-30`). If "post-commit" were the criterion that line would be unnecessary; it exists because **post-commit at move *n* is pre-commit for move *n+1***. **Timing's sufficiency is a function of the encounter's ply horizon, and the runtime already splits on exactly that axis**: `events.ts:154-159` *forces* bounded sessions (packs, with an authored `plyHorizon`) onto authored boundaries and *forces* unbounded ones (Just Play, imported — the owner's "proper game against a bot") onto the closable `attempt_end` window. Corollary found while measuring: `PositionOpponentPolicy.mode` is `"human_common" \| "strong_engine"` only (`types.ts:61-62`) and `events.ts:159-161` refuses `theory_strict`/`perfect_tablebase` outside packs — so **band-conditioned Maia is the only honest opponent an unbounded game has, and both of `06` §5's Act I and Act III bosses require a pack by type** | `05` §3a-i, ADR-0006, `06` §5, campaign |
| **D320 📊 The product measures neither "played well" NOR "run succeeded" — it measures a third thing, and that is the honest ceiling** | 📊 **2026-08-16, same dossier, code census.** Eight sites compare a learner move to anything; **exactly one** emits a per-move verdict and it is an authored table lookup (`lineMembership` → `on_line \| classified_deviation \| unknown`, `line.ts:121-165`, class copied verbatim from the pack at `:156-159`). The one **computed** judgement of learner behaviour is `TempoVerdict` (timing against an *authored* budget, `tempo.ts:14-23,252-263`) and it has **zero authored users**. Prediction grading was deleted in schema 0.8→0.9 (`7d461e1`): `grading{source, topK, minMass}` removed, replaced by a threshold-free `prediction.recorded` that **nothing reads**, and **0 packs author a `prediction` interaction**. **No learner number exists anywhere** (`accuracy`/`mastery`/`skillLevel`/`learnerElo`/`percentile`/`streak`/`xp` = 0 hits; every `targetElo` is an *opponent* band), and the refusal ships in three lines of copy — *"never a mastery score"* ×2 and **"That is a grade of this attempt, not a verdict on the position"** (`outcome-presentation.ts:170`). And *"did this run succeed"* is computed nowhere — independently re-confirmed here: `attempts` PK is `(run_id, branch_id)` (`storage.ts:2525`) and `verdict` is forced to `"open"` unless the pack compiled an objective rule (`progress.ts:62-65,90,127`), matching the *"No run-level verdict exists"* row above. **What it CAN say: whether an authored or tablebase-decided objective survived the path you submitted.** So *"you'd need proper 2000 Elo skills"* is a claim about the **encounter's configuration**, never about the learner — which is the only version that can be true, and it means **the curve can be declared but never adapted**. Named price, on record: ChessMonitor's most-loved number is a manufactured FIDE-Elo estimate with proven pull | law 8, `06` §0, campaign, ChessMonitor quickpass |
| **D321 🐞 `permittedAssistance` ignores its own `sessionKind` input, and `solo` is unreachable in production** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier.** `AssistanceContext` declares three fields (`assistance.ts:21-25`) and the body reads **two**: `sessionKind` is threaded through every call site and **never read**, so `{sessionKind:"pack"}` and `{sessionKind:"position"}` are provably identical — the honesty ceiling is parameterised on **role and disclosure state only**, not on what kind of run this is, although `05` §4 names "what assistance is permitted here" as one of its four varying axes. Separately, `"solo"` exists only in `assistance.ts:24,28` and tests: the server enum is `RUN_ROLES = ["host","participant","spectator"]` (`storage.ts:32-33`) and `DrillScreen` defaults to `host` (`:102`), and since `:28` treats `solo` and `host` identically the variant is semantically inert. Recorded because the campaign's inner gate is specified to sit *inside* this outer one | `05` §4, `06` §3 law 1, honesty gate |
| **D322 🐞 The corpus's opponent band is a two-point step function while its declared learner band is continuous** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier, whole-`content/` census over the 89 pack-shaped documents.** `difficulty.minOnlineRapid`/`maxOnlineRapid` (74 of 89) covers every 100-point bin from 1400 to 2000 with **42–58 packs each** (1000: 21 · 1100: 28 · 1200: 31 · 1300: 23 · 1400: 58 · 1500: 46 · 1600: 43 · 1700: 44 · 1800: 43 · 1900: 42 · 2000: 42 · 2100: 7). `opponentPolicy.targetElo` (73 of 89) is **≤1394: 30 · 1400–1799: 4 (one a browser fixture, so 3 real) · ≥1800: 39** — 1800 alone accounts for 36. So the content says *"this pack is for a 1400–2000 player"* and puts an **1800** opponent in front of them in 36 of 45 real authored packs. Cause of the asymmetry: the seed emitter clamps to `[1100, 2000]` and says so honestly (*"an authoring convention, not a Maia capability claim"*, `sourcing/position-seeds.ts:171,231`) while hand-authored packs pick round numbers; against the engine's real `[1000, 2400]` the corpus uses **1100–1939**. **The 1000→2000 journey has a measured hole exactly where it is supposed to climb, and the band lever is measured, enforced (`TARGET_ELO_OUT_OF_RANGE`) and repaired — it is simply unused** | R10, `00` §Target player, campaign, content scale-up |
| **D323 🐞 `DESIGN-GAP:` the shipped `plyHorizon` ramp runs 11 → 8 → 24 — Act II is the SHORTEST** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier.** `authoredBoundary.plyHorizon` is declared by **47 of 89** documents, median **10**, mean **12.5**, range **2–40**; per phase **opening n=21 median 11 (2–14) · middlegame n=11 median 8 (6–10) · endgame n=13 median 24 (8–40)**. Read as `06` §5's three acts that is 11 → 8 → 24, so the act that is supposed to escalate is the shortest encounter in the corpus. The finding matters twice because **horizon is simultaneously a difficulty parameter and an honesty parameter** — a longer encounter is harder *and* has more game left for a disclosure to contaminate, which is exactly why `events.ts:154-159` forces unbounded sessions onto the re-closing window. Either the act structure or the middlegame horizons are wrong; an authoring correction is owed either way | `06` §5, D319, Plan Drill, campaign |
| **D324 🔬 R10 measured that the band moves the DISTRIBUTION; nothing has measured that it moves the RESULT** | 🔬 **2026-08-16, same dossier — the highest-value unrun experiment in the campaign cluster.** R10 states explicitly that no claim is made about play quality at any band, and the whole 1000→2000 trajectory rests on the opponent band being a *difficulty* lever rather than a *policy* lever. Cheap, law-8-clean (engine vs engine, no human graded): **Maia-vs-Maia, N ≥ 200 games per arm, bands {1000, 1400, 1800, 2200} against a fixed band-1400 reference, on R5's stratified position set. Pass = monotone score across all four arms with non-overlapping 95% CIs; fail = any adjacent pair inverting or overlapping.** If it fails, the curve has no rungs and Act II has neither an oracle nor a dial. Related standing gate: H5 and K5 both assume band-conditioned opposition behaves like graded opposition and neither has evidence | R10, H5, K5, campaign, `06` §2b |
| **D325 💡 The career curve is SUPPRESSION, not accumulation — and it runs down the distance axis, not the count axis** | 💡 **2026-08-16, same dossier — the tested form of the owner's *"more weapons early… 2000 Elo at max"*.** Starting all-on contradicts D78 directly (all-on is the *unreadable* state, so a large early loadout is a worse experience, not an easier one), so the honest ramp holds the slot **count** flat and moves the **distance**: early ≈3 lenses at `ranking` vs band 1000–1200 → mid ≈3 at `fact` vs 1400–1800 → late ≈3 at `kind` vs 1800–2200 → **boss suppressed to 0**. Each row is a smaller-sufficient-set than the last. **Full suppression is what makes *"you'd need 2000 Elo skills"* TRUE WITHOUT MEASURING THE LEARNER** (D320): a band-2000 opponent, a bounded horizon and no assistance is a complete specification, and the learner reads the result themselves. **The suppressing boss therefore carries the top of the curve exactly and no further** — and the ceiling is **per phase**: opening `theory_strict` (difficulty = how much theory you know, human-outcome measured only to ply ~20), middlegame ≤2400 Maia + authored plan (no oracle), endgame `perfect_tablebase` (**infinite** — so the act's objective must be `hold`/`save`/`convert`, never `win`). **And the on-ramp's relaxation is a HORIZON relaxation, not a distance one, which is what already ships**: 2–8-ply branches plus `immediate_guard` make post-commit *be* the boundary — measured, **24 of the 26 on-ramp candidate packs declare `immediate_guard`**, and the two that do not are exactly the two whose declared band is above 1400 | D78, `06` §5, `00` §Target player, capability-suppressing boss, campaign |
| **D326 🐞 The nine `AssistanceConfig` axes are NOT all monotone — five are, and four are each broken differently** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier, per-axis code read — corrects `06` §1 and the "all nine are monotone" clause of the campaign-effect finding.** Strictly monotone and live: **`markers`, `guided`, `humanSplit`, `corpus`, `ambient` (5)**. `boardLighting`: **collapsed top** (`sight` and `evidence` render through one branch, `DrillScreen.svelte:300`) **and a broken floor** (`SILENT_ASSISTANCE.boardLighting = "legal"` is not the axis minimum — D101). `spoken`: **not a chain** — `browser` and `provider` are siblings that exclude each other and add no propositions. `voice`: **substitutive, not additive** — `persona` replaces the deterministic sentences rather than adding to them. `arrows`: **inert** — read by no renderer (D84). The one-maximal-element argument still holds for the five that are live, so the conclusion (a deck needs opportunity cost that monotone axes lack) is unchanged; the premise is not. Separately: `guided` is `"free"` **unconditionally with no server-side gate anywhere**, is reachable only inside an opened pivotal marker (`DrillScreen.svelte:305-309`), and is **not band-shaped** despite `05` §3b's explicit "natural default for the 1000–1400 on-ramp… and it fades" | `06` §1, D84, D101, `05` §3b, campaign |
| **D287 📊 `score cp` and `wdl` are ONE Maia output, and the capability register counts them as two** | 📊 **2026-08-16, `design/research/maia-wdl-versus-human-outcome.md` §3 — and it upgrades [[D236]] from an observation to a property.** Upstream builds the triple through `_probabilities_to_permille` (largest-remainder over a softmax, `/opt/maia3/maia3/uci.py:186-193`), so it sums to **exactly 1000 by construction**, not by luck — **27,330/27,330** candidate rows over three command shapes confirm it `[V]`. And `cp_from_wdl(wdl) = win − loss` (`:208-210`) holds on **27,330/27,330** rows, so **expected score = 0.5 + cp/2000, exactly**: for any expected-score question the two fields carry one number, and the only thing `wdl` adds is the draw component. `capabilities.ts:107-109` lists `per-move wdl` (`unmeasured`) and `per-move score cp` (`reached`) as **two capabilities with two dispositions**. Also settled from source rather than by inference: the frame is the **root side to move** (`invert_wdl`, `:203-205`, applied to a value read on the child with the Elos swapped), and the read is deterministic — **799/799** rows byte-identical across three runs, two in fresh containers, extending R5's policy result to the value head | Maia, D236, capability register, cluster B |
| **D288 📊 Maia's WDL agrees with human outcomes — conditionally, and the condition is free** | 📊 **2026-08-16, same dossier §6.** Over **5,379** human-decided move pairs (R9's committed readings, R9's inherited thresholds) sign agreement is **Maia WDL 72.2%**, Maia policy 70.0%, **explorer play counts 76.8%**, Stockfish d12 84.2% — against a 50% floor and a **94.2–99.3% measured ceiling** (the ground truth's own cross-band reproducibility). So on the population as a whole the WDL is the **weakest** instrument measured and loses to a number arriving in the same HTTP response. **Split by the ratio of the two play counts it inverts**: where the counts are within 2× — the half popularity cannot answer — WDL holds **65.1%** [62.3, 67.9] while the counts fall to **54.7%** and Stockfish holds 78.9%. On R9's engine-tied pairs WDL (**64.6%**) is indistinguishable from raw counts (64.1%). **Verdict: a real, non-redundant signal on the hard half; not an upgrade on the easy half.** The condition — the count ratio — is computable before the Maia call, from the response that carries the ground truth | Maia, R9, cluster B, `06` §2a |
| **D289 📊 The WDL does NOT reach past R9's ply-20 wall, and the control rules out the alternative story** | 📊 **2026-08-16, same dossier §8 — the finding that decides the campaign question.** Pooled agreement by ply bucket: **81.2 → 75.3 → 68.8 → 61.2 → 47.9%** (16–19 is n = 73, p = **0.82** against chance) `[V]`. If deep pairs were merely noisier every instrument would decay together — **Stockfish depth 12 over the identical pairs is flat, 84.8 / 82.9 / 85.8 / 80.8 / 87.7%**, and the explorer's own counts decay only mildly. What crosses the wall is **availability, not validity**: `wdl` is emitted on **1,788/1,788** rows at ply 40+ and the within-position spread *grows* from **10.3 pp** (ply 0–9) to **35.9 pp** (ply 40+) — an instrument that never abstains and speaks more emphatically the further it gets from any check. **`design/06` §2a's three-value difficulty-availability axis is unchanged and is now measured rather than inherited; the middlegame stays authored** | Maia, R9, `06` §2a, campaign |
| **D290 🐞 Maia's WDL is a function of (position, path); the object it would be compared to is a function of (position)** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier §9.1.** Probing the same 279 positions history-conditioned (production, `--use-uci-history` is the image entrypoint) and bare-FEN gives **281 of 5,222** shared move rows with an identical `cp`, median \|Δ\| **29 cp**, p90 92, max **576** `[V]` — and `cmd_position` rebuilds the history buffer per command (`uci.py:405`, `:437`), so it is genuine conditioning, not leakage. The explorer aggregates transpositions by construction (R9 §8.2: median **3.3%**, max **93.2%** of a position's games arrive by another move order). **A quantity that moves by 29 cp depending on how the position was reached cannot be attributed to the position** — which is what any evidence row, condition or marker rendering it would be doing. The verdict is unaffected either way (bare arm 72.5% vs production 72.4%), so this is a *attribution* defect, not a measurement one | Maia, evidence attribution, transposition, cluster B |
| **D291 🔬 The band dial moves Maia's WDL without moving it TOWARD the band — [[D324]]'s question, answered on the outcome side** | 🔬 **2026-08-16, same dossier §9.5.** Between adjacent bands Maia's per-move value moves by **0.74–1.41 pp** (so the dial is not inert — R10 stands), and the correlation between *its* movement and the *human population's* movement over the same moves is **Pearson 0.021–0.044 with sign agreement 47.2–52.0%** `[V]`. The test has power: subtracting sampling variance leaves a real human band movement of **1.24–2.21 pp**. [[D324]] asks whether the band moves the RESULT and proposes a Maia-vs-Maia experiment; this is the same question asked against *real* results and the answer is that **the WDL's band response carries no information about the band's actual outcome shift**. It does not settle D324 (that arm measures played games, this one measures the value head) but it is evidence about which way D324 will come out, and it is already paid for | Maia, D324, R10, H5, K5, `06` §2b |
| **D292 🐞 Maia's per-move value is UNDER-dispersed by half and its draws are 35–40% low — the audit's spread figure reads the wrong population** | 🐞 **2026-08-16, same dossier §7.** On the moves humans actually play often enough to measure, the median within-position spread is **4.85–4.95 pp** for Maia against **9.15–9.66 pp** for the human result, with Maia sitting 0.8–1.0 pp high `[V]`. `engine-layer-capability-audit.md` recorded WDL *"spreading 0.191 median expected score across a position's listed moves"* — that is over the whole MultiPV-20 list, most of which nobody plays, and on the measurable subset the direction reverses. Any consumer built on these numbers would systematically **understate** how much the choice matters. The draw component (the only thing `wdl` adds over `cp`, [[D287]]) is correlated (r **0.54–0.59**) and biased low by 35–40% at every band, with the band direction right | Maia, engine capability audit, calibration |
| **D293 💡 The WDL threshold that would be defensible is derivable, and it costs two-thirds of the population** | 💡 **2026-08-16, same dossier §7.3.** Agreement rises monotonically with Maia's own gap between the two moves — **45.6%** in the bottom decile (\|Δcp\| 0–9, i.e. **below chance**: when Maia says two moves are within 0.45 pp, its ordering is not information) to **97.1%** in the top. Sweeping the gap and comparing against the free incumbent on the identical pairs, the smallest value at which WDL beats explorer play counts with non-overlapping CIs is **\|Δ cp\| ≥ 71** (a 3.55 pp expected-score gap), reaching **86.7%** over **34.1%** of decided pairs `[V]`. It is admissible in *form* under `gates.md`'s engine-condition rule clause 2 — a gap between two listed moves is off the instrument's optimality boundary, it is not `bestmove` in disguise — and it is **read off a curve rather than chosen**. [[D289]] is why it should not be spent | Maia, engine-condition rule, cluster B |
| **D294 📊 The binding-experiment field worked: the first `unmeasured` disposition to be discharged came back a refusal** | 📊 **2026-08-16.** `capabilities.ts:108` carried `{ capability: "per-move wdl", disposition: "unmeasured", experiment: "D87 compare Maia WDL with R9 ground truth" }` — a **string in production code** that told a later agent exactly what to run, against what, and why nobody could answer it yet. That is the whole reason this dossier exists rather than a re-derivation of the question, and it is the mechanism `gates.md`'s engine-condition rule clause 3 requires. **The experiment ran and the honest destination is (B) *published as deliberately unreached, with the measurement as the reason* — not (A) reached and not (C) impossible.** Worth recording as a process result and not only a Maia result: the repo has three other `unmeasured` capabilities carrying named experiments (`searchmoves`, asymmetric `SelfElo`/`OppoElo`, explorer `moves` beyond 12) and this is the first evidence that the field is load-bearing rather than decorative. Flipping the register row is code and belongs to cluster B | process, capability register, engine-condition rule clause 3, cluster B |
